campaign/guides/factions/01-overview.md:3: Each faction page covers how that faction recruits, what it offers members and demands in return, how its missions run, and where it stands in the Grand Game. History, internal structure, politics, and the lore a member would absorb over time live in the **Organizations** pages in the Setting. The mechanics here stand alone; the Organizations pages deepen them.
campaign/guides/factions/01-overview.md:7: Renown is tracked as a separate numerical score for each faction a character belongs to. A character who joins both the Harpers and the Doom Raiders has two independent scores, each starting at 0. Ranks and benefits are per-faction; reaching Renown 10 with the Harpers does nothing for a character's Doom Raiders standing.
campaign/guides/factions/01-overview.md:9: **Calibration.** A character who completes all six faction missions and earns available Grand Game bonuses will reach approximately Renown 30–35 by the late heist quests, enough for the Renown 25 rank in their primary faction and Renown 10–15 in a secondary one. Build late-game faction payoffs with that range in mind.
campaign/guides/factions/01-overview.md:13: **Tracking.** Keep the per-faction Renown score on the session notes alongside the character sheet. Renown changes take effect immediately; a character who earns enough for a new rank gains its benefits before the next scene.
campaign/guides/factions/01-overview.md:17: Each faction operates as an ongoing relationship. Contacts push for information, react to the party's choices in the Grand Game, and bring their own agendas to bear as events escalate. Faction missions are structured as mini-arcs: each has an investigation phase, a complication, and at least two resolution paths. No single skill check determines success. Full write-ups are in the **Faction Events** quest journals; each faction's folder holds a First Meeting event, missions (m01–m06), standalone events (s0N) that fire on specific triggers, and rank-up events (r03, r10, r25, r50). They award no Milestone Points.
campaign/guides/factions/01-overview.md:19: **A note on the Zhentarim.** Two Zhentarim cells operate in Waterdeep. Davil Starsong's **Doom Raiders** are the player-joinable faction covered in the **Organizations** pages. Manshoon's faction is the villain cell, which the faction-mission journals call **the Splinter**. They are hostile to the Doom Raiders and to the party.
campaign/guides/factions/01-overview.md:21: **Mission delivery.** Unless noted, missions arrive via the faction's preferred method: paper birds for Harpers, flying snakes for Doom Raiders, animal messengers for the Enclave, *Sending* spells for Force Grey.
campaign/guides/factions/01-overview.md:25: **Renown.** Missions are calibrated by tier: Level 2–3 missions pay 2 Renown, Level 4–5 pay 3, Level 6–7 pay 4. Each mission lists specific bonus conditions worth +1 each; award only those, not a general judgment call. A clean failure forfeits the award but costs nothing; a mission that causes collateral damage costs −1. Supplementary renown from upholding faction values (+1) and achieving major faction goals (+2) stacks on top; a dedicated member should reach Renown 30+ by the late heist quests.

campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:5: ## Grand Game Stance
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:7: The Harpers have watched the Cassalanters for years and found nothing — which is itself suspicious. No noble family is this clean, this charitable, this free of political enemies. Mirt has been building a dossier for two years and has not broken the surface. Their overriding concern is the concentration of power in any single hand — including the Lords' Alliance, Force Grey, or the party itself. They want the vault gold dispersed too broadly for any faction to consolidate, the Stone of Golorr delivered to Harper custody for study, and whatever is dreaming below the city left undisturbed.
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:12: - Advance warning when a villain faction names a Harper member a priority target (Wise Owl, Renown 25)
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:17: - That the vault gold not be given to any one faction or individual
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:28: | **Fireball!** | Mirt quietly tells a Harper character that he suspects Cassalanter money stood behind the Howling Hatred cult three years ago, and that he wants proof. Their sudden interest in the Grand Game worries him. |
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:39: The full scene is the [Harpers First Meeting](../../quests/faction-events/harpers/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md) event.
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:47: - **Feed credible intelligence to Mirt (+1).** Report actionable information about a Grand Game faction's current position, assets, or plans. One award per faction per act; vague tips don't qualify.
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:49: - **Protect a civilian from faction crossfire (+1).** Directly intervene to prevent a non-combatant from being harmed as collateral damage during Grand Game operations. Applies once per significant incident.
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:51: - **Recover or deliver the Stone of Golorr to the Harpers (+3).** The single highest-value act a Harper operative can perform short of ending the Grand Game.
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:52: - **Refuse to act against civilians on faction orders (+1).** A character who declines a mission that would require harming innocents — and explains why — earns quiet respect. The Harpers value moral clarity alongside operational effectiveness.
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:59: | 3 | **Harpshadow** | Mirt answers one direct question per tenday about faction activity or Waterdeep politics, drawing on the Harper intelligence network. Safe house contacts in the Dock Ward, Trades Ward, and Castle Ward share street-level intelligence on any faction you ask about — known agents, movement patterns, recent activity. Each contact refreshes once per tenday. |
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:61: | 25 | **Wise Owl** | Mirt arranges a private audience with Laeral Silverhand if the matter is urgent. Harpers provide cover for one sensitive operation per quest: forged documents, coordinated distractions, or witnesses who saw nothing. Harper informants embedded in Xanathar's Guild, Sea Maidens Faire, and the Splinter can be activated for one specific intelligence request each per quest (DC 13 Charisma check; failure means the informant is unavailable for two tendays). A second *persona* is learned. When any villain faction designates a character as a priority target, Mirt's network detects it and passes warning within 24 hours. |
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:62: | 50 | **High Harper** | Full access to Harper intelligence archives for the North. May request a team of three Harper agents for one operation. Mirt personally accompanies the party on one mission of their choosing. Mirt reveals that he is a Masked Lord of Waterdeep — if the party has not already worked it out — and can be asked to act in that capacity once (one request, one use). May request a covert Harper extraction from anywhere in the city, or ask the High Harpers to formally expose one villain faction's activities to the Open Lord. A third *persona* is learned. |
campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:79: - **Harpers** organization page (lore): who the Harpers are, operations, key members, Grand Game agenda

campaign/guides/factions/02-harpers.md:84: - Standalone events: [The Cell Is Compromised](../../quests/faction-events/harpers/s01-the-cell-is-compromised/ev-01-the-cell-is-compromised.md)

campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:5: ## Grand Game Stance
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:7: The Lords' Alliance wants the 500,000 gold pieces returned to Waterdeep's city treasury. In Laeral Silverhand's framing, the gold was taxed from Waterdhavian citizens and merchants, and Lord Neverember's ouster doesn't change who owns it. She will see it recovered and properly disbursed, and she will credit those who made the recovery possible, but she won't bend institutional procedure to do it.
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:11: Two outcomes move her faster. **Ledger Delivered** shows her where the gold lies. **LA Grand Game Escalated** tells her who holds the Stone and who is racing for it.
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:22: - A commitment that the gold will reach Waterdeep's treasury or an approved dispersal, and not a single faction's coffers.
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:41: Every character who belongs to no other faction is a candidate and answers for themself. Jalester explains what the Alliance is and what it gives, answers questions and then delivers the Condition: "Assignments from us are not optional." Each candidate gives their own answer after three seconds of silence, and a refusal is final.
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:45: Full scene: [**Lords' Alliance First Meeting**](../../quests/faction-events/lords-alliance/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md). Acceptance marks the **Lords' Alliance Joined** outcome for each member by name, and a refusal marks **Lords' Alliance Offer Closed**.
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:53: - **Report Grand Game activity to Jalester (+1).** Brief Jalester on any confirmed faction operation, location, or agent. One award per significant piece of intelligence, and vague tips don't qualify.
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:54: - **Bring the Grand Game to Laeral's attention (+2).** Give Jalester the Stone's holder, the vault's place and two villain factions, so that he can escalate to the Open Lord. This triggers **Escalation to the Open Lord** and pays each member at the table once in the campaign. Laeral does not appear. She answers by a note that Jalester reads at the table the next evening.
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:69: | 25 | **Warduke** | Once per quest, with three days' notice, four **Warrior Veterans** from the Watch fight beside the member for one operation on the surface. The six Alliance missions are not eligible. Every third day, a sheet at the Portal's bar tells the member where the villain faction response teams were last seen and what each is made of. Once per quest, the member sends Jalester one written request to a Masked Lord whom nobody names: a Door, a Seal or a Pressure. Laeral receives the member at Piergeiron's Palace the morning after the rank is named. Once per quest, the member can then ask her for one private audience, which gives either a Read on one faction's escalation tier or a Ruling on one planned act. |
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:76: Honest failure costs nothing. A target who escapes, a failed roll or a beaten party does not fire the visit, and neither does a reason that fits the table of excuses in **Suspension and Dismissal**. These include illness, a captive's life, another faction's emergency and a Watch detention.
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:86: | Joining, 2nd level | [**The Long Watch**](../../quests/faction-events/lords-alliance/m01-the-long-watch/overview.md) | Guard a crew of Dungsweepers' Guild sweepers on their Trades Ward circuit for a tenday. A leak puts the crew at risk, and a carrion crawler comes up from the sewers on day nine. | +2 |
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:87: | Renown 3, 3rd level | [**The Exile's Return**](../../quests/faction-events/lords-alliance/m02-the-exiles-return/overview.md) | Find Harko Swornhold, an exile working for Xanathar's Guild in the Dock Ward, within three nights. Jalester orders him put to the sword, and Harko's surrender is the turn. | +2 |
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:88: | Renown 5, 4th level | [**The Red Wizard's Price**](../../quests/faction-events/lords-alliance/m03-the-red-wizards-price/overview.md) | End the Splinter's deal with the Thayan wizard Esloon Bezant at the Skewered Dragon before the handshake at half past seven. Combat, a Watch sergeant, a bribe or a taken broker all work. | +3 |
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:89: | Renown 8, 5th level | [**The Archer Above**](../../quests/faction-events/lords-alliance/m04-the-archer-above/overview.md) | Find Vhaspar Coldwater, a retired Watch sharpshooter who has killed three people from high windows, and take him alive. He can name Halek Farran, and a scrap in his coat points at the Cassalanter villa. | +3 |
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:90: | Renown 10, 6th level | [**Fentree's Ledger**](../../quests/faction-events/lords-alliance/m05-fentrees-ledger/overview.md) | Recover the private ledger of the dead trade investigator Orvane Fentree from the broker Rezka before she sells it at the Green Dragon Inn. The ledger shows the gold was paid out in Waterdeep. | +4 |
campaign/guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md:91: | Renown 13, 7th level, after **Kolat Towers** | [**An Audience with the Open Lord**](../../quests/faction-events/lords-alliance/m06-an-audience-with-the-open-lord/overview.md) | Attend a formal audience with Laeral Silverhand at Piergeiron's Palace. She asks three questions about the Stone, the four factions and an offer from the Splinter, and she issues a Masked Lord's Writ. | +4 |

campaign/guides/factions/04-emerald-enclave.md:5: ## Grand Game Stance
campaign/guides/factions/04-emerald-enclave.md:11: The Enclave's Grand Game goal is containment. Whatever the Vault of Dragons wakes up, Jeryth wants it handled before it reaches the surface.
campaign/guides/factions/04-emerald-enclave.md:37: Every character who belongs to no other faction is a candidate and answers for themself. A white cat carries Melannor's message to the first nature-minded candidate: "Interested in joining the Emerald Enclave? Come meet us at Phaulkonmere in the Southern Ward. The gate will be open." Any candidate can walk to the estate, whoever the cat chose. At the gate Melannor asks after his brother Talisolvanar, who is Tally Fellbranch, and then walks the garden with the party. Jeryth Phaulkon speaks from the oldest oak and explains that she cannot appear.
campaign/guides/factions/04-emerald-enclave.md:43: Full scene text is in [**Emerald Enclave First Meeting**](../../quests/faction-events/emerald-enclave/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md). Acceptance marks the **Emerald Enclave Joined** outcome for each member by name.

campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:5: ## Grand Game Stance
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:11: The Order steps outside the Grand Game entirely. It does not position for the vault or compete for faction influence. It hunts specific evil, and if the party helps find it, the Order throws its full weight behind them when it matters.
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:39: Every character who belongs to no other faction is a candidate and answers for themself: accept, decline or ask for time. A declined offer stays open, and the Event writes no closing outcome. Candidates who accept swear the oath at the Halls of Justice in the Castle Ward, which is open every day. A priest of Tyr seals it over a silver gauntlet, and the member becomes a Chevall at Renown 1 with the benefits below. Savra walks the party to a Guild hall in Order insignia when the first member swears, which cuts the renovation total of Trollskull Manor by 100 gp once. **Breaking Up the Brawl** reaches each member at the manor the next morning, once the member is 2nd level.
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:41: Full scene, branches and Savra's profile sidebar: [Order of the Gauntlet First Meeting](../../quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md). Each oath marks the **Order of the Gauntlet Joined** outcome by name.
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:52: - **Tithe a quarter of your own share of a large haul (+1).** When the party carries out a haul of 1,000 gp or more in one operation, each member owes 25% of their own equal share to a worthy cause. A member who pays before Savra's knock at dusk on the second day earns +1, once in the campaign. Payment after she asks earns nothing. The full procedure is in [The Tithe](../../quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/s01-the-tithe/ev-01-the-tithe.md).
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:53: - **Refuse to take part in or cover up an atrocity, even on faction orders (+1).** A character who holds a moral line and can explain why to Savra earns her respect in a way tactical victories cannot.
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:76: | Renown 1, 2nd level | [**Breaking Up the Brawl**](../../quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/m01-breaking-up-the-brawl/overview.md) | Stop four hired men from cornering a Xanathar's Guild dealer in a Field Ward square at noon. The man who paid them works for the Splinter, and the party can learn what he looks like. | +2 |
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:77: | Renown 3, 3rd level | [**The Black Viper Investigation**](../../quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/m02-the-black-viper-investigation/overview.md) | Find who gave *The Waterdeep Wazoo* a false story that the Black Viper is Lady Ammalia Cassalanter's twin. The trail runs through a forged record, a household secretary under an alias and a raven that is an imp. | +2 |
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:78: | Renown 5, 4th level | [**The Shard Shunners**](../../quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/m03-the-shard-shunners/overview.md) | A halfling gang is robbing the night guards of a Field Ward tavern. Find out why and settle it before the Watch fills the taproom on the sixth night. | +3 |
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:79: | Renown 8, 5th level | [**Spined Devils in Twelvedog Court**](../../quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/m04-spined-devils-in-twelvedog-court/overview.md) | Spined devils have trapped three residents in a Field Ward court. Savra fights beside the party, and the summoner's papers point to an address on Aveen Street. | +3 |
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:80: | Renown 10, 6th level | [**The Shrine on Aveen Street**](../../quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/m05-the-shrine-on-aveen-street/overview.md) | Get into a chandler's cellar that Savra cannot enter without a warrant. The party recovers a ledger in Victoro Cassalanter's hand, which Savra enters as an exhibit with a Watch clerk. | +4 |
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:81: | Renown 13, 7th level, after **Kolat Towers** | [**The Writ and the Weight**](../../quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/m06-the-writ-and-the-weight/overview.md) | On the night the Lords hear Savra's petition, a Horned Devil that calls itself a bailiff comes to the Halls of Justice for the exhibit. The party defends the nave beside Savra and two Priests of Tyr. | +4 |
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:90: - [Order of the Gauntlet Faction Events](../../quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/) — six missions, first meeting, standalone events, rank-up events
campaign/guides/factions/05-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:93: - First Meeting: [Order of the Gauntlet First Meeting](../../quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md)

campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:5: ## Grand Game Stance
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:9: She learns the rest incrementally through the party. Mission 1 adds Hlam's warning about the buried thing waking. Mission 2 brings a report of a vessel fixed under the *Eyecatcher*'s hull, which is the Bregan D'aerthe submersible, and she never learns whose it is. By Mission 4 she knows where Nihiloor's pool lies, from one report by an agent inside the Guild who has not answered since. When the party delivers the full Grand Game picture, with all four factions, the Stone, the vault and the timeline, she goes still for a long time and then begins writing.
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:20: - A complete picture of the Grand Game, once the party has it to give — the most valuable thing they can do for Waterdeep
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:23: **Grand Game goal:** The vault gold to the treasury. The pool, the placement records and the hosts already walking the city dealt with. The Splinter's arcane operations dismantled. The Stone of Golorr studied and understood before it's sealed away again. These are her priorities in order. Nihiloor always escapes the party, so she plans around him and not around his death. Her concern is which outcome leaves Waterdeep standing. The rest is scoring.
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:33: | **Vault of Dragons** | If the party has delivered the Grand Game briefing: Vajra is present at the vault opening, provides active magical containment, and stands ready to deal with anything the vault holds that shouldn't be held. If not: she arrives late and finds the situation requiring cleanup she wasn't prepared for. |
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:37: Every character who belongs to no other faction is a candidate and answers for themself. Vajra's first *Sending*, exactly 25 words, goes to the candidate who spoke to Renaer last in the **Finding Floon** warehouse. If nobody climbs to the Tower, a second *Sending* reaches a different candidate the next morning. If that also goes unanswered, **Force Grey Offer Closed** is marked with the unanswered names, and the offer returns when the party advances a level. She receives the candidates at her standing desk at Blackstaff Tower, where the door opens before anyone knocks, and offers Gray Hand status on Renaer's word. Each candidate who accepts gets a letter of authorization, and no first mission is handed over. She closes with: *"Try to get some sleep. The work does not wait for people to be rested."*
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:39: Full scene: [**Force Grey First Meeting**](../../quests/faction-events/force-grey/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md)
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:45: - **Report a significant arcane threat to Vajra before it becomes a crisis (+1).** Intellect devourers in city officials, a necromancer in the City of the Dead, an illegal gate or summoning circle discovered during Grand Game operations — any magical threat the Watch can't handle. Once per confirmed new threat.
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:46: - **Bring the Grand Game fully to Vajra's attention (+2).** A formal briefing with sufficient evidence for Vajra to escalate to Laeral Silverhand. One of the most consequential acts a Gray Hand can perform; it transforms the political landscape of the campaign's final act.
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:47: - **Purge an intellect devourer infestation or free a mind-controlled Waterdhavian (+1).** Directly countering Xanathar's mind-control operations is Force Grey's most urgent Grand Game priority. A single implanted citizen counts as one incident; discovering and purging an entire network counts as two.
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:50: - **Keep Laeral Silverhand's involvement in the Grand Game clean (+1).** Force Grey's value to the Open Lord lies partly in its deniability. Characters who resolve a problem without creating a political incident earn Vajra's appreciation in a way flashy violence doesn't. Once per quest.
campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:60: | 10 | **Senior Griffon** | The party receives a *wand of secrets* only if it holds none. Once per quest, with three days' notice, the member calls Ysmay Halvane, a Tower mage, to one operation. City officials are Friendly, and so is any Masked Lord whose identity the member has already learned. Vajra signs a written authorization for the City of the Dead at night, a sealed Watch evidence vault or a private armory (DC 12 Charisma (Persuasion), no check if the member answers her four questions plainly), once per tenday and never while a villain faction is at Lockdown. |

campaign/guides/factions/06-force-grey.md:88: - Standalone events: [The Full Picture](../../quests/faction-events/force-grey/s01-the-full-picture/ev-01-the-full-picture.md)

campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:3: Game mechanics for running the Doom Raiders (Davil Starsong's Zhentarim cell) as a player faction. For who they are, see the **Doom Raiders** organization page.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:5: ## Grand Game Stance
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:7: The Doom Raiders are the legitimate Black Network in Waterdeep, and the faction is at war with itself. Davil's cell arrived in Waterdeep first. Manshoon's Zhentarim splinter came later as a rival cell, peeled off part of that network, and now works to dismantle Davil's people and everything they built. This internal conflict is the Doom Raiders' defining preoccupation. The Grand Game is secondary.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:11: The Doom Raiders do not tell the party until Mission 4 forces the truth out. **Skeemo Weirdbottle has been a traitor from the start.** The Master of Magic has been feeding intelligence to Manshoon's cell and to House Gralhund since before the party was recruited. Everything the party has done for the faction has been visible to the Splinter. Davil has suspected something for months. Tashlyn confirmed it during his arrest.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:14: - Istrid Horn's lending operation is available to faction members at reasonable rates: up to 200 gp at Renown 1, up to 2,500 gp at Renown 10.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:21: - Loyalty as the Doom Raiders define it: the party is one of theirs. When something goes wrong, the faction will do what it can. The faction expects the same in return.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:32: | **Kolat Towers** | Davil has the pass-amulet distribution ledger (from Mission 5) and Ziraj's rooftop diagram (from Mission 6). Between them, the Doom Raiders can offer the party two independent paths through Kolat Towers' outer defenses, provided both missions are complete. This is the faction's contribution to **Kolat Towers**. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:39: Full scene — including all five Yagra greeting variants and Davil's read-aloud dialogue — in [Doom Raiders First Meeting](../../quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md).
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:47: - **Protect a Doom Raider commercial front from disruption (+1).** If a Black Network legitimate business is targeted by Manshoon's agents, the Watch, or a rival faction, characters who intervene earn renown. Once per incident.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:50: - **Broker a deal or alliance that benefits the Doom Raiders (+1).** Using faction membership to arrange a favorable agreement with a merchant, guild, or neutral party, involving a concrete, ongoing benefit. One-off transactions do not qualify.
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:57: | 3 | **Wolf** | Davil or Tashlyn provides one piece of actionable intelligence on a rival faction's activities per tenday, drawn from the Network's informant web. Access to a Black Network safe house in the Dock Ward. Restricted goods available at a 20% discount through the supply chain, including thieves' tools, forgery kits, disguise kits, poisons, and unlicensed weapons. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:60: | 50 | **Dread Lord** | Eight Black Network operatives are available for one major operation. Davil proposes the character for a seat on the reforming Zhentarim Council, a position of genuine continental influence within the Black Network. Doom Raiders safe houses in every ward. Davil offers formal partnership for the vault operation: a distraction crew draws off one faction's forces, a locksmith backup stands by, and a wagon team handles extraction logistics, in exchange for 10% of whatever the party walks away with. |
campaign/guides/factions/07-doom-raiders.md:73: **Two days after Gralhund Villa, the City Watch arrests Davil and holds him at Castle Waterdeep pending questioning about the Zhentarim's role in the street violence.** Contact passes to Tashlyn Yafeera during his detention; see the [**Davil's Arrest**](../../quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s01-davils-arrest/ev-01-davils-arrest.md) Event. Davil is released at the end of Mission 4, once the Lords conclude his cell isn't responsible for the recent killings; see the [**Davil's Return**](../../quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/s02-davils-return/ev-01-davils-return.md) Event.

campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:5: ## Grand Game Stance
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:7: Jarlaxle's purpose in Waterdeep is intelligence — what the city's power structure looks like from the inside, which Masked Lords carry real weight with the Lords' Alliance council, and what it would take to buy Luskan a seat at that table. The half-million dragons in Neverember's vault are a welcome side opportunity. The political picture is the actual mission.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:20: - Discretion, applied aggressively. Bregan D'aerthe has operated in Waterdeep for months without the City Watch knowing drow are involved. A party that exposes the faction's drow composition ends the relationship immediately.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:23: Jarlaxle's Grand Game goal: hold Eye #3, collect political intelligence, keep all four villain factions sufficiently disrupted that none can move on the vault without the party's cooperation, position Bregan D'aerthe as the preferred **Sea Maidens Faire** ally, and use the vault gold — returned publicly to Waterdeep — to buy Lords' Alliance recognition of Luskan's reformed governance.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:39: *Full write-up: [Bregan D'aerthe — First Meeting](../../quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md)*
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:45: - **Deliver intelligence that expands Jarlaxle's picture of the Grand Game (+1).** Any confirmed new information about a faction's Golorr artifact, lair location, or internal politics that he doesn't yet possess. Jarlaxle tells the character, with characteristic courtesy, if the information is already known.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:49: - **Retrieve the nimblewright shipping records from the Sea Maidens Faire without tipping off Jarlaxle's crew (+2).** A successful heist on D'aerthe's own ships, which Jarlaxle discovers and appreciates as a demonstration of capability. Characters who pull this off earn renown regardless of whether they then join the faction; existing members earn Jarlaxle's genuine delight at being robbed by his own people.
campaign/guides/factions/08-bregan-daerthe.md:58: | 10 | **Officer** | Jarlaxle assigns a drow **Spy** as a personal operative available between missions. Their loyalty is ultimately to Jarlaxle. The character gains access to D'aerthe's nimblewright shipping records — the only reliable means of tracing any construct sold in Waterdeep back to its buyer. One Uncommon magic item, personally selected by Jarlaxle because he has already read the character's intentions more clearly than they'd like. D'aerthe intelligence assessment on any faction, NPC, or location in Waterdeep on request — delivered within 48 hours, once per quest. |

campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:39: *Independent discovery path: any character asking about Xanathar weapons, black-market arms, or Guild suppliers in the Dock Ward is pointed to Grinda in Mistshore within a day (DC 12 Charisma check, no faction membership required). This is the Xanathar chain's street-level entry point.*
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:67: Xanathar's paranoia means its response to ambiguity is escalation. A single threat to an outpost pushes the faction directly from Suspicious to Alert, skipping the intermediate tier.
campaign/guides/factions/09-xanathars-guild.md:77: > Anything involving Sylgar — Xanathar's goldfish — brings the faction directly to Lockdown without warning, regardless of the current tier. No prior incident is required. No warning shot follows.

This is the Manshoon chain's street-level entry point.*
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:23: *Front Room.* Avareen and Zorbog's quarters. Avareen carries a pass-amulet to Kolat Towers. While the party explores, a flying snake arrives with *A Directive to Zorbog* signed by "Manshoon," ordering Zorbog to kidnap Fenerus Stormcastle of Quill Alley, a "known associate of these new meddlers in the Grand Game."
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:31: **Escalation:** Manshoon moves to Alert (Tier 3). Flying snakes carry the alarm within hours if any escape; Vevette Blackwater's team deploys within 3 days. If Xanathar cross-faction spillover applies, Manshoon moves to Lockdown (Tier 4). **Founders' Day Clock: 1–2 days.**
campaign/guides/factions/10-manshoons-zhentarim.md:41: **Gralhund complication:** If the Gralhunds remain active in the Grand Game, their agent Chirada may be watching Yellowspire from across the street. The Gralhunds may mount their own raid on Yellowspire to access the teleportal, inserting a third party into whatever operation the characters are planning.

campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:3: Game mechanics for running the Cassalanters as a villain faction. For who they are, see the **Cassalanters** organization page.
campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:5: ## Grand Game Stance
campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:7: The Cassalanters' posture in the Grand Game shifts based on whether the party has agreed to work for them. The soul pact comes due on Flamerule 11, the twins' ninth birthday, which is the contract's due date. Flamerule 1, Founders' Day, is the feast and the real deadline, and until then the family needs the Stone of Golorr. Whether the party becomes an asset or a threat shapes how they pursue it.
campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:11: If the party refused or turned Hostile, the campaign takes a harder shape. Imps summoned at the Asmodean Shrine maintain surveillance on the characters, feeding the Cassalanters continuous intelligence. Seffia's team joins the Eye heist at **Xanathar's Lair** as a competing faction. Willifort attempts to assassinate Jarlaxle, fails, then targets the characters in a follow-up attempt. This may push Jarlaxle to propose an alliance against the Cassalanters.
campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:59: - Any character who examines the ritual markings and makes a successful DC 15 Intelligence (Arcana) check determines that the ritual was a demonic divination — a weakly powered version of *legend lore* — seeking something tied to "Neverember," "the wealth of Waterdeep," "Golorr," and "that which has been lost to the waters of Lethe."
campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:95: - *Bregan D'aerthe's Report on the Grand Game* (Sea Maidens Faire)
campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:103: - **Running the Villains** (GM Guide): Grand Game overview, villain escalation framework
campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:104: - **The Grand Game in Play** (GM Guide): between-sessions faction procedure
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:26: > A city with one villain stops moving the moment you deal with them. Here, the other factions keep working, keep reacting, and stay ready to fill any vacuum you create. Your players' choices shift the balance between them, and that balance is never settled. Four-faction play creates genuine decisions that a single-villain campaign can't support: which lair do you hit first? Whose Eye do you trade for an alliance? The overhead is real, which is why **The Grand Game in Play** gives you a weekly routine for tracking it.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:32: **Feature All Villains** means keeping all four factions moving between sessions. Faction Response Teams deploy when the party hits assets; the party's choices shift which factions gain ground. Each faction responds to threat differently: Xanathar escalates on instinct, Manshoon prefers capture over kill, the Cassalanters exhaust legal and social leverage before calling up infernal muscle. That difference should be legible at the table. See [The Grand Game in Play](grand-game-in-play.md) for the between-session tracking procedure.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:34: **Fix-Up and Structural Robustness** means applying the Three Clue Rule to every investigation beat: at least three independent paths to each conclusion, with at least one passive path that finds the party rather than waiting to be found. Build situations, not scripts. If the party goes somewhere the design didn't anticipate, there's a faction there. See [Structural Rules](structural-rules.md) for implementation.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:42: **Act II: The Investigation** (Levels 3–4) opens Ches 22 when the fireball kills someone the party knows. It ends at Gralhund Villa, a quinpartite faction confrontation, with the Stone of Golorr in the party's hands.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:59: | [Running the Villains](running-the-villains.md) | Before **Finding Floon** — MacGuffin chain, faction knowledge at campaign start, escalation tiers |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/about-this-campaign.md:60: | [The Grand Game in Play](grand-game-in-play.md) | Before **Trollskull Alley** — faction starting positions, between-session tracking procedure |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:11: | III | The Heists | 4–7 | Faction Outposts, Xanathar's Lair, Cassalanter Villa, Sea Maidens Faire, Kolat Towers | 16–28 | Can the PCs recover all three Eyes before any faction assembles them? |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:21: Finding Floon opens on Ches 1 with a missing-persons job that pays in real estate: a deed to a dilapidated tavern in the North Ward. Trollskull Alley expands that into two tendays of renovation work, neighborhood politics, and faction recruiters appearing at the door with offers the party isn't yet equipped to fully refuse or trust. The act has no single villain and no grand stakes, by design. Waterdeep opens district by district as the players earn their place in it: the guilds, the Watch, the neighbors, and the city's wider machinery. The tavern opens to the North Ward on Ches 20; the Twin Parades fill Waterdeep's streets on Ches 21. By then the players know their neighborhood, their neighbors, and at least one faction whose name will matter later. Act I works because the city feels like somewhere worth protecting before anything threatens it.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:25: The fireball goes off the morning after the parades. Someone the party knows is among the twelve dead. What starts as street-level forensics (witnesses, a nimblewright trace, one strange lead after another) accelerates north toward a noble villa where every major faction has converged on a single object. Gralhund Villa is the act's climax: a quinpartite confrontation inside a building where everyone has a competing claim on the same night. When it ends, the party holds the Stone of Golorr. Act II is short by design; its job is to collapse the mystery into a complication bigger than the premise and hand the party something they don't yet know how to use.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:33: The Vault of Dragons is a convergence, not a heist. The Faction-State Summary reads every flag the party set across Act III and populates the final confrontation accordingly: which villains survived, which factions arrived to stake their claim, what the Stone's Full Awakening revealed. Aurinax, the adult gold dragon guarding the vault, has four paths to resolution and no obligation to take the simplest one. After the two-wave faction confrontation, 500,000 gold pieces sit on the vault floor. What the party does with them is the campaign's last decision, and the campaign doesn't make it for them.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/adventure-summary.md:58: - [The Grand Game in Play](grand-game-in-play.md) — faction positions at campaign start
campaign/guides/gm-guide/bonds-and-flaws.md:55: > | 6 | Doom Raiders membership: the faction will eventually ask for something uncomfortable. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:5: Each of you chooses one Debt of the City. Unlike a backstory event, a Debt doesn't end. It follows your character. It's a social and narrative anchor that plants you inside the city's power structures and the Grand Game before the first session is over.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:62: > Floxin works for Manshoon, not the Doom Raiders. A character whose Debt is held by Floxin is already entangled with the campaign's primary villain faction, whether they know it or not. Davil Starsong's cell is a separate organization and won't automatically come to this character's aid. Let the character discover the distinction on their own.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:86: Renaer Neverember trusted you with something he doesn't trust easily: a name, a location, a transaction that implicates someone who isn't his father and is still active in Waterdeep. His father's corruption is already becoming common knowledge. This isn't. He told you because he needed someone outside his usual circles to hold the information, because he thought you'd know what to do with it if something happened to him.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:92: ### The Holder: Renaer Neverember (Independent)
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:94: Renaer Neverember is the estranged son of Dagult Neverember, the former Open Lord who looted the Vault of Dragons before his removal from office. Renaer is nothing like his father, which has cost him: politically sidelined by association with Dagult's name, he works quietly to repair both his standing and his city.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:176: - [Grand Game in Play](grand-game-in-play.md) — how Debt holders operate in the faction landscape
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:15: The remix runs all four simultaneously for three reasons. First, a city where four powerful factions are genuinely competing gives players organic reasons to act without the plot needing to push them. If the Cassalanters are poisoning people, Xanathar is having informants killed, and Manshoon is blackmailing city officials, the party doesn't need a quest to care. Second, four-faction play creates genuine choice: which lair do you hit first? Whose Eye do you trade for an alliance? A single-villain campaign has only one answer to those questions. Third, no single villain defines the campaign's identity, which means it can accommodate different playstyles at once. The group that wants political intrigue gets it through the Cassalanters; the group that wants dungeon delving gets it through Xanathar's Lair.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:17: The overhead is real. The faction state tables in **Running the Villains** exist because you need them. If tracking four escalation states feels like too much, there's a lighter approach: let the factions escalate automatically on a calendar, skip the response-team mechanics, and only track the flags that directly affect whichever heist the party is currently running. The full system is there for GMs who want it; the stripped version still runs four active villains.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:23: Dragon Heist is marketed as a heist campaign. The original is an investigation that ends in a dungeon: **Fireball!** sends the party chasing clues about a construct, which leads to the Stone of Golorr, which leads to a faction confrontation at Gralhund Villa, which eventually leads to a faction lair. The party arrives and fights through it. Nothing about that is wrong, but calling it a heist raises expectations the design doesn't meet.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:25: We apply a five-step framework to every Act III lair: Identify the Score, Gather Information, Surveillance, Preparation, and Operation. The framework solves a specific problem. The original design rewards players who engage with faction politics but does almost nothing for players who ask good questions, case the target, and plan their approach. Under the heist framework, planning pays off. A party that does careful surveillance of Cassalanter Villa gets better entry options and intelligence on Willifort's detect thoughts range. A party that wings it still has a path through, but they spend more of the operation improvising rather than executing a plan they built.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:37: Splitting the three Eyes across three separate lairs accomplishes three things. The party must engage all four factions rather than concentrating on the one that currently holds the Stone; even a party that despises the Cassalanters still has to get inside their villa to recover Eye #2. The distribution creates negotiation currency: trade an Eye for an alliance, offer one as bait, lose one to a rival faction and steal it back. And the Stone's gradual awakening (fragmentary impressions at one Eye, a partial map at two, the Full Awakening at three) gives the party a reason to invest in each lair heist independently rather than treating the first two as obstacles between themselves and the vault.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:39: This requires one thing from you: keep track of which faction holds which Eye, and make sure players understand that all three are necessary. The Stone's partial awakenings handle most of the exposition: each one leaves an impression that confirms another Eye exists and points a direction. Trust the impressions to do their job.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:79: Bregan D'aerthe recruits during **Trollskull Alley**, alongside every other player faction. Any PC can join. J.B. Nevercott's visit to Trollskull Manor happens in every campaign where the party hasn't severed contact, which means Jarlaxle is already operating in the city from Act I, through a persona that commits to nothing and explains nothing.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:97: Players will propose other ends for the money, and each one runs through a path that already exists. A party that gives the gold away to the city's poor first has to move it, so run the transport path and its complications, then hand it out. A party that trades the gold to a faction is running the political-leverage path, with the coin in the deal instead of the location. Neither needs new machinery.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:105: That framing wastes a good character. Aurinax is a gold dragon who swore an oath to Dagult Neverember and spent years in the dark beneath a mausoleum waiting for an authorization that grows less likely every month. Neverember has been publicly ousted, his authority to bind such oaths stripped with him. The dragon has had a long time to think about what a promise to a corrupt man still means, and he doesn't have a clean answer.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:107: Four resolution paths exist, none of them predetermined by the GM. Oath-release is what Aurinax wants and will accept without a fight: Renaer Neverember presented formally, the Masked Lord's Writ from **Lords' Alliance** Mission 6, or the release phrase from the Stone's Full Awakening all constitute genuine authority in his eyes. Negotiation without release requires a DC 18 Charisma (Persuasion) check; he can be talked into a partial concession, with advantage if Vajra or a Bahamut worshiper is present. Legal argument is the most interesting path: Neverember's ouster is public record, and a party willing to argue that a disgraced lord's binding oath has lapsed gives Aurinax permission to agree with something he already half-believes. Combat is available because Aurinax defends himself, the dragonstaff, and the gold, but he won't initiate, won't chase a retreating party, and can't be intimidated. His Legendary Resistance defeats charm attempts outright.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:136: - [The Grand Game in Play](grand-game-in-play.md) — how simultaneous villains operate in practice
campaign/guides/gm-guide/design-notes-running-the-campaign.md:137: - [Running the Villains](running-the-villains.md) — the four-faction escalation system in detail
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:1: # The Grand Game in Play
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:3: The Grand Game doesn't wait. While the party rests, the factions are moving: issuing orders, reading intelligence, replenishing losses, and adjusting to whatever the PCs did last session. Between sessions, use this routine to keep four active villain factions coherent without rebuilding them from scratch each session. For the reference tables (faction knowledge at campaign start, the full escalation tier table, and the inter-faction operations matrix), see **Running the Villains**.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:7: Running four simultaneous factions is less complicated than it sounds, because only one or two are visible in any given session. The others are working in the background. Between sessions, spend five minutes asking three questions about each faction:
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:15: The factions know things the PCs don't. Response teams, surveillance operations, and intelligence networks run in the background throughout the campaign. When PCs find documents in a faction outpost, they're reading real intelligence that has already shaped the faction's next move. The faction is playing an active game.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:17: Play factions like thinking opponents, not obstacles. Each one has goals, pursues them methodically, and reacts to setbacks. A faction loses interest in the PCs the moment the PCs stop mattering to its plans. Some factions will try to kill the party, but only because the PCs represent a specific threat to a specific goal.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:20: > **Three questions per faction:**
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:25: > **Four things to track per faction:**
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:28: > - What the faction is pursuing this tenday, independent of the PCs
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:29: > - Whether the faction's Grand Game position has shifted (Eye acquired or lost, operative compromised, alliance changed)
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:33: After each session, update a short faction record for each villain faction. Four items per faction.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:35: **Current knowledge of PC identities and capabilities.** Write down what each faction knows about the party: names, descriptions, and demonstrated ability. If the PCs used a disguise during a heist, note whether the disguise held. If a faction captured someone, record what they extracted.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:37: **Response status.** Record which escalation tier each faction currently occupies and what would push it to the next one. Note whether a response team has been deployed and whether the PC threat has reached faction leadership.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:39: **Independent tenday activity.** Write one sentence describing what the faction is doing this tenday regardless of PC involvement. This keeps factions present in the narrative even when the party is focused elsewhere.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:41: **Grand Game position.** Record any shift in the faction's standing: an Eye acquired or lost, a key operative killed or compromised, an alliance formed or broken, a safe house burned.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:43: Inter-faction activity continues off-screen. If the party spends three sessions pursuing Cassalanter outposts, Xanathar and Manshoon haven't been idle. Keep a one-line answer ready for what each faction did during that time. When PCs canvass contacts, read Watch reports, or press informants, they may turn up traces of that off-screen activity. The world feels inhabited rather than scripted when the factions are visibly busy with their own agendas.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:47: Each faction moves through four tiers: Unaware, Suspicious, Alert, and Lockdown. The tier table and faction-specific trigger lists live in **Running the Villains**. What matters here is how each faction moves through those tiers in practice, because the pace and texture differ significantly.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:49: **Xanathar** escalates faster than any other faction and less rationally. Its paranoia means a single unexplained loss can push it from Suspicious to Alert without any intermediate step. Anything touching Sylgar, its goldfish, bypasses the tier sequence and produces Lockdown behavior immediately. When Xanathar reaches Alert or Lockdown, plan for overreaction: more force than the situation warrants, a readiness to burn assets to make a point.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:59: For most of the campaign, the factions are primarily watching each other. Their competition over Neverember's Enigma predates the PCs' arrival and would continue without them.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:65: Jarlaxle and Manshoon maintain a fragile non-interference arrangement. Each considers the other the only genuine peer in the Grand Game, and neither wants direct conflict while other factions are more immediate problems. The arrangement is under strain: Manshoon has identified Fenerus Stormcastle as a Bregan D'aerthe cut-out and ordered his abduction. If that abduction proceeds, Jarlaxle will act unilaterally, and the non-interference collapses.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:69: Factions fill vacuums. When the PCs weaken one faction significantly, killing a response team, burning an outpost, or compromising an operative, the others notice the suddenly quieter territory or the absence of a known contact. They don't know what the PCs did. They know that a piece of the board has gone quiet, and thinking factions move on opportunity. For the specific active operations and the documents that cross faction lines, see the inter-faction operations matrix in **Running the Villains**.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:72: > This is the Grand Game's self-reinforcing pressure. If the PCs dismantle Xanathar's Guild outposts, Manshoon's Zhentarim moves into the vacated Dock Ward territory. If the PCs weaken Manshoon, Xanathar's paranoia briefly lifts and it turns outward. The PCs' choices produce real chain reactions because thinking factions respond to changing conditions. The inter-faction operations matrix in **Running the Villains** shows where each faction's active operations overlap and which documents cross faction lines; consult it whenever the party clears territory a rival faction would want.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:78: **The Doom Raiders** are a player-joinable faction, not a villain faction. If the party has turned them hostile, Davil Starsong will negotiate the moment he sees a path to de-escalation. What he wants is information: something useful about the party's other activities or contacts. A working relationship can be bought back with the right currency. Turning the Doom Raiders hostile closes the Zhentarim renown track and the cell's contact network; restoring goodwill reopens them.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:84: They can also be negotiated out of the Grand Game entirely. The vault gold is a means, not an end. Everything they have done since signing Asmodeus's contract has aimed at buying their children's freedom before the twins' ninth birthday, and once a party discovers the full truth and approaches them directly, the social mask drops. Victoro's polished banker act vanishes and a frightened father takes his place. Ammalia weeps. They will take any genuine alternative the party offers, immediately and without negotiation. Two exits from the Game are available. In the cooperative path, the party secures the vault gold on their behalf; the Cassalanters exit satisfied, but every coin from Neverember's hoard goes to Asmodeus and the party receives nothing. In the contract path, the mace of disruption, recovered from Caladorn Cassalanter's ghost in the villa's family crypt, strikes the Asmodeus statue in A7, severs the infernal contract, and renders the gold irrelevant; the children are freed without payment, but Asmodean retribution follows within days. Both routes require reaching **Cassalanter Villa** first. The full resolution mechanics, including the sacrifice clause that runs parallel to the gold payment, are there.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:88: He can also be reasoned with, and even joined. Membership opens during **Trollskull Alley**, before the party knows who Jarlaxle is, through the same surveillance-and-recruitment sequence that runs for every other faction. Joining changes the shape of **Sea Maidens Faire** completely: D'aerthe members find the Faire is a home base rather than a heist target, and Jarlaxle sends the party after another faction's Eye instead. Reporting the initial surveillance to the Watch closes membership permanently and leaves Jarlaxle a neutral third party in **Sea Maidens Faire**, never a potential ally. For parties who have not joined, two paths to a voluntary resolution exist before Tarsakh 20, when the ships depart. The alliance path asks for a credible political commitment to Luskan's Lords' Alliance bid in exchange for Eye #3; it requires a DC 16 Charisma (Persuasion) check, with advantage when the party brings concrete commitments from two or more faction contacts, and Jarlaxle detects empty promises. It bypasses the heist entirely, but strains every faction relationship built to that point. The patron path is available to D'aerthe members: at Renown 10 or above, Jarlaxle gives the Eye outright as a reward for proven service; at Initiate or Soldier rank (Renown 1–9), he runs the Zardoz Betrayal Pitch instead, a competence and loyalty test disguised as a job, and a party that reports back honestly sees him drop the disguise, reveal himself, and offer the Eye freely. Full mechanics for all three routes are in **Sea Maidens Faire** and the **Bregan D'aerthe** Factions guide page.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:96: - [Running the Villains](running-the-villains.md) — faction knowledge at campaign start, full escalation table, inter-faction operations matrix, response team rosters, and revelation lists
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:97: - [Structural Rules](structural-rules.md) — faction response team mechanics, festival calendar, Three Clue Rule
campaign/guides/gm-guide/grand-game-in-play.md:98: - [Setting: Grand Game](../../setting/grand-game.md) — in-world texture of how power moves in Waterdeep
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:3: You don't choose a faction during character creation. You can tell your GM which factions interest you, but formal membership happens in play. Every major faction in Waterdeep initiates contact with your characters during **Trollskull Alley**, and you decide how to answer when they do. Your character can belong to more than one faction at once — memberships in rival factions can pull your party in different directions. That tension is by design. Talk it through when it comes up.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:5: Use this table to plan your character. Each faction's contact introduces themselves in play.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:22: - **Starting Renown.** Your Renown with a faction starts at 0 the moment you join.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:24: - **Earning Renown.** You earn Renown by completing faction missions and by taking the specific Grand Game actions each faction lists under its own Earning Renown section. No other source awards Renown; general good roleplay earns none on its own.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:25: - **Losing Renown.** Serious offenses cost 2 Renown each: betraying a mission, harming faction members, or working against the faction's interests. Renown never falls below 0. The Lords' Alliance is the exception: it never lowers Renown and suspends or dismisses a member instead.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:26: - **Lords' Alliance discipline.** The Lords' Alliance is the only faction that enforces consequences for refusing an assigned mission, or abandoning it past its stated deadline, without compelling reason. An honest attempt that fails costs nothing: formal suspension until reinstated, or outright dismissal.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:36: > Each rank adds to the benefits you already have. Reaching Renown 10 with any faction means you keep everything from Renown 1 and 3 as well.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:42: - Feed actionable intelligence about a faction's position, assets, or plans to your contact.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:44: - Protect a civilian from faction crossfire, or refuse an assignment that would harm innocents.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:49: | **Harpshadow** | 3 | Your contact answers one direct question per tenday about faction activity or Waterdeep politics. Street-level contacts in the Dock Ward, Trades Ward, and Castle Ward share intelligence on any faction once per tenday: known agents, movement patterns, recent activity. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:51: | **Wise Owl** | 25 | The faction covers one sensitive operation per quest: forged documents, coordinated distractions, or witnesses who saw nothing. Informants inside rival factions can be activated for one specific intelligence request each per quest (DC 13 Charisma check; failure means the informant is unavailable for two tendays). You learn a second *persona*. When a villain faction designates you as a priority target, your handlers warn you within 24 hours. The faction can arrange a private audience with city leadership if it's urgent. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:52: | **High Harper** | 50 | Full access to Harper intelligence archives for the North. Request a team of three Harper agents for one operation. A senior Harper personally accompanies you on one mission of your choosing, drawing on a connection to Waterdeep's power structure that opens doors across the city. Request a covert extraction from anywhere in the city, or ask the High Harpers to formally expose a villain faction's activities to the Open Lord. You learn a third *persona*. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:59: The Alliance rewards intelligence that reaches the right authorities and actions that protect the city's stability. Missions it assigns aren't optional; it's the only faction to say so plainly.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:61: - Report any confirmed Grand Game faction operation, location, or agent to your contact.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:62: - Bring enough evidence to formally escalate the Grand Game to the Open Lord's attention.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:71: | **Lioncrown** | 50 | Full political backing of the Open Lord. She clears one outstanding charge or wanted status against your name with her signature. She answers three direct questions about the Grand Game or Waterdeep's political situation, honestly. You may accept an attaché who carries her seal and works beside you in the field. The backing is yours whether or not you take him. The attaché is Sevel Dastar, a Spy. Laeral admits her decline in her answer to the first question, which is the one time she does. She also asks the member to look for Joroth Brighthelm's missing team in Undermountain, and a member who takes the card is marked **Undermountain Commission Accepted**. If **Laeral Told of Jalester** is marked, Jalester is on desk duty at the Palace and Sevel is already the member's handler. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:82: | **Springwarden** | 1 | Recognized member. Each member receives a *charm of restoration* with 3 charges, which casts *lesser restoration* for 1 charge or *greater restoration* for 2. The charm vanishes when the charges are spent. Phaulkonmere is a haven for you and the friends who arrive with you. No faction's response team will enter it. The charm needs no components, so *greater restoration* needs no diamond dust. A companion can receive the spell but cannot cast it. The haven admits the member and up to eight companions who arrive with them, from dawn to dusk. A member who holds the east gate key (**East Gate Key Held**) can enter at any hour. It gives a Long Rest and free care for animals, and it hides nobody from a lawful arrest. Leading an enemy inside or drawing steel loses the haven for two tendays. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:93: - Destroy or banish a devil or demon (CR 3 or higher) during Grand Game operations, or rescue a mind-controlled citizen.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:118: | **Senior Griffon** | 10 | A Tower mage, Ysmay Halvane, joins one operation per quest. The party receives a *wand of secrets* if it has none. City officials are Friendly, and so is any Masked Lord you have already identified. The Blackstaff signs written authorization to enter the City of the Dead at night, a sealed Watch evidence vault or a private armory. Ysmay needs three days' notice. The authorization is one slip per tenday (DC 12 Charisma (Persuasion), or no check if you answer her four questions plainly), and Vajra refuses it while any villain faction is at Lockdown toward the party. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:131: | **Fang** | 1 | Recognized Network member. A faction money-lender offers loans up to 200 gp at favorable rates, no questions asked. Stolen goods sell at 60% value through Doom Raiders fences instead of the standard 50%. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:132: | **Wolf** | 3 | Your contact provides one piece of actionable intelligence on a rival faction per tenday, drawn from the Network's informant web. Access to a Black Network safe house in the Dock Ward. Restricted goods (thieves' tools, forgery kits, disguise kits, poisons, unlicensed weapons) available through the supply chain at a 20% discount. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:133: | **Viper** | 10 | The Network arranges one forged document per quest (identity papers, licenses, letters of introduction), indistinguishable from genuine without magical verification. A Doom Raider ally serves as muscle on one operation per quest. A faction specialist in forgery, lockpicking, alchemy, or disguise assists on one job at no charge; after that, 50 gp per day. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:135: | **Dread Lord** | 50 | Eight Black Network operatives for one major operation. Your contact proposes you for a seat on the reforming Zhentarim Council. Doom Raiders safe houses in every ward. The faction offers an end-game operational partnership: a distraction crew, a locksmith backup, and a wagon extraction team, in exchange for 10% of whatever you recover. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:139: The faction rewards operatives who expand its intelligence picture and protect its embedded assets. Discretion is the primary operational requirement.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:141: - Deliver intelligence about a rival faction's internal position or politics that the faction doesn't yet possess.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:142: - Protect an embedded faction informant's cover within a rival organization; use faction-provided cover without compromising it.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:146: | **Initiate** | 1 | Recognized operative. Safe house access through the faction's harbor network. Access to drow-made equipment at cost (hand crossbows, *+1 bolts*, darkvision-enhancing gear) through faction supply lines. All faction members extend professional courtesy and won't act against you without orders from above. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:147: | **Soldier** | 3 | Your handler shares one piece of intelligence per tenday drawn from the faction's embedded network, including intelligence from sources inside rival factions. Access to a cover identity support network. A 20% discount on black-market purchases brokered through the faction. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:148: | **Officer** | 10 | The faction assigns you a drow **spy** for between-mission work. Their loyalty is ultimately to the faction. Access to intelligence records useful for tracing illicit activity in the city. One Uncommon magic item from the faction's collection. An intelligence assessment on any faction, NPC, or location in Waterdeep on request, delivered within 48 hours, once per quest. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:150: | **Houseless Noble** | 50 | You're named an honorary member of the faction's inner circle. An extensive intelligence network across Faerûn opens to you, with contacts from the Underdark to the Sword Coast. Dedicated sea transport and crew for any mission you undertake. |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:154: Each of the six standard factions assigns a named contact who handles recruitment and mission delivery. Full mission tables and First Meeting scenes are in the **Factions** guide pages. Full profiles are in the **Organizations** pages. The table below adds what the player version withholds: contacts and the faction's actual agenda.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:158: | Harpers | **Mirt** (primary); **Remallia "Remi" Haventree** (secondary — identity withheld until Mission 4) | Intelligence on faction activity; protection of vulnerable NPCs; exposure of corruption |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:167: Bregan D'aerthe is the only villain faction available to player characters, and joining it changes the shape of a major quest. Faction lore and key members are on the **Bregan D'aerthe** organization page; quest hooks, renown and ranks, missions, response teams, and outposts are on the **Bregan D'aerthe** Factions guide page.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:184: For parties who join, **Sea Maidens Faire** inverts: the Faire becomes a home base and Jarlaxle sends the party after another faction's Eye rather than holding the Faire as a heist target. The quest's inversion is the point — BD members experience the Grand Game from Jarlaxle's side of the table, where the resources and the objectives both belong to him.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:190: The factions' interests are incompatible by design. The Lords' Alliance wants the gold returned to the city treasury intact. The Doom Raiders want Manshoon destroyed; his cell threatens their entire operation. Force Grey wants the Grand Game resolved before it spills into public view and embarrasses the city. Bregan D'aerthe wants a political favor that extends well beyond Waterdeep's walls.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:192: A party with members in multiple factions will face these tensions as individual character obligations. When a Harper PC and a Doom Raider PC disagree about whether to hand the Stone to Mirt or protect Manshoon's rival operation, that's a character moment. Let it play as one. Treat each faction position as a real constraint on that character, give the players space to negotiate it between themselves, and resist resolving it by erasing one character's obligation. The disagreement is the texture of the campaign's second act.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:194: The practical limit: faction pressure should complicate a decision the party cares about, not force someone to act against the group's survival. Almost every faction obligation has a tenday of slack built in. Surface the competing positions as information; let the players find the path.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/player-factions-overview.md:200: The seven factions above are the organizations available for player characters to join. The other major powers active in Waterdeep during the Grand Game — **Xanathar's Guild**, **Manshoon's Zhentarim splinter**, and the **Cassalanters** — are adversary factions. Players may interact with, negotiate with, or broker deals with these organizations, but formal membership is not available through normal play. See **Notable Figures** for details on their leaders and agents, and **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) for how to run them at the table.
