campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:3: Read this page before the campaign begins and keep it close. Every faction listed here is active from the first session, whether or not the players have encountered them yet.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:9: The current Grand Game revolves around Neverember's Enigma: the 500,000 gold dragons that disgraced former Open Lord Dagult Neverember embezzled from the city and hid in a vault beneath the Brandath Crypts. The vault's location is locked inside the Stone of Golorr, an aboleth-created artifact Neverember blinded before he lost it. He stripped out three gemstone Eyes the Stone needs to reveal anything useful. Getting into the vault requires recovering all three Eyes and restoring them to the Stone.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:13: For the between-sessions procedure (the tenday faction check, faction state tracking, escalation pace, and when factions stand down), see **The Grand Game in Play**.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:19: **The Stone of Golorr** — an aboleth creation. At campaign start it is in Dalakhar's possession; Dalakhar was a rock gnome spy in Neverember's employ, carrying the Stone to Trollskull Manor when the fireball killed him. The Gralhunds recovered it from the blast site. The party acquires it during **Gralhund Villa**. The Stone can name the location of Neverember's Vault, but Neverember blinded it by removing three Eyes before losing control of it. Without all three Eyes seated in the Stone, it reveals nothing useful.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:23: **Eye #2** — held by the Cassalanters. Their agents found it in the Brandath Crypts through infernal divination and extracted it from Neverember's magical wards. Locked in the vault beneath Cassalanter Villa.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:25: **Eye #3** — held by Jarlaxle Baenre, aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*. It began in Renaer Neverember's mourning locket. Manshoon's Zhentarim seized the locket the night before Ches 1 and pried it open at the Candle Lane warehouse, Xanathar's Guild raided the warehouse and carried off the Eye with the rest of the back office without knowing what it was, and Bregan D'aerthe agents already inside Xanathar's hideout lifted it the same night. It reached Jarlaxle before the party reached the hideout, and Manshoon's cell never held it. Jarlaxle has known of the Stone, the Eyes and the vault since he arrived in Waterdeep. He is active from Act I through his personas — J.B. Nevercott in Trollskull Alley and Zardoz Zord at the Field of Triumph — and his villain escalation keys off the **Jarlaxle Informed** flag set during **Fireball!**, which records only that the party made contact with him.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:45: Each faction moves through four escalation tiers when the PCs interfere with their operations. Tiers advance when a previous tier's response fails or when the faction's intelligence confirms the PCs are a serious threat. Reaching Lockdown usually requires the PCs to hit multiple faction assets first.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:57: > Anything involving Xanathar's goldfish — threatening it, harming it, even alarming it — triggers Lockdown immediately, regardless of the current escalation tier. No intermediate response fires. The faction moves straight to Noska Ur'gray's elimination squad.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:72: | **Xanathar vs. Cassalanters** | Parallel competition — no direct contact | Xanathar has intelligence on the Seven Masks Theater but no active operation against the Cassalanters. If the Cassalanters go hostile to the PCs, Seffia's team enters the Xanathar lair heist as a competing faction. | None currently active |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:73: | **Xanathar vs. Bregan D'aerthe** | Cautious rivalry — deep infiltration | Nar'l Xibrindas, Xanathar's own advisor, reports to Jarlaxle by *Sending* spell — Bregan D'aerthe's best intelligence source on Guild movements. Xanathar suspects a mole; it hasn't identified Nar'l. Fel'rekt Lafeen observed the Gralhund Villa operation. Bregan D'aerthe purchases equipment through Grinda Garloth, a dependency neither faction has acknowledged. | *Correspondence with Nar'l Xibrindas* (Sewer Hideout), *A Recommendation for Materiel* (Fel'rekt Lafeen), *Report on the Seven Masks Theater* (Xanathar's Lair) |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:78: When the PCs hit a faction outpost, check whether any inter-faction document is present and whether finding it opens a thread toward a second faction. The Revelation List section of each villain faction's Factions guide page shows where each document is found.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:84: - **Xanathar's Guild**, **Manshoon's Zhentarim**, **The Cassalanters**, and **Bregan D'aerthe** organization pages: key members and Grand Game agenda. **Xanathar's Guild**, **Manshoon's Zhentarim**, **The Cassalanters**, and **Bregan D'aerthe** Factions guide pages: response teams, outposts, each faction's escalation row, and revelation lists.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:85: - **The Grand Game in Play** (GM Guide): between-sessions procedure: the tenday faction check, faction state tracking, escalation pace, and when factions back down.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/session-zero.md:15: Every useful ally in this campaign has done something questionable. That's not a warning about moral compromise — it's a description of Waterdeep. Characters whose ethics prevent all morally grey employment will find large stretches of this campaign frustrating. Be honest about that at the table now, not after the third faction recruiter appears.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/session-zero.md:23: Point the players to four sections of the Players' Guide: [Character Creation](../players-guide/character-creation.md), [Debts of the City](../players-guide/debts-of-the-city.md), [Bonds and Flaws](../players-guide/bonds-and-flaws.md), and [Faction Affiliations](../players-guide/faction-affiliations.md).
campaign/guides/gm-guide/session-zero.md:51: **Joining a villain faction requires a table conversation.** Four rival factions are actively dangerous and will attempt to recruit player characters over the course of the campaign. Whether a player accepts those overtures requires the same discussion.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/session-zero.md:53: **The two Zhentarims.** In Waterdeep, "the Zhentarim" describes two separate organizations with nothing in common except the name. Davil Starsong's Doom Raiders are criminals who present as legitimate businesspeople and will recruit the party during Trollskull Alley; they're a joinable player faction. A rival Zhentarim cell operates elsewhere in the city, answers to a different and far more dangerous leader, and is openly hostile to the Doom Raiders. Establish this at session zero; it's the distinction most likely to confuse players once the city's faction dynamics become visible. Joining the Doom Raiders doesn't mean working for the campaign's primary villain faction.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/session-zero.md:55: The [Faction Affiliations](../players-guide/faction-affiliations.md) page uses the same player-safe framing. The rival cell's leader is not named there, and you don't name him here either. The Bregan D'aerthe appear on the faction preview table without their contact. He introduces himself in play.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/session-zero.md:90: - [Structural Rules](structural-rules.md) — fireball victim bond, faction response teams, Three Clue Rule
campaign/guides/gm-guide/session-zero.md:94: - [Players' Guide: Faction Affiliations](../players-guide/faction-affiliations.md) — faction preview for players, Two Zhentarims note
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:7: **The Doom Raiders** are Davil Starsong's cell — a player-joinable faction that presents a public face of legitimate business and moderate criminal enterprise. They operate openly, hold meetings in taverns, and recruit adventurers. They don't acknowledge Manshoon's cell as legitimate Zhentarim, and they surface during **Trollskull Alley** as the first Zhentarim contact the party encounters.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:9: **Manshoon's Zhentarim** are the villain faction. They operate from Kolat Towers, conduct assassinations, and maintain mind-controlled servants. Manshoon regards the Doom Raiders as deserters who stole his name.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:14: > Every scene involving a "Zhentarim" NPC requires knowing which cell is present. The Doom Raiders and Manshoon's Zhentarim won't coordinate or share intelligence. If their operations run parallel, they'll try to disrupt each other. Davil Starsong's renown mechanic is entirely separate from Manshoon's faction state. Track them separately and never conflate the two when reading attunement flags.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:18: Each villain faction maintains mobile squads — groups of 2–4 members deployed when the party hits faction assets. Response teams are reactive, not proactive: they investigate intrusions, follow up on flagged activity, and act on orders from faction lieutenants.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:20: **Deployment triggers.** A response team activates when the party intrudes on a faction asset, when faction surveillance flags their activity, or when a lieutenant issues direct orders after receiving a report. These triggers are independent. One faction's response doesn't automatically alert others; factions don't share intelligence freely. Party actions that become visible citywide — Watch involvement, a public confrontation on a main thoroughfare — may reach several factions simultaneously, but covert intrusions rarely do.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:22: **Clue-seeding.** Every response team carries intelligence props: a coded letter, a partial map, a faction token, a list of names. These items reward parties who search bodies or question captives rather than simply winning the fight. Each item connects to a different investigation thread. When preparing a response team encounter, assign at least two props and know which thread each one feeds.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:24: Faction state tracking runs between sessions; the full procedure is in [The Grand Game in Play](grand-game-in-play.md).
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:29: - **Manshoon's Zhentarim** — professional mercenary tactics; more willing to negotiate mid-fight than any other faction; there are always more members nearby than visible.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:36: The festival calendar is structural, not flavor. Festivals provide social cover for heist preparation, create timing pressure, and anchor the Grand Game to specific dates. Track it actively.
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:87: | Finding each villain faction's lair | **Faction Outposts** |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:103: | Tally Fellbranch (he/him) | ev-03 | Connected to Renaer and Melannor; his death damages two faction relationships |
campaign/guides/gm-guide/structural-rules.md:115: - [The Grand Game in Play](grand-game-in-play.md) — between-sessions procedure, faction state tracking
campaign/guides/players-guide/about-this-campaign.md:10: **Make It a Heist.** Every major score, whether it's a faction's stronghold, a rival's stash house, or the vault at the center of it all, is something you can case, plan against, and hit on your own terms. You'll scout entrances, map guard patterns, and choose an approach before you commit. Charging in the front door works. So does bluffing your way past a guard captain, bribing a servant for a floor plan, or slipping in through the sewers. The city rewards a plan.
campaign/guides/players-guide/about-this-campaign.md:20: A choice made in one faction's favor is a choice made against another's. A fortune lies hidden somewhere in Waterdeep, and everyone has a claim to it: the factions, the nobles, your own characters. We're not going to tell you the right answer, because there isn't one. What your character decides to do with that fortune, if they ever get the chance, says more about them than any stat block could.
campaign/guides/players-guide/about-this-campaign.md:34: - [Faction Affiliations](faction-affiliations.md) — which factions are available and why
campaign/guides/players-guide/character-creation.md:22: **Drow** characters carry drow faction interest into every session. Coordinate with your GM before play begins so the attention your character draws fits the story you both want to tell.

campaign/guides/players-guide/character-creation.md:82: - [Faction Affiliations](faction-affiliations.md) — faction preview; formal choice deferred to **Trollskull Alley**
campaign/guides/players-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:5: Each of you chooses one Debt of the City. Unlike a backstory event, a Debt doesn't end. It follows your character. It's a social and narrative anchor that plants you inside the city's power structures and the Grand Game before the first session is over.
campaign/guides/players-guide/debts-of-the-city.md:53: Renaer Neverember trusted you with something he doesn't trust easily: a name, a location, a transaction that implicates someone who isn't his father and is still active in Waterdeep. His father's corruption is already becoming common knowledge. This isn't. He told you because he needed someone outside his usual circles to hold the information, because he thought you'd know what to do with it if something happened to him.
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:3: You don't choose a faction during character creation. You can tell your GM which factions interest you, but formal membership happens in play. Every major faction in Waterdeep initiates contact with your characters during **Trollskull Alley**, and you decide how to answer when they do. Your character can belong to more than one faction at once — memberships in rival factions can pull your party in different directions. That tension is by design. Talk it through when it comes up.
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:5: Use this table to plan your character. Each faction's contact introduces themselves in play.
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:22: - **Starting Renown.** Your Renown with a faction starts at 0 the moment you join.
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:24: - **Earning Renown.** You earn Renown by completing faction missions and by taking the specific Grand Game actions each faction lists under its own Earning Renown section. No other source awards Renown; general good roleplay earns none on its own.
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:25: - **Losing Renown.** Serious offenses cost 2 Renown each: betraying a mission, harming faction members, or working against the faction's interests. Renown never falls below 0. The Lords' Alliance is the exception: it never lowers Renown and suspends or dismisses a member instead.
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:26: - **Lords' Alliance discipline.** The Lords' Alliance is the only faction that enforces consequences for refusing an assigned mission, or abandoning it past its stated deadline, without compelling reason. An honest attempt that fails costs nothing: formal suspension until reinstated, or outright dismissal.
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:34: > Each rank adds to the benefits you already have. Reaching Renown 10 with any faction means you keep everything from Renown 1 and 3 as well.
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:40: - Feed actionable intelligence about a faction's position, assets, or plans to your contact.
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:42: - Protect a civilian from faction crossfire, or refuse an assignment that would harm innocents.

Street-level contacts in the Dock Ward, Trades Ward, and Castle Ward share intelligence on any faction once per tenday: known agents, movement patterns, recent activity. |
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:49: | **Wise Owl** | 25 | The faction covers one sensitive operation per quest: forged documents, coordinated distractions, or witnesses who saw nothing. Informants inside rival factions can be activated for one specific intelligence request each per quest (DC 13 Charisma check; failure means the informant is unavailable for two tendays). You learn a second *persona*. When a villain faction designates you as a priority target, your handlers warn you within 24 hours. The faction can arrange a private audience with city leadership if it's urgent. |
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:50: | **High Harper** | 50 | Full access to Harper intelligence archives for the North. Request a team of three Harper agents for one operation. A senior Harper personally accompanies you on one mission of your choosing, drawing on a connection to Waterdeep's power structure that opens doors across the city. Request a covert extraction from anywhere in the city, or ask the High Harpers to formally expose a villain faction's activities to the Open Lord. You learn a third *persona*. |
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:54: The Alliance rewards intelligence that reaches the right authorities and actions that protect the city's stability. Missions it assigns aren't optional; it's the only faction to say so plainly.
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:56: - Report any confirmed Grand Game faction operation, location, or agent to your contact.
campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:57: - Bring enough evidence to formally escalate the Grand Game to the Open Lord's attention.

She clears one outstanding charge or wanted status against your name with her signature. She answers three direct questions about the Grand Game or Waterdeep's political situation, honestly. You may accept an attaché who carries her seal and works beside you in the field. The backing is yours whether or not you take him. |

campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/01-overview.md:7: **Source:** `sources/Appendix_D_-_Running_the_Tavern.md` — the full polished content, including all narrative callouts, staff candidate profiles, and faction visit scenes. campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/01-overview.md:13: **Tarsakh 20 — Sea Maidens Faire departure.** Jarlaxle departs Waterdeep aboard the *Sea Maidens Faire* fleet at dawn on Tarsakh 20. After that date, the **Sea Maidens Faire** alliance path is no longer available and the faction relationship defaults to adversarial. This is the **Sea Maidens Faire** deadline only; it has no connection to **Cassalanter Villa**.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/01-overview.md:17: **Tavern impact (Founders' Day):** Founders' Day is one of the highest-revenue days of the year. Foot traffic doubles across the North Ward as civic ceremonies and street celebrations draw crowds; taverns throughout the city — including Trollskull Alley — see dramatically increased walk-in custom. The Grand Game factions are all active in the streets. Revenue Roll for this day uses the Fame bonus as if Fame were 20 points higher, and the Events table roll is mandatory rather than optional.

campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/03-staff-and-hiring.md:86: | 3 | **Fennick Doss** | CN gnome | Arrives impeccably dressed. Sits on his own hat. Retrieves it, examines the damage with evident sadness. "I play very well for most of an evening, usually. The second half is less reliable — I drink, it affects my timing, and the lyrics become interpretive." This is the only warning he gives, and he gives it sincerely. Brilliant for the first two hours. Drinks continuously through the night: each subsequent hour the deterioration compounds — timing slips by the third hour, lyrics go genuinely interpretive by the fourth, by closing he's composing original pieces with no recognizable melody. Has played private events for every faction in Waterdeep and will not disclose a single thing he heard. One absolute standard. Cutting him off from alcohol means a clean performance (+3 Revenue net) but he refuses to return without a formal apology. | None | 8 gp | +1 Fame/tenday. Hours 1–2: +3 Revenue. Hour 3: 0. Hour 4+: −2 Revenue. Cut him off: +3 net that night, then he's gone without apology. |
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/04-fame.md:9: **Design note:** The original WDH system (1 gp promotion spend = +1 to business roll) is replaced by this track. Famous taverns attract notable patrons; notable patrons cause memorable events; memorable events build more fame. Staff choices, faction incidents, and what happens in the common room have mechanical teeth.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/04-fame.md:33: **Calibration:** At 5 Fame/tenday average with no special investment, the journey from 0 to Institution takes ~70 tendays. With active play (events, faction meetings, notable patron moments), 50–60. Either way, it spans most of the campaign. Passive staff gains will plateau the tavern somewhere in Fashionable; reaching Renowned and Institution requires the party to treat the tavern as a social project.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/05-revenue.md:11: - **Faction Pressure Penalty:** When a faction actively disrupts the tavern's neighbourhood, −10 to next 1d3 rolls.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/05-revenue.md:30: 1. **Fixed Costs (60 gp/tenday):** Guild fees + staff wages. Adjust for faction leverage.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/05-revenue.md:45: **GM guidance:** Don't run the Revenue Roll every session. When the party is deep in an investigation arc, run it in the background and report the result in a line. The roll matters most during the early game when money is tight, and during any period when faction pressure is actively disrupting the neighbourhood. After Fame 31+ (Respected), hand-wave the roll unless something dramatic happened that tenday.

campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/09-response-teams-at-the-tavern.md:5: Response Teams are detailed fully on the villain faction Factions guide pages. This section covers how each team behaves specifically at the tavern — when they come, what they want, what the visit looks like at the bar rather than in an alley.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/09-response-teams-at-the-tavern.md:11: **Direct attack conditions:** Attacks come at night, after hours, when the tavern is empty or nearly so — and only if the party has taken an Eye and it's been tracked to this address, or they've publicly humiliated a faction leader in a way demanding visible response.
campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/09-response-teams-at-the-tavern.md:49: **If Doom Raiders are known allies:** She places a card face-down on the table. "The people I work for are going to win. There's a number on that. If you find what they're looking for and don't deliver it to Starsong first, that number is yours. No faction." Stands and leaves before the card is turned over.

campaign/guides/trollskull-manor/09-response-teams-at-the-tavern.md:77: **Escalation:** Once the party is in direct competition for the Gold, Willifort shifts from information-gathering to interference: impersonating party members to damage faction relationships, or arranging for a Cassalanter-connected Watch lieutenant to take a new interest in the tavern's compliance record.

campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/03-reading-room.md:13: > *"Neverember's dragons are an answer to our prayers."*
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/04-victoros-office.md:27: Also in the hidden compartment: the **Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game**. This document is a hand-written summary of the current faction competition for the Stone of Golorr, prepared by Victoro for his own reference. It references Manshoon's operations directly, identifies Kolat Towers by name and location, describes Zhentarim splinter movements in the Castle Ward, and includes the note: *"The wizard who calls himself Manshoon guards his fortress with a force field that admits only those he has keyed."* This is the Three Clue Rule path to Kolat Towers — Kolat Towers.
campaign/locations/cassalanter-villa/04-victoros-office.md:29: A second copy of the Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game is kept in C22 (Ammalia's study), ensuring that parties who do not reach C6 still have access to the Kolat Towers clue.

campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/design-notes.md:9: ***The Two Zhentarims in Finding Floon.*** Dragon Heist presents the Zhentarim as a single faction. This remix uses Finding Floon to establish the faction fracture that defines the Manshoon subplot. Yagra Stonefist (a Doom Raiders operative, Davil Starsong's people) is at the Yawning Portal bar, openly wearing the Zhentarim pendant, fighting Xanathar's Guild thugs. The Candle Lane warehouse belongs to Manshoon's cell, Urstul Floxin's people, operating separately from Davil. Players who later join the Doom Raiders in Trollskull Alley and then raid Kolat Towers in Kolat Towers will have seen both Zhentarim factions from Scene 1: one as a potential ally, one as an adversary, both operating under the same symbol. Players feel the distinction before anyone explains it.
campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/ev-01-yawning-portal.md:37: Yagra's line is the first signal that the Dock Ward killing was personal, and that she is not the same faction as whoever runs the Candle Lane warehouse. The distinction surfaces here before anyone names it.

He does not know Renaer Neverember met Floon after Volo left the Skewered Dragon.

campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/design-notes.md:5: **Seven Events for an Episodic Quest.** Finding Floon has four events because Finding Floon is a linear investigation: it flows from clue to clue, from the surface to the sewers, and ends when Floon is found. Trollskull Alley is structurally different: the party has a home base, multiple concurrent obligations, and no investigation to follow. The seven-event structure imposes narrative progression on open-ended content without prescribing player choices. **Welcome to Trollskull Alley**, **The Guild Gauntlet**, and **The Neighbors** run sequentially because the party cannot receive faction invitations before they have somewhere to receive them. **The Factions Come Calling** overlaps the renovation period with recruitment running parallel to neighbor introductions. **The Field of Triumph** slots into the renovation period: the party is recruited, fighting in the city's arena, and hiring staff all in the same stretch of time. **The Grand Opening** is the renovation's endpoint and the quest's emotional culmination. **The Twin Parades** is the city's calendar, not the party's. It falls when it falls.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/design-notes.md:19: **Trollskull Alley carries less original mechanical content than any other quest by design.** The tavern renovation system lives in the **Trollskull Manor** guide. The staff candidates live in the **Trollskull Manor** guide. The faction recruitment scenes live in the **Factions** guide pages. The faction missions live in the **Faction Events** quest journals. Trollskull Alley's job is to establish *when* each of these systems becomes relevant and *what the party is doing* when those systems activate, not to duplicate the content. A GM who reads Trollskull Alley and then the **Trollskull Manor** guide will have a complete picture of the renovation period. A GM who reads only Trollskull Alley will have enough to run the quest. That detail is there when they want it.
campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/design-notes.md:49: **The Most Trollskull Alley-Specific Faction Content.** Force Grey Missions 1 and 2 are the only Level 2 faction missions designed primarily to feed information threads rather than build renown in isolation. Hlam's warning (Mission 1) gives the first specific reference to Manshoon by implication. Zelifarn's barnacle-covered shipwreck lair at the harbor bottom (Mission 2) contains physical evidence of unusual deep-harbor activity pointing toward Skullport. Force Grey is the only faction where the Level 2–3 missions function as intelligence operations. Parties who join Force Grey in Trollskull Alley arrive at Xanathar's Lair with more operational context than any other faction affiliation provides.

campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/ev-07-the-twin-parades.md:26: The party is participating in Waterdeep civic life now — they have neighbors in this crowd, faction contacts, and a tavern that people here have already visited. The Twin Parades fall on Ches 21, the opening day of Fleetswake (Ches 21–30), the maritime festival that fills Waterdeep Harbor with tall ships. The **Sea Maidens Faire carnival fleet sails into harbor this morning**. From the parade route's eastern end, the party may notice the carnival flotilla's banners in the harbor. The *Scarlet Marpenoth* is not part of the arrival: Jarlaxle's submarine has been moored in the harbor all year, since he reached Waterdeep in Marpenoth 1491. **Thread: Sea Maidens Faire reconnaissance window begins.** The Fleetswake period (Ches 21–30) is when Jarlaxle's ships are in harbor and accessible.

campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/flowchart.md:31: **Welcome to Trollskull Alley, The Guild Gauntlet, and The Neighbors are sequential.** The party cannot receive faction invitations before they have somewhere to receive them. campaign/quests/act-i/trollskull-alley/overview.md:40: - **Renaer Neverember** (independent) — party ally from Finding Floon; offers no-interest loan up to 200 gp; townhouse available as workspace
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:5: ***Three Phases, Not One Chain.*** The original WDH investigation is a sequential chain: find the nimblewright → House of Inspired Hands → detector → Gralhund Villa. One missed check severs any link. The redesign distributes each critical revelation across three independent paths so no single failure blocks progress. Phase 1 (identify the construct) is crime-scene reading. Phase 2 (find all owners) is city-scale elimination. Phase 3 (determine guilt) is convergence under faction pressure. They teach different investigative skills in sequence without being mechanically dependent on each other.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:45: ***Jarlaxle's Conditional Activation.*** If the party reaches the Faire, Jarlaxle enters the Grand Game as an active participant and may pre-position BD agents at Gralhund Villa. If the party bypasses the Faire entirely, he remains a background presence — aware, watching, not deployed. This is not a punishment for skipping Scene 4. It is a realistic consequence of investigative depth. The party that turns over more stones gives Jarlaxle more surface to react to.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:55: ***Non-Essential, Consequential.*** ev-06 and ev-07 cannot block the party's path to Gralhund Villa. But a party that pursues them gains: the death mark (characterization of Xanathar's Guild's sophistication), the Cassalanter-to-vault link through Kalain's account of Victoro's visit, the vault-opening method, and the unsigned Eyes letter confirming Neverember's deliberate separation of Stone and Eyes. These are among the most consequential intelligence finds in the campaign, gated behind exactly the investigative habits this quest tries to build: exhaustive canvassing, leaving contact information, following up on letters addressed to dead men.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/design-notes.md:61: ***The First Quest Where All Four Villain Factions Pursue the Same Object.*** Finding Floon establishes the Guild and the Zhentarim as adversaries. Trollskull Alley introduces the Cassalanters as patrons and BD as observers. Fireball is the first quest where all four reach for the same object at the same time in the same city. The design makes this concrete: Xanathar gazers in the alley, Zhent parallel investigation, BD conditional observers, Cassalanter condolence note and dinner invitation. These are not flavor — they are the Grand Game becoming visible for the first time.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:12: > - Renaer Neverember arrives within two hours carrying Dalakhar's paper bird — the quest's proactive failsafe for Revelation #2.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:15: > - All four villain faction response teams activate.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:88: > 1. **Faction channel.** Renown 1+ in the Harpers, Lords' Alliance, Order of the Gauntlet, or Force Grey. The faction arranges a cleric through its temple network.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:94: > **Dalakhar** (up to 5 questions): Stole the *Stone of Golorr* from a beholder called Xanathar, deep in the dungeons beneath the city. Worked for the rightful Open Lord — Neverember, not Silverhand. The Stone is the key to a hidden hoard of gold dragons sealed somewhere in the city. He heard of adventurers who rescued Renaer Neverember and decided their hands were safer. Renaer vouched for them.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:192: Each active faction contact responds to the explosion in their own register. Deliver as flying snake messages, chance encounters, or requested meetings.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-01-the-fireball.md:251: A fireball detonated in Trollskull Alley at first light on Ches 22nd, killing twelve — including the Trollskull Alley victim. The crime scene, the witnesses, and Dalakhar's paper bird in Renaer's hands told the same story from three directions: a metal figure on the cooperage roof, a stolen Stone, every faction in Waterdeep already moving. The investigation had a category and a direction by nightfall. It needed one more day to get an address.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-02-the-house-of-inspired-hands.md:96: 2. **Faction intelligence.** Any faction contact can identify "nimblewright" from witness descriptions and direct the party to the Gond temple. Harpers fastest (Remallia knows the high priest personally).

campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:40: > > "The family had some acquaintance with Lord Neverember's later projects. campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-05-the-cassalanter-dinner.md:46: **What the Cassalanters know and do not say:** Victoro knows Dalakhar worked for Neverember, the Stone is the key to a concealed vault, all three Eyes were separated from the Stone years ago, and the vault's physical entrance is in the Brandath family crypt. He withholds all of this. His goal tonight is assessment — whether the party is worth cultivating.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-06-the-death-mark.md:13: > - Four faction contacts react to the death mark with distinct intelligence fragments.

campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:9: > - Kalain is paranoid and fragmented — she believes every visitor is Neverember's assassin.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:15: > **Trigger:** The party has Kalain's letter (Kalain's Letter: Received from ev-06) and chooses to follow the address, OR the party visits the harbor district and hears about a reclusive painter who knew Neverember from an NPC referral.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:19: A dilapidated tower near the harbor — once notable, now overlooked. Kalain is a half-elf painter, once celebrated in Waterdavian artistic circles, now alone. Former companion of Lord Dagult Neverember. She dismissed her household staff years ago and has not hired replacements. The tower smells of linseed oil and something older.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:33: Once settled, she speaks in fragments that require patience to assemble. She does not speak in order. Some things she says are about Neverember; some are about other things entirely; some are both.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:37: **Dalakhar worked for Neverember.** She says so openly. She adds that she is glad he is dead, meaning Neverember, not Dalakhar. It takes a moment to establish which dead man she is talking about.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:39: **The Melairkyn ceremonial vault.** Neverember was obsessed in their final months together with something he called a "Melairkyn ceremonial vault." She cannot recall the specifics — the memory feels burned away, like a word she cannot retrieve. This is the *Stone of Golorr*'s influence, still active in anyone who has been near it. The suppression is pervasive and consistent. She cannot be prompted through it; the memory is genuinely inaccessible.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:41: **Victoro Cassalanter's visit.** She will not volunteer this. If the party asks whether anyone else has come asking about Neverember's projects, she confirms: a man named Victoro Cassalanter visited a few weeks ago. He was asking many of the same questions Dalakhar had asked years before — the vault, Neverember's last projects, whether she kept his papers. He was polite. She found his politeness the most unsettling thing about the visit.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:57: **1. Neverember's crossed-out task list.** A page of notes in a cramped, efficient hand, most items struck through. One entry near the bottom is unfinished: *"move the dragon to the Melairkyn ceremonial vault."* The entry was never struck out. Pays off in Vault of Dragons: the dragon is Aurinax.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:59: **2. Kraddoc correspondence.** Letters to Hammond Kraddoc of the Vintners', Distillers', and Brewers' Guild confirming large bribes paid to cover a contaminated-liquor scandal in the Dock Ward. Neverember's private leverage over the Guild. Not directly relevant to the Stone but potentially useful for a party building political assets.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:61: **3. Melairkyn notes.** Research notes on the Melairkyn dwarves and their cult of Dumathoin. Most are fragments — partial citations, library call numbers, observations about the Vault of Dragons as a Dumathoin ceremonial site. One section is complete and explicit: *"A ceremonial vault can be opened by standing before Dumathoin's doors and striking the scale of a dragon with a mithral hammer in the place where the sun's light should fall."* The vault-opening method, written in Neverember's hand. Pays off in Vault of Dragons.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:63: **4. The unsigned Eyes letter.** Dated four years ago, in a different hand: *"The last of the three Eyes has been secured."* No signature. No context. The implication: all three Eyes were in Neverember's hands four years ago, and he deliberately separated them from the Stone. A party that has been tracking the Eye count will recognize the significance immediately. Pays off in Vault of Dragons: confirms the separation was intentional, not a consequence of theft.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:75: The party has the unsigned letter confirming Neverember held all three Eyes four years ago. Read by Vault of Dragons (Vault of Dragons: the Full Awakening scene, confirming the deliberate separation).
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:87: Kalain kept Neverember's papers in a locked box without knowing what they contained. Her tower near the harbor held the vault-opening method, evidence of Neverember's intent to separate the Eyes from the Stone, and the first direct link between Victoro Cassalanter and the vault — weeks before the fireball.
campaign/quests/act-ii/fireball/ev-07-kalainsalley-tower.md:91: The reclusive painter Kalain received the party with suspicion and fragmented speech. Through careful conversation, she confirmed Victoro Cassalanter had visited weeks before asking the same questions. Lord Dagult's lockbox in her tower held four documents, including the vault-opening method and an unsigned letter confirming Neverember had deliberately separated all three Eyes from the Stone four years ago.

campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:9: What breaks if the party doesn't get the Stone? The campaign continues — but every subsequent negotiation runs from weakness rather than strength. Jarlaxle deals with petitioners differently than he deals with principals. Whichever faction holds the Stone begins locating the vault within days. The party is racing an active countdown rather than holding a controlling position. This is consequential.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:25: The GM running this quest should prepare the adversary rosters and understand each faction's goals, then play the situation as it develops.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:33: Parties that find the BD team during ev-02 reconnaissance and raise it with Jarlaxle get a smile and a redirect. Parties that find it and don't raise it get a warmer reception next time. Parties that never find it get a practical demonstration that faction intelligence is always partial. This connects directly to the **Fireball!** design note on investigation quality having consequences: the relationship with Jarlaxle is indexed to how the party handles exactly these moments.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:45: If the chase fails and the Stone reaches a faction safe house, the campaign continues from a weaker position. Jarlaxle will deal with the party. Xanathar will not. Manshoon's cell now knows the party is active and failed. These consequences reshape the social landscape of subsequent arcs without ending the campaign. The Stone is not irretrievable — but retrieving it from a faction lair is **Faction Outposts**' problem, not this quest's.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:55: The "Bad Time to Be a Zhent" crackdown and Davil's arrest are the city's direct response to what happened on Saerdoun Street, reshaping the faction landscape for every arc that follows. The Watch's sweep removes Davil from play, puts Tashlyn in a provisional leadership role she has not asked for, and signals to every faction in the city that the Grand Game has become visible to law enforcement.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/design-notes.md:57: Manshoon's cell goes quieter. Xanathar's North Ward operations become more careful. Jarlaxle leaves no evidence at Artheyn Manor and is never definitively connected to the villa's events. The party will be questioned if they were seen. Their choices during the crackdown (who they protect, who they hand to the Watch, how they handle Istrid Horn) define their faction relationships in the second half of the campaign.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-01-what-the-factions-say.md:9: > - Six faction contacts each deliver a distinct tactical perspective and agenda.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-01-what-the-factions-say.md:17: The party has roughly forty-eight hours. The faction contacts know it. Each one reaches out in the forty-eight hours after the fireball's investigation delivers the address — notes, flying snakes, casual encounters that are not casual. The GM can deliver these in any order; they do not require the party to seek anyone out. The contacts find them.
campaign/quests/act-ii/gralhund-villa/ev-01-what-the-factions-say.md:21: The quietest nudge, carrying the clearest self-interest. Mirt delivers his input in person if possible — over food, without an agenda on the table, at a time of his choosing. He does not order the party to move on the villa. He offers gratitude. Stated as gratitude, not as a threat, not as a condition. He notes, almost as an aside, that the Watch's involvement in these matters "tends to end them." He is not wrong. He does not explain that the Harpers have been tracking the Stone for weeks, or that the party, arriving at the villa first and bringing it to him, gives him leverage over every faction that hears about it later.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m01-the-handkerchief-and-the-girl/design-notes.md:15: Nevercott's single instruction exists to be tested. Nobody can see the party break it, and the party holds the cloth for an hour. The debrief asks the question directly. An honest answer earns a bonus, and a lie costs every bonus if Vessin's book contradicts it. Vessin writes down what the party says in front of her, so the party learns that the company keeps records even on children in crates. That fits the faction's long game, in which the company asks for competence and discretion and rewards honesty it did not ask for.

campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:5: The exposé comes from the source: a lurid piece on devil worship and orgies among unnamed Waterdhavian noble families, written by Jarlaxle and delivered by break-in. The restored draft made it accurate. It described a ritual space matching the Cassalanter villa's lower temple and credited a witness account to Nar'l Xibrindas, so Xanathar's Guild would have known the Cassalanters were infernalists before the party did. That contradicts the standing rule that no other faction knows.
campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/m02-the-wazoo-affair/design-notes.md:29: Gaxly appears in two faction chains: this one and **The Black Viper Investigation** for the Order of the Gauntlet. The older draft also listed Doom Raiders and Xanathar's Guild. The Doom Raiders notes retire him, and no Xanathar mission uses him. A party that has run both missions meets a man who has had two people leave things on his desk and a third ask him about it. He isn't corrupt. He prints what sells and what he can half verify, which is why every faction can use him.

campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:5: The restored draft treated the note and the offer as a party-wide event and marked **Doom Raiders Joined** as a single True or False for the whole party. The event now follows the Harpers model. Each eligible candidate gets a note, each answers for themselves, and the outcome is marked per recipient with the character's name. A character who already belongs to another faction gets no offer, and companions can sit in on the conversation but gain nothing. This keeps a mixed party from being forced into one affiliation and gives every later mission an exact membership record to read.
campaign/quests/faction-events/doom-raiders/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:17: The restored draft had one scene and no way for a candidate to say no. The event now has a refusal path (Davil pours a second drink and Yagra walks the candidate out), a deferral path, and a late-acceptance rule. Yagra gets a real beat: the five greetings from Appendix B keyed to **Yagra Courteous**, an arm-wrestling exploration that changes how she addresses the character and nothing else, and a walk to the door. Davil answers eleven likely questions in his own voice, so a table that improvises off the script has something to read. Ziraj's roof watch and Tashlyn's two-line note introduce the faction's other faces without giving them a scene they don't yet need. Istrid appears the next morning because her 200 gp loan is the first tangible Fang benefit, and the draft left it as a sentence.

The event names the Interrogation House as the writer of **Manshoon Named**, which the **Faction Outposts** conversion must supply.

The event treats him as a safety net, because that is light for a Renown 25 ally. His mission is any task inside a named quest. The six Enclave faction missions are excluded, since The Grells in the Dock Ward and The Dreamer's Reach say where he stands. Dragon Heist makes him Chaotic Good, and the campaign keeps Neutral Good. In Force Grey a dead Rhendar ends the benefit, and this event spares the garden's keeper because he appears again in later quests.

campaign/quests/faction-events/lords-alliance/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:5: The earlier draft ended on the Condition, with Jalester leaving after three seconds, and then asked whether "the party" accepted. Nobody answered, nothing enrolled a character, the Cloak rank was never given, and the 200 gp grant that **The Factions Come Calling** promises never arrived. The event now follows the Harpers, Force Grey, Emerald Enclave and Doom Raiders models. Every character in no other faction is a candidate and answers for themselves, and **Lords' Alliance Joined** is marked with each name. The Condition stays word for word, with its three seconds. The earlier draft's facts about the lost operatives and the two assignments Jalester took back to Laeral are restored.
campaign/quests/faction-events/lords-alliance/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:9: The other First Meetings leave the door open to a deferred candidate. This one does not, because Jalester never chases. A candidate who declines outright is marked **Lords' Alliance Offer Closed**, and the offer is not repeated. A candidate who asks for time has ten days to answer at the table. The outcome is marked only on an explicit decline, or when the tenth day ends with no answer. A character who was in another faction when the meeting ran gets a late scene within a tenday of leaving it, which covers **BD Contact Severed**. The corner table beside the well replaces the earlier "if they are regular presences" rule. Jalester waits at dusk on the day the row fires. If nobody has come, he walks to the manor on the third morning, so the placement is fixed.
campaign/quests/faction-events/lords-alliance/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:16: > Every character who belongs to no other faction is a candidate, and each candidate answers for themselves. Jalester speaks first to a candidate with the Vassal, Guard, Noble or Soldier background. If several qualify, or none does, he speaks first to the candidate with the highest Charisma score. A tie goes to the name that comes first alphabetically. The civic-minded note in **The Factions Come Calling** only decides whom he addresses first. Nobody is tested for residence or alignment at the table.
campaign/quests/faction-events/lords-alliance/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:18: > Jalester makes no offer to a character who already belongs to another faction, or who was dismissed from this one. Those characters and any companions can sit at the table and listen, and they gain nothing from it. If every candidate declines, end the Event with no membership outcome.

campaign/quests/faction-events/lords-alliance/r25-warduke/design-notes.md:9: The source put Laeral at the Portal as the member's first direct contact. **An Audience with the Open Lord** already seats the party in her room at Renown 13, and most members reach it well before Renown 25, so that line was wrong for them. The listener below the Portal's well is also a reason to keep her away from it. This event moves her to the long room at the Palace the next morning. The long room keeps the size stated in the Open Lord's audience. The event branches on **Writ Issued**: if it names the member, this is a second meeting and she never calls it a first. If it does not, she is meeting the member for the first time, and **LA Grand Game Escalated** adds one line. Her four keeper sentences survive, and a measured silence stays between them. The private audience is a Read or a Ruling. The Harper audience is an urgent Watch response to a dated danger, and the two do not overlap.

campaign/quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:5: The earlier draft ended on the oath, with Savra taking "members who accept" to the Halls of Justice, and then wrote one party-level outcome. Nobody was asked a question, the Chevall rank was never given, and the renovation help in **The Factions Come Calling** never arrived. The event now follows the Harpers, Force Grey, Emerald Enclave, Doom Raiders and Lords' Alliance models. Every character in no other faction is a candidate and answers for themselves, and **Order of the Gauntlet Joined** is marked with each name when the oath is sworn. The oath is a separate scene that can be sworn on any day, so a candidate who asks for time is never lost. Appendix B and Dragon Heist both give the oath as find evil, pursue it, destroy it, protect those who cannot protect themselves and stand when standing is required, and the page uses that wording.
campaign/quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/00-first-meeting/design-notes.md:9: The earlier draft had Savra return after **Fireball!** or **Gralhund Villa** for a second approach. Those quests give her hooks to members only, so a non-member would never meet her again. The offer now stays open at the Halls, and there is no closing outcome, because the Order wants recruits. A character who leaves another faction meets Savra at the Halls within a tenday. The Savra contact object is written once here: she knocks once, waits to be asked in, and leaves a note signed "S.B." with Tally Fellbranch if nobody is home. Later events refer back to it.
campaign/quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:16: > Every character who belongs to no other faction is a candidate, and each candidate answers for themselves. Savra speaks first to a candidate with a Cleric or Paladin class or an Acolyte, Guard or Soldier background. If none qualifies, she speaks first to a worshipper of Helm, Torm or Tyr. After that she picks the candidate with the highest Wisdom score, and a tie goes to the name that comes first alphabetically. The note in **The Factions Come Calling** about paladins and clerics only decides whom she addresses first. Nobody is tested for class, alignment or faith at the door.
campaign/quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/00-first-meeting/ev-01-first-meeting.md:18: > Savra makes no offer to a character who already belongs to another faction, and she tells that character so. Those characters and any companions can sit and listen, and they gain nothing from it. If no character qualifies, the Event does not occur. If every candidate declines, end the Event with no membership outcome.

campaign/quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/s01-the-tithe/design-notes.md:11: The tithe is now owed by each member from their own equal share, so a non-member, a companion or a member of another faction is never pressed. A clock fixes the word "unprompted": Savra knocks at dusk on the second day, and only a payment receipted before that counts for the +1. Payment after she asks earns no Renown and a single "Good."

campaign/setting/deities/asmodeus.md:83: > - **Who knows (secrecy rule):** Nobody, before the party finds out. Every faction is suspicious of the Cassalanters because they are too nice to be this rich and this purebred, and none knows about the pact, the soul contract or the infernalism. The one exception is Jarlaxle, who knows through Vessa, his doppelganger spy in the Cassalanter social circle. He tells Bregan D'aerthe members nothing unless they work it out. The Emerald Enclave senses infernal influence in the Sea Ward through Jeryth and does not know whose it is. Savra Belabranta of the Order of the Gauntlet hunts infernal cults in Waterdeep and wants to learn what the Cassalanters conceal, which is suspicion only.

campaign/setting/deities/cyric.md:60: > - **The ordinary Waterdhavian's view:** Voice him as a god nobody names lightly. A sage, a priest or a regular at the bar says he is the Prince of Lies, a god of deceit and strife who was once a mortal adventurer, and that he became a god on the slopes of Mount Waterdeep at the end of the Time of Troubles. They add that he is blamed for the murder of Mystra and the Spellplague, that his worship is banned in most lands and flourishes in Amn, and that a good person who means to lie to someone calls on Leira instead of him. Faerûnians use "the Dark Sun" as a warning that intrigue and infighting have gotten out of hand in a household (SCAG, 2014), but whether Waterdhavians do is [research]. Nothing in this conversation ties him to a faction in the city.

campaign/setting/deities/gond.md:81: > - **Fireball!:** The event **The House of Inspired Hands** sends the party to the temple once they know a nimblewright set off the blast. Three paths lead there. Rishaal's Lantanese engineering text names the House, any faction contact can identify a nimblewright from witness descriptions, and Barnibus Blastwind's Watchful Order contacts can identify the construct within 48 hours of the blast. The Harpers are fastest, since Remallia Haventree knows the high priest personally. A note from Vajra Safahr to a Force Grey member the next morning says the blast left a signature the Watchful Order describes as a nimblewright detonation, "registered Lantanese manufacture, auditable through the House of Inspired Hands".

campaign/setting/deities/ilmater.md:121: > - **Conflict, the Triad:** The campaign's Order files use "the Triad" for the priests of Tyr, Helm and Torm (`campaign/quests/faction-events/order-of-the-gauntlet/r10-whitehawk/ev-01-whitehawk.md:148-150`; `campaign/setting/organizations/04-order-of-the-gauntlet.md:19`; `campaign/guides/players-guide/faction-affiliations.md:95`). `r50-righteous-hand/ev-01-righteous-hand.md:312` says Torm "is one of the Triad the Order serves", which fits either reading. campaign/setting/deities/ilsensine.md:39: - **Clergy:** The priesthood is small. In an illithid community it forms the Venerator Creed, which is one faction among many and works with the Abysmal Creed, whose members act as missionaries to other peoples (*Drizzt Do'Urden's Guide to the Underdark*). Priests live like monks, seldom leave their temples, and serve as healers and thrallmasters (*Lords of Madness* 3e; *Expanded Psionics Handbook* 3e).

campaign/setting/deities/lolth.md:67: > - **The ordinary Waterdhavian's view:** Voice her as the god of a distant and hostile people. A sage, a priest or a regular at the bar says she is the Spider Queen, the chaotic evil god of the drow, shown as a giant spider or as a drow woman with a spider's body. They add that her priestesses rule the drow houses of the Underdark, that her worshipers offer blood sacrifice and are taught to kill anyone outside the faith, and that spiders are her omens. A well-read speaker mentions FRHoF's report of aboveground cults drawn by trails of spiders, with no cult placed in Waterdeep. The only line on the city's attitude to drow is that Fel'rekt Lafeen knows what happens to drow the Watch catches (`campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/s02-kreb-drops-the-cover/ev-01-kreb-drops-the-cover.md:136`). Which of her names the city uses is unsourced. [research]

campaign/setting/deities/lolth.md:94: > - **The coin:** In **Houseless Noble** every speaker mentions her with contempt (`campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/r50-houseless-noble/ev-01-houseless-noble.md:27`). The black coin shows a spider caught in its own web with a blade slanting through it. Zardoz calls her "the old eight-legged lady with a knife in her" and says she is on the coin "so we remember who we told to go to hell" (`:233`). A member who has marked **Jarlaxle Unmasked** hears Jarlaxle call the spider "the goddess my people were taught to kneel to, with a knife through her" (`:243`). A DC 14 Intelligence (Arcana or History) check reads the design as an old drow house sigil with the spider canceled by the blade, a mark of defiance against the goddess (`:249`).

campaign/setting/deities/mask.md:9: > - **Where it is used:** Mask never appears in person, and no quest, faction or NPC in the campaign names him. The page serves a player who wants a cleric or devotee of the god of thieves, and it covers Liar's Night, the one Waterdeep holiday that names him.
campaign/setting/deities/mask.md:42: - The wiki says his priesthood ran independently from city to city, so that action against one enclave would not endanger the others. Where one thieves' guild dominated a region, a temple or shrine of Mask was often joined to the guild hall by hidden tunnels. In a larger city with rival guilds, his temple stood underground on ground that every faction accepted as neutral (*Forgotten Realms Adventures*).
campaign/setting/deities/mask.md:66: >   - Waterdeep's laws: "By law, there are no active thieves' guilds in Waterdeep" (`sources/adventure-wdh.json:1102`). The same sentence describes Xanathar's Guild as a faction.
campaign/setting/deities/mephistopheles.md:56: - In the Reckoning he fought beside Dispater, Mammon and Geryon against Baalzebul's faction, which included Zariel, Belial and Moloch. Geryon turned on him, and the pit fiend generals of both armies turned against their lords. He suffered least of the rebels (*Guide to Hell* 3e).

campaign/setting/deities/oghma.md:82: > - **Eiruk Weskur:** A Chondathan human priest of Oghma, neutral good, and a member of Renaer Neverember's Black Tears. His page lists him as a background figure with no scripted appearance, and the Renaer's Friends table in **Tavern Time** (entry 15) can bring him to the tavern. His page says he wants "to serve Oghma by gathering and sharing knowledge" and believes "knowledge shared is knowledge doubled." In a fight he would "heal first, fight second", and his page adds that his faith "teaches that knowledge includes knowing when to swing a mace."

campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:8: > - **Page type:** GM-only operations page for a villain faction. campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:9: > - **Campaign role:** Villain faction; the Cassalanters shift between a passive and a hostile posture depending on whether the party agreed to help them.
campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:13: **Purpose:** Lets the GM run the Cassalanters as a reactive faction: which posture they take, which team they send, what each outpost holds and what the family does as the party pushes.
campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:15: Game mechanics for running the Cassalanters as a villain faction. For who they are, see the **Cassalanters** organization page.
campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:17: ## Grand Game Stance
campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:19: The Cassalanters' posture in the Grand Game shifts based on whether the party has agreed to work for them. The soul pact comes due on Flamerule 11, the twins' ninth birthday, which is the contract's due date. Flamerule 1, Founders' Day, is the feast and the real deadline, and until then the family needs the Stone of Golorr. Whether the party becomes an asset or a threat shapes how they pursue it.
campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:23: If the party refused or turned Hostile, the campaign takes a harder shape. Imps summoned at the Asmodean Shrine maintain surveillance on the characters, feeding the Cassalanters continuous intelligence. Seffia's team joins the Eye heist at **Xanathar's Lair** as a competing faction. Willifort attempts to assassinate Jarlaxle, fails, then targets the characters in a follow-up attempt. This may push Jarlaxle to propose an alliance against the Cassalanters.
campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:71: - Any character who examines the ritual markings and makes a successful DC 15 Intelligence (Arcana) check determines that the ritual was a demonic divination — a weakly powered version of *legend lore* — seeking something tied to "Neverember," "the wealth of Waterdeep," "Golorr," and "that which has been lost to the waters of Lethe."
campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:107: - *Bregan D'aerthe's Report on the Grand Game* (Sea Maidens Faire)

campaign/setting/geography/city-of-the-dead.md:49: - **Permits:** A Force Grey member who has reached Senior Griffon can ask Vajra Safahr for a written authorization to enter at night. It is given once per tenday, never while a villain faction is at Lockdown, and it does not cover a crime committed inside.
campaign/setting/geography/city-of-the-dead.md:79: - The treant wakes as anyone approaches and growls, "Only those of Brandath blood are welcome here! Begone!" If someone without Brandath blood tries to open the mausoleum, it animates two nearby trees and attacks. It lets **Renaer Neverember**, who has Brandath blood, and his companions pass, and tells him in passing that his mother was a lovely person.
campaign/setting/geography/city-of-the-dead.md:80: - The Brandath family is an old Waterdhavian family. Lord Dagult Neverember married into it, and Lady Alethea Brandath was Renaer's mother.
campaign/setting/geography/city-of-the-dead.md:90: > - **What lies below:** The Vault of Dragons lies beneath the Brandath family mausoleum, and the Stone of Golorr names it when the party attunes to the Stone. Neverember found the vault while preparing his wife Alethea's interment and used it to hide his embezzled gold. See **The Vault of Dragons and Neverember's Embezzlement**.
campaign/setting/geography/city-of-the-dead.md:91: > - **The Eye:** Neverember hid one of the Stone's three Eyes in a crypt near Alethea's tomb. The Cassalanters found it there through their infernal divination and took it before the campaign opens. It is Eye #2, and they hold it.
campaign/setting/geography/city-of-the-dead.md:102: > - **The gate on the last day:** Savra Belabranta holds the main gate for a Whitehawk member, Ziraj watches the approach from the rooftops if he survived **Ziraj's Last Hunt**, and a Doom Raiders crew with four wagons waits at the gate in **Dread Lord**. Dagult Neverember arrives at the gate afterward with hired muscle and Watch loyalists (see **The Open Lord's Fall**).

campaign/setting/geography/city-of-the-dead.md:142: > - **Conflicts:** The two Brandath names. The campaign's pattern is Mausoleum for the building and Crypts for the approach (`waterdeep-lore.md:20`). Pages that blur it: `campaign/guides/gm-guide/running-the-villains.md:9`, `:32` and `:40` put the vault "beneath the Brandath Crypts"; `campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:25` and `:52` say "beneath the Brandath Mausoleum", and `:88` puts an Eye in "a crypt of the Brandath Mausoleum"; `campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:24` and `:34`, `campaign/setting/faction-operations/10-cassalanters.md:72` and `campaign/guides/factions/11-cassalanters.md:60` write the singular "Brandath Crypt"; `campaign/setting/deities/moradin.md:67` says "Brandath mausoleum"; the Emerald Enclave mission and Sir Ambrose's page write lower-case "Brandath crypts". The Alexandrian itself says both "beneath the Brandath family crypts" (PDF 19 p.130) and "beneath the Brandath mausoleum" (p.130), and the published text keys only "the Brandath family mausoleum" (`adventure-wdh.json:8358`). This page states the rule in GM Notes and does not edit the other pages.
campaign/setting/geography/dragonward-and-walking-statues.md:18: Both features lead back to the Castle Ward. The dragonward begins beneath Ahghairon's Tower in the Palace courtyard, and six of the eight statues stand in the ward. The campaign treats them as setting rather than as party resources. The statues are sites in two faction events, and the dragonward is the reason the dragonstaff of Ahghairon is worth taking.

campaign/setting/geography/dragonward-and-walking-statues.md:113: > - **How he came by it:** Maaril, the archmage who last wielded the staff, was his friend and invited him to stay in the city's Dragon Tower. Shortly before the Spellplague Maaril went mad, left Waterdeep and gave Aurinax the staff. Dagult Neverember then let Aurinax keep it as payment for guarding the vault. Dragon Heist places that bargain when Neverember became Open Lord, and the campaign places it after he found the vault (see **The Open Lord's Fall**).
campaign/setting/geography/dragonward-and-walking-statues.md:114: > - **What the staff is worth:** Whoever holds it decides which dragons the ward admits, one creature at a time. Jarlaxle wants it as leverage over Waterdeep. The structure notes for **Vault of Dragons** add that Manshoon would take it if he could and that Neverember still believes he has a claim to it.

campaign/setting/geography/dragonward-and-walking-statues.md:168: > - **Derived:** The wards of the Lady Dreaming and the Hawk Man. Dragon Heist names none, but it gives the Castle Ward six statues (`adventure-wdh.json:31007`), puts the Swordmaiden in the North Ward (`:31276`) and puts the Honorable Knight near a four-ward corner (`:30693`). That leaves these two in the Castle Ward, and the wiki agrees (`wiki.txt:74` and `:76`). The God Catcher's ward is confirmed by Dragon Heist's own faction text: "the God Catcher, one of the enormous statues in the Castle Ward" (`adventure-wdh.json:4059`), where the notes cited the wiki alone. The Griffon and the Sahuagin Humbled are Castle Ward because Dragon Heist gives the ward all of Mount Waterdeep (`:31007`).

campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:110: > - **Joroth Brighthelm's team (campaign only):** Laeral tells a Lioncrown: "Six tendays ago I sent a man named Joroth Brighthelm into Undermountain. The same event says the dispatch named Level 10, Falkir's Fist and the Eye of the Spider, and Dungeon of the Mad Mage puts both on Level 10 (`sources/adventure-wdmm.json:447`). campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:111: > - **Bregan D'aerthe's eyes (campaign only):** A drow woman at the Yawning Portal's balcony table "has been at that table every evening for two weeks, cataloguing who descends into Undermountain and who comes back" (`campaign/quests/act-i/finding-floon/ev-01-yawning-portal.md:29`). She is **Ryvarra**, and the Finding Floon design notes say that Jarlaxle has had eyes on the party "since their first night in the city" (`design-notes.md:11`). The company's Skullport drop in **Houseless Noble** is a ropewalk on the lower quay: "Xanathar's Guild holds the whole place, so say nothing of us in the street" (`campaign/quests/faction-events/bregan-daerthe/r50-houseless-noble/ev-01-houseless-noble.md:284`).
campaign/setting/geography/undermountain-and-skullport.md:114: > - **No help below (campaign only):** The campaign's faction contacts stop at the surface. "Sending works anywhere below, except to Halaster. Vajra casts the reserve above ground because she will not go below" (`force-grey/r50-force-grey-commander/ev-01-force-grey-commander.md:231`). **Vajra Safahr** matches the published rule that Halaster cannot be reached by *sending*. No spell carries a Lords' Alliance summons to a member below ground (`lords-alliance/r50-lioncrown/ev-01-lioncrown.md:16`), the Emerald Enclave's sprig does not work there (`emerald-enclave/r10-autumnreaver/ev-01-autumnreaver.md:134`), and the Veterans of **Warduke** "do not go down the Portal's well or into Undermountain" (`lords-alliance/r25-warduke/ev-01-warduke.md:115`).

campaign/setting/geography/waterdeep.md:10: > - **Quests and events:** Every quest is set in Waterdeep. The table under **The Campaign's Main Sites by Quest** says where each one plays, and the ward pages list the faction events.
campaign/setting/geography/waterdeep.md:16: Waterdeep is a walled port city on the Sword Coast, built on a plateau below Mount Waterdeep and known as the City of Splendors. The adventure calls it the most civilized city on the Sword Coast and the coast's grandest metropolis. Open Lord Laeral Silverhand governs it from the Palace beside the secret Masked Lords, the City Watch polices its streets, and the guilds touch every trade. The campaign opens at the Yawning Portal on Ches 1 and runs to Flamerule 11, with all four villain factions active in the city throughout, as **The Grand Game** sets out.
campaign/setting/geography/waterdeep.md:25: - **Taxes and fees:** Dagult Neverember set a monthly tax of 1 shard per person over ten in the first year of his reign, and the City Guard collects it door to door on the last day of each month. A person who pays a single dragon receives a writ that exempts them for twelve months. A ship that touches dock pays 5 dragons for a stay of up to fourteen days, and a stall in the Market rents for 1 nib a day. The roads charge no toll, and the adventure tells a traveler to report any Guard who asks for one.
campaign/setting/geography/waterdeep.md:42: | Sea Ward | The wealthiest ward, a district of walled villas, grand temples and the Field of Triumph. | **Cassalanter Villa**, **House of Inspired Hands**, the Field of Triumph, Rosznar Villa, Neverember House | **Sea Ward** |
campaign/setting/geography/waterdeep.md:66: - **The Open Lord and the Masked Lords:** Ahghairon founded the system in 1032 DR after he turned the warlord Raurlor's sword into a serpent and took the warlord's throne. He sat openly as lord while a council of near-equal lords ruled beside him, and their names are hidden even from one another. **Laeral Silverhand** holds the office now, since the other Lords voted **Dagult Neverember** out. A petitioner who is invited meets the Open Lord or the Masked Lords in the audience chamber of the Palace of Waterdeep, where Ahghairon's throne stands. Castle Waterdeep is a redoubt of last defense, the Palace is the seat of government, and no source gives the Masked Lords a seat or an office of their own. See **The Open Lord and the Masked Lords**.
campaign/setting/geography/waterdeep.md:80: - **Guild Law:** It sits outside the legal code, though the Watch and the magisters take a guild's traditions seriously. Joining is optional, but a non-member finds that guild members refuse their coin, which covers nearly everyone who sells the necessities of life. No thieves' guild is active in the city, and Xanathar's Guild is a faction with no guild standing.

campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:8: > - **What this page is:** the institution entry for Waterdeep's police force, and the third of the four governance pages. It covers how the Watch is organized, where it is based, who is named in it, how fast it answers noise, and which faction benefits name it. It also holds short entries for the City Guard with the Griffon Cavalry, the City Navy and the Watchful Order of Magists and Protectors, because players and sources keep confusing them with the Watch.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:9: > - **Where it is used:** read it before any scene in which the party meets a patrol, reports a crime, is arrested or makes noise near a walled villa. Read it before you voice Saeth Cromley or Barnibus Blastwind, and before you resolve a faction benefit that names the Watch.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/city-watch.md:81: > - **How the Watch fits the Grand Game:** The Watch is trying to do its job, and it knows that some cases are politically untouchable. Most officers are not corrupt by rule and can be pressed from above. The Watch has informants in every ward, and every faction has informants in the Watch. Laeral's information comes largely from the Watch and from Jalester, and it is about three moves behind (see **A City Watching Itself**).

campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/code-legal-and-magisters.md:62: > - **Running a hearing:** In the Dock Ward courthouse the magister is Umbero Zastro, and in the Castle Ward courthouse she is Hester Barch. Zastro gives the party an hour to plead and asks questions while he listens. He finds a character who deceives him guilty of hampering justice. A party that pleads for leniency with a DC 14 Charisma (Persuasion) check receives an alternate punishment for any crime that does not carry death or exile, and the check has advantage for a character with renown in a respectable faction.

campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/open-lord-and-masked-lords.md:17: The Open Lord and the Masked Lords are the ruling council of Waterdeep, made up of one lord who sits in public and a body of other lords whose names are kept secret. Ahghairon founded the arrangement in 1032 DR, after he turned the warlord Raurlor's own sword into a serpent that killed him. Ahghairon then seated himself on the warlord's throne in the audience chamber, in front of the leaders of the city's armies and powerful families. From that seat he decreed that he would sit openly as lord while a council of other lords of nearly equal power ruled with him. The Open Lord still sits on that throne. Laeral Silverhand has held the office since the other Lords voted Dagult Neverember out a few years ago.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/open-lord-and-masked-lords.md:32: - **How the office changes hands:** The only recorded removal is Dagult Neverember, whom the other Lords voted out while he was in Neverwinter. Laeral stepped into the vacancy reluctantly, at the request of the Masked Lords. The published adventure gives no procedure for choosing or removing a Lord.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/open-lord-and-masked-lords.md:49: **Dagult Neverember** (Lawful Neutral, Illuskan Human, he/him) was Open Lord until the other Lords voted him out, and he is now Lord of Neverwinter. His history belongs to **The Open Lord's Fall**.
campaign/setting/governance-and-guilds/open-lord-and-masked-lords.md:68: > - **Faction ties of the other Lords:** Mirt is a senior Harper, and Laeral is the Lords' Alliance's highest authority in the city. Ulkoria belongs to the Watchful Order. Caladorn was a Cassalanter. The campaign gives no faction to Thardouk Starbuckler or Dorgar Adarbrent.

campaign/setting/grand-game.md:1: # The Grand Game
campaign/setting/grand-game.md:7: ### The Grand Game
campaign/setting/grand-game.md:9: **Purpose:** Gives the GM the in-world frame for the faction competition — not faction stats, but the texture of how power actually works in Waterdeep.
campaign/setting/grand-game.md:13: *What the Grand Game Is*
campaign/setting/grand-game.md:15: - Not formally organized; no governing body; called the Grand Game because everyone who plays it understands they are playing it
campaign/setting/grand-game.md:19: - **Informants**: the Watch has informants in every ward; every faction has informants in the Watch; Jarlaxle has informants in every other faction; Skeemo Weirdbottle compiles all of it
campaign/setting/grand-game.md:21: - **The Yawning Portal**: Durnan has heard everything; he shares selectively; every faction knows this and cultivates him anyway
campaign/setting/grand-game.md:32: | Xanathar's Guild | Stone was with Dalakhar (now Gralhund/PCs); Dalakhar worked for Neverember | Eye #1 | Pursuing the Stone; hunting Dalakhar's trail |
campaign/setting/grand-game.md:36: | Doom Raiders | Aware of Grand Game at high level; not primary competitors for vault | Nothing | Maintaining territory; watching |
