campaign/setting/history/00-the-history.md:9: > - **Where it is used:** The GM opens it to find the page that answers a history question, such as where a faction came from, who held the city before Laeral Silverhand, or what date it is.
campaign/setting/history/00-the-history.md:11: > - **Spoiler level:** Everything above the Overview is GM-only. The Overview is the public history of the city and names no faction secret.
campaign/setting/history/00-the-history.md:21: > - The Cassalanters belong to the city itself, and Dagult Neverember built his fortune in Waterdeep. Their dealings in 1489 DR and the years just before it are the source of the Grand Game's prize and of the Founders' Day deadline.
campaign/setting/history/00-the-history.md:22: > - Each page is a continuous narrative history, written so that one era sets up the next. No page carries a timeline table, and no page is organized by faction.
campaign/setting/history/00-the-history.md:33: - **The Open Lord's Fall** (about 1467 DR to 1489 DR, with the aftermath running to 1492 DR): Dagult Neverember's years in office, about 21 to 22 of them, the embezzled gold, the Stone of Golorr and the vote that removed him. It is the origin of the Grand Game's prize.
campaign/setting/history/00-the-history.md:35: - **The Players Arrive** (Marpenoth 1491 DR to Ches 1, 1492 DR): Where each faction and object stood on the night before the campaign, and which of those dates the sources fix.
campaign/setting/history/00-the-history.md:42: **Tone:** Grave and measured, in the Ember history register. The hub summarises and leaves the telling to the Era pages, where named people make decisions for understandable reasons. The writing principle for the journal is a continuous narrative history organized by era, with each era setting up the next, no timeline tables and no faction-organized subsections.
campaign/setting/history/00-the-history.md:58: - **The Grand Game**: the in-world frame for the factions that the later eras explain.
campaign/setting/history/00-the-history.md:59: - **Xanathar**, **Manshoon**, **Jarlaxle Baenre** and **The Cassalanters** (Villains): the four villain pages that the Era pages for each faction feed.

campaign/setting/history/01-ahghairons-gifts.md:141: > - **Research:** The closing idea ("built to survive bad rulers, and the campaign is the test") is campaign framing with no source. So is the old claim that the dragonward, the Code Legal and the City Watch were protections built against tyranny. The Open Lord "designed to be removed if they fail the city" has been dropped, since only the Neverember vote supports any removal.
campaign/setting/history/02-the-long-shadow.md:43: - Anyone can join after committing a serious crime. Low-ranking members speculate about their boss, and few have any inkling that it is a beholder (WDH, Chapter 1 faction entry).
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:59: - Luskan is a free port on the northern Sword Coast. Five High Captains rule it, and each leads a faction called a Ship. The five Ships are named for their founders, Taerl, Baram, Kurth, Suljack and Rethnor. The wiki names these Ships after their captains in 1372 DR and lists no High Captains for 1480 DR, so this page names the Ships and not the people who lead them now. The old block's "Ship Captains council" means the High Captains.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:65: - He also wants to oust Neverwinter and its lord, Dagult Neverember, from the Alliance. The published adventure calls Neverember his fiercest political opponent.

campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:120: - **Laeral Silverhand** and **Dagult Neverember** (Notable Figures): the two leaders on the other side of the Lords' Alliance scheme.
campaign/setting/history/03-bregan-daerthes-long-road.md:160: > - **Settled by the user (Session 47):** When Jarlaxle learned of the vault. "He's known about it since getting to Waterdeep, whenever that was." The body has him arrive already knowing, and the nimblewright ledger (first sale Marpenoth 3, 1491 DR) puts his arrival no later than that. The **Jarlaxle Informed** outcome set in **Fireball!** (`villains/jarlaxle.md` lines 3 and 13) records only that the party spoke with him. It gates his Bregan D'aerthe team at **Gralhund Villa** and his Phase 1 Zardoz Zord activity, and it tells him nothing new. `running-the-villains.md` lines 25 and 41 and `fireball/overview.md` line 38 stand. Overruled: the Alexandrian line that he is unaware of the Stone and the Grand Game at the start (`1. The Villains.pdf` p.17).

The wiki ties Neverember to the Zhentarim through his advisor Rian Nightshade (The Rise of Tiamat, 5e), which could reconcile the two. This page does not adopt it.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:8: > - **What this is:** The Era page for the Cassalanters' bargain with Asmodeus, the backstory of the villain faction that runs on a fixed deadline. It is the fifth of eight eras, after **The Founding of Chains** and before **The Open Lord's Fall**.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:17: **Span:** 1489 DR to 1492 DR, three years. The published adventure says only "three years ago" (lines 18294 and 18403). The year 1489 DR is that phrase counted back from 1492 DR. The Forgotten Realms Wiki states 1489 DR for the pact on its Cassalanter, Victoro, Ammalia and Osvaldo pages, and those figures derive from the same adventure line. The Alexandrian timeline dates Neverember's deposition to 1489 DR and gives no pact year. The campaign opens on Ches 1, 1492 DR.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:71: - The 500,000 Dragons is the sum Neverember embezzled. The family holds about 500,000 gp of its own, and the Vault's 500,000 gp is the shortfall.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:88: > - No faction or NPC outside the household knows this. They suspect the Cassalanters because the family is too nice to be this rich and this purebred, and they know nothing.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:116: - **The Founding of Chains** and **The Open Lord's Fall**: the eras on either side. Neverember's embezzlement, which the Cassalanters' near bankruptcy may trace to, belongs to the later one. The Alexandrian author's backstory supports the link (see Draft Notes).
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:123: - **The Grand Game**: where the Cassalanters sit among the factions.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:130: > - **Timeline.** 1489 DR: Neverember is deposed, the family is on the verge of bankruptcy, the pact is signed and Osvaldo is taken. The contract claims each child at nine, and Osvaldo was already nine. The deposition year is sourced. The pact year is the arithmetic of "three years ago" (`adventure-wdh.json` line 18294), and the wiki restates that arithmetic as 1489 DR.
campaign/setting/history/05-a-debt-in-blood.md:145: > - **Conflicts:** The link from Neverember's embezzlement to the family's near bankruptcy is not in the published adventure, which gives the near bankruptcy and the disappearance but no cause. The Alexandrian author's backstory supports it ("bad investments combined with Dagult's embezzlements"), so it is a remix source and not only a campaign invention (`arc-g` line 11).
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:8: > - **What this is:** The Era page for Dagult Neverember's years as Open Lord and the vote that ended them. It is the sixth of eight eras, after **A Debt in Blood** and before **A City Watching Itself**, and it is the origin of the Grand Game's prize.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:9: > - **Where it is used:** The GM reads it before voicing Renaer Neverember, Laeral Silverhand or Aurinax, and before answering any contact's question about who Neverember was and why every faction wants what he hid.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:11: > - **Spoiler level:** Everything above the Overview is GM-only. The Overview gives Neverember's years as Open Lord and Laeral's succession as the public knows them, and it leaves out the embezzlement, the Stone, the vault and the Eyes.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:13: **Purpose:** Lets the GM say what Neverember did with the city's gold, how he lost his office, and where the vault, the Stone and the three Eyes stood when the vote was taken.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:15: **Keywords:** Dagult Neverember, Open Lord, Masked Lords, Laeral Silverhand, Neverwinter, Renaer Neverember, Alethea Brandath, the Stone of Golorr, the Vault of Dragons, Aurinax, Neverember's Enigma
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:17: **Span:** About 1467 DR to 1489 DR, which is about 21 to 22 years in office, with the aftermath running to the campaign opening in 1492 DR. The start year is 1467 DR in the Alexandrian timeline and the wiki. The published adventure confirms Neverember as Open Lord in 1475 DR and dates the ouster only to "a few years ago". The year 1489 DR comes from the Alexandrian timeline and the wiki, and the Era 5 page uses it. The campaign opens on Ches 1, 1492 DR.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:19: **Other Names:** Neverember's Enigma (the cryptic phrase the factions use for the missing gold and the secret behind it); the Ouster. "Neverember's Enigma" is in the Alexandrian timeline and in **Running the Villains**. "The Ouster" is a GM name. `[research]`
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:24: > - Dagult Neverember was Open Lord of Waterdeep from about 1467 DR to about 1489 DR, some 21 to 22 years. His money and political skill helped the city recover, and he levied a monthly tax that is still collected. He spent growing amounts of time rebuilding Neverwinter.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:27: > - Neverember remains Lord of Neverwinter. His son Renaer lives in Waterdeep, estranged from him, and carried one of the three Eyes in a mourning locket without knowing it.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:28: > - At campaign start Neverember still wants the gold, and Aurinax is still bound to a man who no longer holds the office.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:32: - Dagult Neverember is an Illuskan human noble who built a mercantile fortune out of Waterdeep. He became Open Lord about 1467 DR, and the published adventure confirms him in office in 1475 DR.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:43: - During his tenure Neverember began embezzling from the Waterdeep treasury. The published adventure puts the total at half a million dragons.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:45: - The Alexandrian author says Neverember used the local Zhentarim's resources to carry out the embezzlement, and this is how Manshoon's cell learned of it. See **The Founding of Chains** for the timing problem.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:48: - While he held office, the factions of the city noticed that he was engaged in some large scheme and knew only its outline. They began to call it Neverember's Enigma.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:53: - Neverember found the vault while arranging the interment of his wife, Lady Alethea Brandath. He moved the remains of Lord Anri out of the oldest family mausoleum and buried her there, and he found the passage the Brandaths had forgotten.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:54: - He stored the embezzled gold in the vault. To protect the secret, a wizard erased the vault's location from his mind and from the minds of his subordinates and trapped it inside the Stone of Golorr. The wizard then disappeared, and Neverember hid the Stone in the Palace of Waterdeep.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:56: - The campaign adds that Neverember blinded the Stone by removing its three Eyes. Without all three, it reveals nothing useful. The Eyes are placed as set out in the Secret below.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:57: - Aurinax, an adult gold dragon, holds the dragonstaff of Ahghairon and can ignore the dragonward. Neverember let him keep the staff in exchange for guarding the vault until Neverember or his appointed vassals came to remove the gold.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:58: - The published adventure says Neverember made the bargain when he became Open Lord. The campaign has him make it after he found the vault, and the second order is the one this page follows.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:63: - The Masked Lords voted Neverember out of office in 1489 DR, while he was in Neverwinter. The published adventure gives no date and says it happened "a few years ago".
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:65: - Neverember stayed in Neverwinter, where he governs as its lord. The campaign pages call his removal an exile and call it public. The published adventure says only that he was ousted, and the wiki says the vote effectively exiled him.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:67: - The wiki says Neverember grew bitter about the vote and blamed Waterdeep's nobles and guilds for it. Resentment of him stayed high in Waterdeep, and some Waterdhavians feared he would start a war to win the city back.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:68: - The wiki says Neverember led the Council of Waterdeep, the alliance against the Cult of the Dragon, until the vote cost him that place, and that he kept attending its meetings afterward (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Dagult Neverember; The Rise of Tiamat, 5e). `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:69: - The wiki says that when Masked Lords were murdered in 1491 DR, Neverember was accused at once. Laeral spoke to him by spell, read his mind and found him innocent (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Dagult Neverember; Death Masks). `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:70: - The wiki gives Neverember a quiet tie to the Zhentarim through his advisor Rian Nightshade. He did not advertise it and would not deny it if asked (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Dagult Neverember; The Rise of Tiamat, 5e). See **The Founding of Chains**. `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:75: - Renaer Neverember is Dagult's son by Lady Alethea Brandath. His mother died when Renaer was still in his youth and left her property to Renaer and nothing to Dagult (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Dagult Neverember and Renaer Neverember; Blackstaff Tower). The published adventure gives only the inheritance (`adventure-wdh.json` lines 32546-32547), which is the minimum this page relies on. Renaer now owns more property than anyone else in Waterdeep, all of it inherited from her.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:83: Dagult Neverember, a wealthy noble who built his fortune in trade, was Open Lord of Waterdeep from about 1467 DR, and his money and political skill helped the city recover from years of neglect. His reign brought the monthly door-to-door tax that Waterdhavians still pay, and he spent much of his time rebuilding Neverwinter, where he is lord today. In 1489 DR the Masked Lords voted him out of office while he was in Neverwinter, and Laeral Silverhand, one of the Seven Sisters, took the seat at their request. His son Renaer lives in Waterdeep, estranged from him, and owns the properties his mother left him.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:88: > - Neverember placed the three Eyes in three places before the vote. He kept one with him in Neverwinter, he gave one to Renaer inside an ivory mourning locket in honor of his mother, and he hid one in a crypt of the Brandath Mausoleum near Alethea's tomb. The Renaer and Mausoleum placements are in the Alexandrian account, and the crypt is in the Vault of Dragons quest opener.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:91: > - Neverember's agents did not know the full picture. Dalakhar was told to watch Renaer and not told why, because Neverember's chief concern was keeping the hidden Eye safe until he could regain the Stone.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:93: > - Neverember still wants the gold back or, failing that, vindication. The vault's location was erased from his mind, so he cannot find it himself, and Aurinax will release the gold only to Neverember or his appointed vassals.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:95: **Tone:** Grave and fair to Neverember, in the Ember history register. State his real improvements and his theft as two facts side by side, with no narrator verdict on either. The Overview stays on the public record and the GM bullets state hidden facts flatly.

campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:149: > - **Research:** The date of Alethea's death, the dates of each step between the embezzlement and the blinding of the Stone, and when the Stone reached Xanathar are all undated in every source. The wiki says only that she died in Renaer's youth. The Alexandrian order has Neverember find the vault while arranging her interment, which puts her death inside his tenure, and no source gives Renaer's age at the time.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:151: > - **Research:** The wiki cites "Death Masks" for several claims on Neverember's tenure and for Laeral's appointment, and the book could not be checked. The 1467 and 1468 DR split is real in the wiki: Lords of Waterdeep gives Neverember's tenure as circa 1468 DR to circa 1489 DR through a template reference, so its edition is not shown. Dagult Neverember gives circa 1467 DR for the office, also through a template, and 1468 DR for New Neverwinter (Neverwinter Campaign Setting, 4e). Both figures stand in the body.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:153: > - **Conflicts:** Exile. `arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md` line 17 ("exile to Neverwinter made permanent and public"), `design-notes-running-the-campaign.md` line 105 ("publicly exiled") and `guides/factions/03-lords-alliance.md` line 7 ("Lord Neverember's exile") say exile. `adventure-wdh.json` line 452 says only voted out, and the wiki says effectively exiled. `arc-j` line 17 also calls the office "Lord Regent of Waterdeep", which no other source uses. This page says "removed" and "voted out" and keeps "exile" for the campaign's wording.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:157: > - **Conflicts:** The Stone's origin. The published adventure (lines 451 and 453) has a wizard erase the memory and calls the Stone an aboleth transformed by magic. The Alexandrian has Neverember find it in Neverwinter and a Netherese arcanist make it centuries ago (`Act_III_Arc_E.md` line 849). `arc-j` line 13 says an aboleth-forged artifact "acquired years before". The Patreon material, per the findings note, has it come from a wizard tower ruin.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:158: > - **Conflicts:** When Aurinax was bound. `adventure-wdh.json` line 31746 says Neverember struck the deal when he became Open Lord. `arc-j` lines 13-15 place it after he found the vault. This page follows the campaign.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:160: > - **Conflicts:** Dalakhar's employer. `setting/grand-game.md` line 32 says Dalakhar "worked for Renaer". The Dalakhar page (line 26), the Alexandrian timeline and the published adventure say he served Neverember, and the Alexandrian has him watch Renaer on Neverember's behalf.
campaign/setting/history/06-the-open-lords-fall.md:168: > - **Routed facts:** The wiki and the published adventure (p.131) record a hostile rivalry between Neverember and Jarlaxle over Luskan's Lords' Alliance bid. It belongs on **Bregan D'aerthe's Long Road** and the Jarlaxle villain page.

campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:15: **Keywords:** Laeral Silverhand, Open Lord, Masked Lords, Dagult Neverember, Force Grey, Vajra Safahr, Lords' Alliance, Jalester Silvermane, Elminster, Writ
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:17: **Span:** About 1489 DR to the campaign (1492 DR). The Alexandrian files date Laeral's accession and Neverember's deposition to 1489 DR, the Forgotten Realms Wiki says about 1489 DR, and the published adventure says only "a few years ago." The start year is therefore derived. The campaign opens on Ches 1, 1492 DR.
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:24: > - Laeral Silverhand became Open Lord about three years before the campaign, after the other Lords voted Dagult Neverember out and asked her to take his place. She accepted reluctantly, was overwhelmed at first, and has since settled into the work.
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:34: - The Masked Lords voted Dagult Neverember out while he was rebuilding Neverwinter. They asked Laeral to fill the vacancy, and she took it reluctantly.
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:36: - The wiki adds that Mystra herself appeared to Laeral and asked her to keep the North stable by becoming Open Lord, and that Laeral won the Masked Lords' support and replaced Neverember (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Laeral Silverhand; Death Masks and Sword Coast Adventurer's Guide, 5e). `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:38: - The wiki lists seven Open Lords to 1489 DR: Ahghairon, Baeron Silmaeril, Lhestyn, Piergeiron the Paladinson, Caladorn Cassalanter, Dagult Neverember and Laeral Silverhand (Forgotten Realms Wiki, Lords of Waterdeep; City of Splendors boxed set Campaign Guide and Sword Coast Adventurer's Guide, 5e). Caladorn was Victoro Cassalanter's father; see **A Debt in Blood**. `[research: wiki only]`
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:52: - Neverember set the gold aside by decree and had a lock made that no Masked Lord could open. The institution that Ahghairon built was locked out of its own treasury.
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:55: - She wants the gold in the treasury. In her framing the money was taxed from Waterdhavian citizens and merchants, and Neverember's exile does not change who owns it.
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:59: ### Her Place in the Grand Game
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:61: - Laeral does not play the Grand Game herself. The Lords' Alliance acts on her behalf, and it will not let the party keep the gold.
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:62: - Members who bring the Grand Game to Jalester draw her interest. Once she is fully informed she makes the recovery a political priority and gives the party real resources.
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:65: - She judges that nobody is winning the Grand Game, and she would like the trading to stop before someone she knows is hurt.
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:66: - This agrees with the City Watch's place in **The Grand Game**: the government is trying, aware that some cases are politically untouchable, and it learns what it knows from informants who are a step behind the factions.
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:78: Laeral Silverhand is the Open Lord of Waterdeep, and she has held the office since about 1489 DR. In that year the Masked Lords voted Dagult Neverember out and asked her to take his place. She is the fifth of the goddess Mystra's seven daughters, an archmage of great age and renown who was once the Witch-Queen of the North and the wife of the wizard Khelben Arunsun. She governs from the Palace with the help of trusted deputies and advisors, among them Mirt, and the city has been quiet under her.
campaign/setting/history/07-a-city-watching-itself.md:83: > - **Timeline.** 765 DR: Laeral is born, in the published adventure. 1467 DR: the Alexandrian files have Neverember in office as Open Lord and proclaiming himself ruler of Neverwinter. The wiki says he took office about 1468 DR, and the adventure shows him in office in 1475 DR. About 1489 DR: the Lords vote him out and Laeral takes the seat, which ends roughly twenty-one years of his tenure. The 1489 DR year is derived.

campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:8: > - **What this page is:** The lore entry for the Stone of Golorr, the aboleth artifact that holds the location of the Vault of Dragons, and for the three Eyes that Dagult Neverember removed to blind it.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:16: The Stone of Golorr is an aboleth that magic has turned into an artifact, and it holds the location of the Vault of Dragons. Dagult Neverember blinded it by removing three gemstone Eyes and putting them in three separate hands, so that a thief who stole the Stone alone would learn nothing. When the campaign opens, Xanathar holds one Eye, the Cassalanters hold one and Jarlaxle holds one.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:23: - **Who made it:** The published adventure has a wizard erase the vault's location from Neverember's mind and trap it in the Stone, and the wizard then disappears. The campaign names no maker, and its quest opener says only that Neverember acquired the artifact "years before".
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:26: - **Memory:** The Stone's influence suppresses memory in people who have been near it. Kalain cannot recall what Neverember told her about the "Melairkyn ceremonial vault", and nothing she tries brings it back.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:27: - **Memory erasure:** The Alexandrian source describes a ritual in which the Stone removes one piece of knowledge from everyone except its attuned user. Neverember used it only on the vault's location, so the vault's existence and the keys to it can still be found. Vault of Dragons refers to this ritual as a way for the party to cover its tracks.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:34: - **Why they were removed:** Without all three Eyes seated, the Stone reveals nothing useful about the vault. Neverember took them out to protect the secret from anyone who might steal the Stone.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:35: - **Where Neverember put them:** He kept one with him in Neverwinter. He gave one to his son Renaer inside an ivory mourning locket made in honor of Renaer's mother, and Renaer never knew what it held. He hid one in a crypt of the Brandath Mausoleum near Alethea Brandath's tomb.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:36: - **How a character learns the Eyes exist:** The first attunement tells the character that three Eyes were removed. The Eyes Letter in Kalain's lockbox, an unsigned note written about four years before the campaign, confirms that Neverember held all three.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:42: - **Eye #2, the Cassalanters:** It lay in a Brandath crypt until the Cassalanters found it through infernal divination and took it from Neverember's magical wards. It is secured behind the base of the statue in the ceremonial hall of the temple beneath **Cassalanter Villa** (A7). The family knows what it does.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:50: - **Before 1489 DR:** Neverember hides the Stone in the Palace of Waterdeep after the vault's location is erased from his mind and trapped in it.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:51: - **After the vote:** Neverember's spies take the Stone from the Palace and are killed leaving the city. It passes from hand to hand until it reaches Xanathar.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:52: - **Ches 20:** Dalakhar steals it from Xanathar's lair on Neverember's orders.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:94: - **What the vault needs:** The complete Stone, with all three Eyes restored, and the command phrase, which the Stone gives at the third Eye. The ceremony itself is covered on **The Vault of Dragons and Neverember's Embezzlement** and in **Vault of Dragons**.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:96: - **What the party can learn early:** **Vault Opening Method Known** records that the party has the Melairkyn notes (dragon scale, mithral hammer and sunlight). **Eyes Letter Found** records the unsigned letter that confirms the three Eyes. Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game in **Kolat Towers** (E9) carries a partly decoded command phrase, and finishing it needs the Full Awakening or more research.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:97: - **If the party holds fewer than three Eyes:** **Vault of Dragons** cannot be run yet. The lair heists can be run in any order, and the Stone's impressions and the faction debriefs point to whichever Eye remains.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:107: - **Player-faction contacts:** At their first meetings, the Harpers, Emerald Enclave, Force Grey, Lords' Alliance and Order of the Gauntlet contacts all refuse to discuss the Stone or the vault.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:111: - **No lair journal yet:** **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa** and **Sea Maidens Faire** have no quest journal, so no Event Outcome marks an Eye restored. The GM counts Eyes restored from the "Eyes recovered" row of the **Vault of Dragons** faction-state table, which should read 3 of 3.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:113: - **Eyes Letter Found:** Set in **Fireball!** when the party has the unsigned letter that confirms Neverember held all three Eyes four years ago. **Vault of Dragons** reads it.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:128: > - **Answering a player who asks what the Stone of Golorr is:** Give the Overview. The party's own evidence, from the Stone itself and from faction debriefs, is the only thing that adds to it.
campaign/setting/lore/stone-of-golorr-and-the-eyes.md:133: > - **Neverember's knowledge:** The vault's location was erased from his mind, so he cannot find the vault himself. He still wants the gold or, failing that, vindication.

campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:1: # The Vault of Dragons and Neverember's Embezzlement
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:8: > - **What this page is:** The lore entry for the gold Dagult Neverember embezzled from Waterdeep, the dwarf-built vault where he hid it, the doors that seal it and the dragon who guards it.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:9: > - **Where it is used:** The GM reads it before the party reaches the City of the Dead, before voicing Aurinax, Laeral Silverhand or Neverember on the gold, and whenever a player asks where the money came from or how the doors open.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:10: > - **Quests and events:** **Vault of Dragons** (still a structure doc) holds the approach in Scene 2, the opening in Scene 3, the vault and Aurinax in Scene 4, the faction confrontation in Scene 5 and the gold in Scene 6. **Fireball!** supplies the rumor in **The Fireball** and the vault-opening method in **Kalain's Tower**. **Faction Outposts**, **Cassalanter Villa** and **Kolat Towers** each hand the party a route to the Brandath Crypts. **An Audience with the Open Lord** issues the Writ that Aurinax accepts, **Lioncrown** gives Laeral's account of the gold, and **Ten Nights in the City of the Dead**, **The Theater's Back Room** and **Dread Lord** mark outcomes that the Vault reads.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:14: **Purpose:** Lets the GM say how much Neverember took, when he lost office, what the vault is, how its doors open and who guards it, and keeps the two Brandath names apart without inventing a date that no source gives.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:16: The Vault of Dragons is a dwarf-built ceremonial vault beneath the Brandath Mausoleum in the City of the Dead. Dagult Neverember filled it with the half a million gold dragons he embezzled from Waterdeep, and the gold dragon Aurinax still guards it for him.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:18: This page covers the theft, the vault, its doors and its guardian. Every faction that hunts the Stone of Golorr is hunting this gold, so the embezzlement is the first cause of the Grand Game. The Stone and its three Eyes have their own page, **The Stone of Golorr and the Eyes**, and this page points to it.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:22: - **What he took:** Dragon Heist says Neverember embezzled "half a million dragons", and a dragon is a gold piece in Waterdeep. The hoard is 500,000 gp and weighs 10,000 pounds.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:23: - **How he held it:** In the campaign, Laeral says Waterdeep's merchants paid the money as taxes and Neverember set it aside by decree. She adds that he had a lock made that no Masked Lord could open and hid the key. She tells this to a Lords' Alliance member in **Lioncrown**, and **A City Watching Itself** repeats her account. No other page describes the method.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:24: - **When it began:** No source dates it. The Alexandrian timeline lists it after Neverember proclaimed himself ruler of Neverwinter in 1467 DR and before he found the vault, and it gives no year for either step.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:27: - **What it cost him:** A wizard erased the vault's location from Neverember's mind and from the minds of his subordinates, so he cannot find his own gold. He wants it back or, failing that, vindication.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:34: - **How Neverember found it:** Dragon Heist says Neverember married into the old Brandath family for its wealth, and Lady Alethea Brandath bore his son Renaer. Alethea asked for a simple tomb among her ancestors. While arranging her burial, Neverember took over the oldest family mausoleum, moved the remains of Lord Anri to a new crypt nearby and buried Alethea in his place. The work uncovered the passage the Brandaths had forgotten and the sealed doors beyond it.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:35: - **What he added:** A two-foot steel door at the end of the Old Crypts, hidden by an illusion, with a DC 22 lock and an *alarm* spell keyed to Neverember alone. The Stone's memory-erasure came later and is covered on **The Stone of Golorr and the Eyes**.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:43: - **How to write them:** The vault lies beneath the Mausoleum. The party walks the Crypts to reach it, and the Eye that Neverember hid near his wife's tomb was hidden in the Crypts.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:60: - **Learning the ceremony:** Four independent paths lead to it. The Stone's vision during the Full Awakening gives its shape, in the lines "Three keys ye seek. Strike scale of wyrm in light of day. As the vault bears witness, so shall it ope to mithral's strike." Kalain's notes in **Kalain's Tower** give it in full, in Neverember's hand. The Cassalanters know it from their own investigation and share it if the party's standing with them allows. Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game from **Kolat Towers** (area E9) gives an outline and a partial Elvish command phrase. The complete command phrase is the Stone's gift at the third Eye, the Full Awakening, and the party speaks it during the ceremony. The ceremony still needs the dragon scale, the sunlight and the mithral hammer.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:61: - **The window:** The 15-minute window matters in Scene 5. A faction that times its arrival to the ceremony rather than to the party's exit can catch the party holding the doors.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:66: - **The bargain:** Neverember let Aurinax keep the dragonstaff in exchange for guarding the vault until Neverember or his appointed vassals came to remove the gold. Dragon Heist places the deal when Neverember became Open Lord, and the campaign places it after he found the vault. No vassal was ever named.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:67: - **What the oath covers:** Only Neverember and his appointed vassals may remove the gold under Aurinax's watch, and none of them is in Waterdeep. He attacks only to defend himself, the staff or the gold.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:70: - **Oath-Release:** Aurinax accepts real authority. That means **Renaer Neverember** presented formally, the Masked Lord's Writ that **An Audience with the Open Lord** issues (the **Writ Issued** outcome), or a release phrase if the Stone or later research produced one. **Hlam**'s presence grants advantage on the Persuasion check that seals it.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:72: - **Legal Argument:** Neverember's ouster is public record, and a party that argues his binding oath has lapsed gives Aurinax grounds to agree.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:92: - **Dagult Neverember:** He wants the gold back, cannot find it and is in Neverwinter when the campaign opens.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:93: - **Renaer Neverember:** Renaer (Chaotic Good, Illuskan Human, he/him) refused to help his father hide the gold. He knows nothing about the vault, and his ignorance is real.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:109: Dagult Neverember was Open Lord of Waterdeep for some twenty years, until the Masked Lords voted him out in 1489 DR. He now governs Neverwinter, and Laeral Silverhand holds the seat he left. Waterdhavians whisper that a large sum of the city's gold went missing during his years, though nobody names a figure.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:114: > - **The Cassalanter link (campaign-only):** **Cassalanter Villa** says Neverember's embezzlement gutted the Cassalanter banking operations, because the family had made loans against civic funds that no longer existed, and Victoro's investments then collapsed in sequence. That ruin is what drove the family to the bargain described on **A Debt in Blood**. No published or Alexandrian text read for this page makes the link. Dragon Heist gives the near bankruptcy and no cause.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:116: > - **Timing of the link:** The embezzlement ran during Neverember's tenure and the pact dates to about 1489 DR, the year of the ouster. No source says when the money went missing in relation to the pact, so do not give the embezzlement a start year at the table.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:120: > - **Neverember at the end:** He arrives at the City of the Dead gate with hired muscle and Watch loyalists after the party has left the vault. The Scene 5 duel with Renaer is optional and player-driven.
campaign/setting/lore/vault-of-dragons.md:122: **Tone:** Grave and fair to Neverember, as on **The Open Lord's Fall**. Treat the vault as a shrine to keeping secrets that happens to hold a fortune, and let the gold come last.

campaign/setting/notable-figures/bregan-daerthe/06-fenerus-stormcastle.md:12: **Resonance.** Fenerus should inspire wariness with a man who has a long criminal record and no reason to be honest; grudging respect when his leverage over the Stone's location proves real; and sympathy for the impossible position that placed him at the intersection of every faction's interest simultaneously.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/02-ammalia-cassalanter.md:23: **Persona.** To the world, Ammalia is well-mannered, well-read, well-traveled, and known for driving a hard bargain. Her butterfly garden is the most beautiful in Waterdeep, and she supervises her youngest children at play there. To those she trusts, she worships Asmodeus alongside Victoro and shares the same pact and the same desperation. Deep down, while Victoro hunts Neverember's gold, Ammalia makes the plans: the feast, the poison, the 99 souls. She also goes to the attic where Osvaldo is bound, and reads him his favorite stories from a chair placed just outside the shielding circle while he curses her name and pleads with her to save him.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/10-seffia-naelryke.md:29: - If the Cassalanters turn hostile to the party, her team joins the Eye heist at **Xanathar's Lair** as a competing faction.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/13-ivrala-sorn.md:16: **Motivations.** Ivrala wants to collect patron and faction intelligence on behalf of the Cassalanters, maintain the server cover, and pass regular reports to Willifort Crowelle.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/cassalanters/13-ivrala-sorn.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Ivrala Sorn is a reliable, professional server who arrives on time, keeps orders correct, and remembers patron preferences. To those who know what they are looking at, she is filing weekly reports on Trollskull Manor's regulars, their faction affiliations, and who is drinking with whom. Deep down, Sister Valdra has seen her face in Cassalanter Villa's staff quarters, and she recognizes it.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/city-officials/01-laeral-silverhand.md:15: **Motivations.** Laeral wants to keep Waterdeep stable after Neverember's ouster, conceal the extent of her diminished power, and resist the urge to reclaim the Blackstaff (which holds Khelben Arunsun's soul).
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/01-davil-starsong.md:12: **Resonance.** Davil should inspire liking with his effortless charm, unease with the silence behind his questions, and sympathy when his arrest and return reveal the cost of leading a faction that eats its own.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/01-davil-starsong.md:26: **Relationships.** Davil is the cell leader of the Doom Raiders (Waterdeep Zhentarim), the faction contact for Zhentarim-aligned PCs, a permanent resident at the Yawning Portal (second floor business room, third floor suite), a colleague of Istrid, Tashlyn, Ziraj, and the traitor Skeemo Weirdbottle, and the unknowing target of Nihiloor's intellect devourer, with Meloon watching him and waiting for a clean opportunity to kill him without witnesses.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/05-tashlyn-yafeera.md:12: **Resonance.** Tashlyn should inspire respect for her martial competence, irritation with her unfettered superiority, and grudging trust when she takes over as faction contact with more efficiency and fewer words than Davil.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/05-tashlyn-yafeera.md:26: **Relationships.** Tashlyn is the Doom Raiders' Master of Arms and Mercenaries, Davil's replacement as faction contact after his arrest, and a leader who communicates exclusively by flying snake, using fewer words and expecting more inference.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/doom-raiders/07-senna-vael.md:26: **Relationships.** Senna Vael is the Doom Raiders' server candidate placed in Trollskull Manor, a direct report to Davil Starsong, and a plant who may be disclosed to the party as courtesy (or leverage) once they progress sufficiently in the Doom Raiders missions. Her intelligence reports cover patron activity and faction traffic, not the party specifically, unless the party becomes interesting.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/gralhunds/05-chirada.md:16: **Motivations.** Chirada wants to recover the Second Eye of the Stone of Golorr for what remains of Gralhund House interests, track the movements of whoever holds the Stone, and complete the mission described in the Missive from Castle Ward without becoming another casualty of the Grand Game.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/harpers/04-bonnie.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, "Bonnie" is the Yawning Portal barmaid who remembers your order, laughs at the right moments, and has the particular skill of making every patron feel like the most interesting person in the room. To those who know what she is, she is the leader of a five-doppelganger operation who found Waterdeep worth staying for, and who will not have that disrupted by a faction's curiosity. Deep down, she reads people professionally and personally, and she has never met anyone she could entirely stop reading — including herself.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-adversaries/01-dagult-neverember.md:1: # Dagult Neverember
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-adversaries/01-dagult-neverember.md:15: **Motivations.** Dagult wants his stolen gold back — or failing that, some measure of vindication that what he did was defensible — wants to repair, or at least stabilize, what is left of his relationship with Renaer, and wants to ensure the fortune he hid does not simply enrich whichever faction gets to it first.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-adversaries/01-dagult-neverember.md:25: **Relationships.** Dagult is the estranged father of Renaer Neverember, the man who bound Aurinax to guard the Vault of Dragons in exchange for the dragonstaff of Ahghairon, the disgraced former Open Lord whose fall from power set the Grand Game in motion, and the reason the Stone of Golorr was ever blinded in the first place.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-adversaries/01-dagult-neverember.md:29: Dagult Neverember is the disgraced former Open Lord of Waterdeep who embezzled 500,000 gp from the city before his removal from office; the gold he hid is the prize every faction in the Grand Game is chasing, and he intends to reclaim it — or at least to ensure no one else profits cleanly from his theft.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-adversaries/02-the-black-viper.md:26: **Relationships.** The Black Viper is Lady Esvele Rosznar of House Rosznar (a member the house would prefer to forget if she were caught), an independent cat burglar with no faction affiliation, a wildcard who can be recruited for a Cassalanter Villa infiltration with a DC 14 Persuasion or Intimidation check, and a recurring complication in any noble estate the party decides to rob. Her base of operations — the Viper Den — sits directly above the Brandath Crypts on the approaches to the Vault of Dragons, which means she has mapped the crypt access in more detail than any living person outside the Cassalanter household. In **Cassalanter Villa** she attends the Cassalanter event as a social guest and slips away mid-dinner to rob her hosts, creating a Batman/Catwoman dynamic with any party also running reconnaissance that evening. In **Vault of Dragons** her knowledge of the crypt is an asset worth cultivating or a complication worth managing. She has been mapping the Brandath Crypts for three months and has located the vault entrance, but cannot open it — she has no Stone, no Eyes, no dragon to negotiate with. Her plan is to be present when someone else opens it and take the most portable, highest-value items before the gold conversation bogs down. She is not interested in the gold itself: 500,000 gp weighs ten thousand pounds and cannot be moved by a lone cat burglar. She is interested in whatever magical artifacts, gems, and documentation are inside. When the party arrives at the crypts in **Vault of Dragons**, Esvele is already there doing reconnaissance. She does not antagonize them unless they attempt to cut her out of the take entirely.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-adversaries/04-filthy-meg.md:18: **Inspirations.** When playing Filthy Meg, channel Madame de Pompadour (information as power), Tyrion Lannister's spymaster instincts (*Game of Thrones*), and Niska (*Firefly*) — the businesslike menace of someone who operates outside every faction's rules.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-adversaries/04-filthy-meg.md:30: Filthy Meg is a heavily tattooed merfolk information broker who operates out of Mistshore near the harbor; she is not affiliated with any faction, which is exactly what makes her valuable, and her prices reflect it.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/01-volothamp-volo-geddarm.md:26: **Relationships.** Volo is a close friend of Floon Blagmaar and Renaer Neverember, the man who outbid Emmek Frewn for the Trollskull Manor deed (which he gives to the party), and a regular at the Yawning Portal who has worn out his welcome with Durnan at least twice.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/02-renaer-neverember.md:1: # Renaer Neverember
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/02-renaer-neverember.md:16: **Motivations.** Renaer wants to live on his own terms without his father's shadow, protect the people who get hurt because of the Neverember name, and prove that estrangement from Dagult is conviction rather than cowardice.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/02-renaer-neverember.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Renaer is the estranged son of Dagult Neverember: striking, well-read, a collector of rare tomes, and the most useful person at any table in Waterdeep because he knows the city's factions, geography, and recent politics the way other people know their own street. He owns more property than anyone in the city, all of it inherited from his mother (Dagult received nothing). To those he trusts, he is self-deprecating about his name, deflects sincerity with humor, and becomes completely serious when the Vault or Floon comes up. Deep down, he shares his father's charm and intelligence but lives in fear that he also shares the belligerence, ill temper, and bad judgment.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/02-renaer-neverember.md:26: **Relationships.** Renaer is the estranged son of Dagult Neverember (former Open Lord, current Lord of Neverwinter), the devoted friend of Floon Blagmaar, the closest living friend of Meloon Wardragon (whose changed behavior has him alarmed), a man who rescued Vajra Safahr from Khondar Naomal's agents and left the Blackstaff permanently in his debt, a Harper-adjacent ally of Mirt and Remi Haventree, and the leader of the Black Tears (his personal circle of trusted companions).
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/02-renaer-neverember.md:30: Renaer Neverember is the estranged son of Dagult Neverember — sharp, well-read, and knowledgeable about every faction operating in the city. He owns considerable property in Waterdeep and fights alongside those he trusts.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/04-floon-blagmaar.md:26: **Relationships.** Floon is the devoted friend of Renaer Neverember (was kidnapped because he was mistaken for Renaer), an acquaintance of Volo (who hires the party to find him), and a man whose gambling debts may lead him to ask the party for 500 gp later in the campaign.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/04-floon-blagmaar.md:30: Floon Blagmaar is a handsome, aimless man in his early thirties who gets by on charm, moves through Waterdeep's taverns on Renaer Neverember's social current, and attracts trouble he does not seek.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/05-meloon-wardragon.md:26: **Relationships.** Meloon is an active member of Force Grey (reporting to Vajra Safahr, whose life he saved more than a decade ago alongside Renaer), the closest friend of Renaer Neverember (whose growing unease, without knowing why, becomes alarm once the occupation takes hold), a Yawning Portal regular whose surface thoughts Bonnie reads as comprehensibly normal until the occupation, and, after the Field of Triumph, a host for Nihiloor's intellect devourer, which reports to the Guild through a courier because telepathy reaches only 60 feet.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/06-dalakhar.md:7: > - **Affiliation:** Agent of Dagult Neverember
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/06-dalakhar.md:14: **Emotions.** Dalakhar most often felt cautious determination, operative's pride, growing fear of pursuit, unshakable loyalty to Dagult Neverember, and exhaustion from days living in the sewers.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/06-dalakhar.md:16: **Motivations.** Dalakhar wanted to deliver the Stone of Golorr to the party (planning to reclaim it later for Neverember), avoid the Zhentarim and Xanathar agents closing in, and serve the man he believed was Waterdeep's rightful Open Lord.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/06-dalakhar.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Dalakhar was a small gnome with dry waste on his boots and sewer-stink on his cloak. To those he trusted, he was Neverember's most reliable agent, the one who succeeded where many others had failed. Deep down, he was afraid that the Zhentarim and Xanathar's Guild were close to catching him, and he was right.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/06-dalakhar.md:26: **Relationships.** Dalakhar was an agent of Dagult Neverember, known to Renaer ("My father didn't trust many people, but he trusted that gnome"), and the last person to hold the Stone of Golorr before it entered the Grand Game.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/08-laraelra-elra-harsard.md:16: **Motivations.** Elra wants to keep Renaer's circle safe, pursue her own arcane studies, and ensure that the Neverember name does not drag her friends into something they cannot handle.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/08-laraelra-elra-harsard.md:30: Laraelra Harsard is a half-elf mage and a member of Renaer Neverember's trusted circle, the Black Tears, known for checking exits and keeping wards on her companions.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/09-osco-salibuck.md:30: Osco Salibuck is a lightfoot halfling rogue who drinks with Renaer Neverember's crowd, keeps his skills understated, and can get in anywhere.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/10-lord-torlyn-wands.md:30: Lord Torlyn Wands is a wizard from Waterdeep's notable Wands family who prefers Trollskull Alley to galas and keeps company with Renaer Neverember's Black Tears.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/11-eiruk-weskur.md:30: Eiruk Weskur is a priest of Oghma and a member of Renaer Neverember's Black Tears, steady and patient, the one the group looks to when their decisions carry weight.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/12-harug-shieldsunder.md:30: Harug Shieldsunder is a shield dwarf fighter and the frontline muscle of Renaer Neverember's Black Tears, a man who says little and holds the line.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/13-parlek-lateriff.md:30: Parlek Lateriff is a human rogue and member of Renaer Neverember's Black Tears, easy to overlook, reliable under pressure, and possessed of skills his friends do not ask about.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/19-aurinax.md:16: **Motivations.** Aurinax wants to keep his oath to Dagult Neverember, retain the dragonstaff of Ahghairon as his agreed payment, and see the gold legitimately transferred before he considers his duty discharged.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/19-aurinax.md:26: **Relationships.** Aurinax is the guardian of the Vault of Dragons (placed by Dagult Neverember), the wielder of the dragonstaff of Ahghairon, an old acquaintance of Hlam, and the final gatekeeper for the 500,000 gp resolution in **Vault of Dragons**.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/20-kalain.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Kalain is a half-elf painter living alone in a dilapidated harbor tower, once celebrated in Waterdavian circles. To those who earn a DC 14 Persuasion check on arrival, she is a woman who helped Dagult Neverember with something she no longer quite understands, and has been waiting for his assassins ever since — losing conversational threads mid-sentence, rubbing her cheek with increasing agitation under pressure, and calling animated figures from her older peaceful canvases (sleeping cats, bowls of fruit, quiet landscapes) to fetch things or run small errands. Deep down, the newer paintings are all monsters wearing the same face, and creatures from them can be loosed against intruders.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/independents-allies/20-kalain.md:26: **Relationships.** Kalain was a former intimate of Dagult Neverember whose abandonment ended both her career and her peace of mind; her tower contains correspondence bearing directly on the Vault of Dragons; her locked document case holds intelligence — the first concrete Cassalanter-to-vault link, a vault-opening clue, and an unsigned letter confirming Neverember's deliberate separation of Stone and Eyes — that is among the most consequential in the campaign, available to any party with enough patience to earn it.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/lords-alliance/01-jalester-silvermane.md:29: **Relationships.** Jalester is a field operative for Laeral Silverhand, a former member of the Steel Shadows mercenary company (Cormyr), the surviving partner of Faerrel Dunblade, and the Lords' Alliance faction contact who delivers missions in person because he cannot bear to delegate.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/order-of-the-gauntlet/01-savra-belabranta.md:26: **Relationships.** Savra is the Order of the Gauntlet faction contact, the head of the Waterdeep chapter (a clerk's office at the Halls of Justice and a rotation of about a dozen Knights and Priests who answer to her), and a member of the Belabranta noble family (griffon breeders for the Griffon Cavalry). Her cult past makes her attuned to the signs of infernal operations. She has never held the rank of Righteous Hand.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/04-nihiloor.md:16: **Motivations.** Nihiloor wants to breed and deploy intellect devourers throughout Waterdeep, pursue its own fell schemes independent of Xanathar's goals, and observe the Grand Game from within the shadows where no one looks.
campaign/setting/notable-figures/xanathars-guild/09-grinda-garloth.md:22: **Persona.** To the world, Grinda is the wizard who lives over a closed dock and disappears into Deepwater Harbor for days at a time, and whose Apparatus of Kwalish is available for rental at rates she considers entirely reasonable. To those who deal with her, she sells services, equipment, and information — about anything except her current client list, which is proprietary. Deep down, she does not care about the Grand Game; she cares about the dragon turtle wreck at 47 fathoms and what might still be salvageable.
campaign/setting/organizations/01-harpers.md:6: > - **Campaign role:** Player faction — intelligence-gathering operatives working against tyranny and the consolidation of the Black Network's ruthless half.
campaign/setting/organizations/01-harpers.md:23: **Secondary Contact:** Remallia "Remi" Haventree — a precise, guarded elf widow who manages Harper intelligence operations in the North Ward. Harder to read than Mirt but ultimately more reliable. Her faction identity is kept hidden from the PCs until she chooses to reveal it; they will not know her as a Harper unless she tells them.

campaign/setting/organizations/01-harpers.md:38: The Harpers will happily allow the PCs to keep Neverember's gold. What they want is the Stone of Golorr. If the PCs hand it over, Mirt considers the debt paid in full and offers a substantial favor in return. If the PCs refuse, the Harpers become noticeably less forthcoming — but will not act against them.

campaign/setting/organizations/01-harpers.md:46: The Harpers are a loose confederation of spies, bards, and scholars working in the shadows to prevent any single power from dominating Faerûn. In Waterdeep, they track the Grand Game from a position of information — their greatest resource and their greatest vulnerability.
campaign/setting/organizations/02-lords-alliance.md:5: > - **Campaign role:** Player faction. Political operatives committed to recovering the 500,000 stolen dragons for Waterdeep's treasury.
campaign/setting/organizations/02-lords-alliance.md:16: The Alliance's position on the Grand Game is plain: the 500,000 stolen dragons belong to the city, and it wants them recovered and returned. campaign/setting/organizations/02-lords-alliance.md:32: ## Grand Game Agenda
campaign/setting/organizations/02-lords-alliance.md:34: The Alliance wants the gold home. Members who bring the Grand Game to Jalester's attention draw Laeral's interest, and once she is fully informed she makes the recovery a political priority and gives the party real resources. The Alliance will not let the party keep the gold. In exchange for returning it, it offers renown and political standing that no other patron in the city can match. A character who pockets the gold and claims ignorance will find that Laeral is a thorough investigator.

campaign/setting/organizations/03-emerald-enclave.md:5: > - **Campaign role:** Player faction. Druids and rangers who watch Waterdeep's natural balance and take no side in the vault gold.
campaign/setting/organizations/03-emerald-enclave.md:18: The Enclave has no stake in Neverember's gold and makes no demands about the Vault or its contents. Jeryth Phaulkon holds that the question belongs to the Lords, and the Enclave follows her. Its help is personal and magical rather than institutional, and it is given freely to those who prove loyal. That makes it an unusually clean patron once every other faction is pulling in a different direction.
campaign/setting/organizations/03-emerald-enclave.md:20: Its Waterdeep presence centers on Phaulkonmere, an old estate and garden in the Southern Ward. The gate stands open to anyone the Enclave has invited, and the garden is older than the street around it. No faction has ever openly violated the sanctuary.

campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:6: > - **Campaign role:** Player faction and villain faction — drow mercenary company operating the Sea Maidens Faire carnival; holds Eye #3 and runs a city-wide intelligence network.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:13: > **Street Contact:** Krebbyg Masq'il'yr — usually at the Seven Masks Theater in the Dock Ward, where he operates as "Kreb Sorrush," the theater's booking manager. A drow gunslinger with a relaxed professional manner and a slight Luskan accent he makes no effort to conceal. He handles casting inquiries, performance schedules, and faction intelligence requests with equal efficiency, switching between them mid-sentence. He does not explain who he works for until the party has already worked for that person twice.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:21: Bregan D'aerthe is a mercenary company of drow exiles and surface operatives, led by Jarlaxle Baenre, who has been outmaneuvering Underdark politics for generations. In Waterdeep, the operation runs out of the Sea Maidens Faire — a traveling carnival that is exactly what it appears to be, and also something else entirely. Jarlaxle does not brawl in the streets. He acquires information, sells it selectively, positions operatives in every major faction, and acts decisively only when the odds are already arranged in his favor.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:23: Joining Bregan D'aerthe means working for Jarlaxle, who genuinely values capable people and will treat his agents well — until their interests diverge from his, at which point he will maneuver them into serving his ends anyway, with a smile. Characters who join this faction are playing a game where the other player is better at it than they are. Pay attention.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:48: ## Grand Game Agenda
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:54: - **Bregan D'aerthe** (Factions guide): Grand Game stance, quest hooks, first meeting, renown and ranks, missions, response teams, outposts, escalation, revelation list.
campaign/setting/organizations/07-bregan-daerthe.md:58: Bregan D'aerthe is a mercenary company of drow exiles and surface operatives, led by Jarlaxle Baenre, running the Sea Maidens Faire carnival as cover for a city-wide intelligence network. He has positioned his people inside every major faction in Waterdeep. Joining him means working for the most capable operator in the city — one who will treat his agents well until their interests diverge from his.
campaign/setting/organizations/08-xanathars-guild.md:6: > - **Campaign role:** Villain faction; the Grand Game's most territorially entrenched competitor, holding Eye #1.
campaign/setting/organizations/08-xanathars-guild.md:17: Its agenda in the Grand Game is territorial. Xanathar held the Stone of Golorr until Dalakhar stole it; it still holds one of the Stone's Eyes. The gold itself holds little appeal; the leverage it represents is everything. Xanathar wants the Stone recovered and the vault kept hidden — a source of leverage it can threaten but never has to use.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:6: > - **Campaign role:** Villain faction; pursues the Stone of Golorr for the Weave knowledge encoded in its aboleth memory.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:13: **A note on the Zhentarim:** Two cells operate in Waterdeep. Davil Starsong's **Doom Raiders** are the player-joinable faction covered on **The Zhentarim (Doom Raiders)** organization page. Manshoon's splinter is a completely separate organization that peeled off from the Doom Raiders' local network when he returned to Waterdeep. The two groups dislike each other. When players ally with the Zhentarim through Davil, they're working with the Doom Raiders, not Manshoon. The villains in this section answer only to Manshoon.
campaign/setting/organizations/09-manshoons-zhentarim.md:19: He keeps a simulacrum in Kolat Towers while Manshoon watches from safer ground. His approach reflects his temperament: precision, patience, no unnecessary violence. The Zhentarim response teams are the weakest in raw muscle of any faction. This is deliberate. Drawing too much attention is the one mistake Manshoon won't make twice.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:8: > - **Campaign role:** Villain faction; racing to acquire the vault's gold before the twins' ninth birthday, Flamerule 11, when their soul pact with Asmodeus comes due; Founders' Day (Flamerule 1) is the feast date and the party's true deadline.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:33: The Cassalanters found a loophole. A ritual can buy out the children's souls for "one shy of a million gold coins" and ninety-nine souls. They hold about half the gold. They need Neverember's vault to cover the remainder.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:37: How the family responds to a party that helps or hinders them is in the **Cassalanters** faction operations page.
campaign/setting/organizations/10-cassalanters.md:116: > - **Conflicts:** Gold held. The page keeps the campaign figure of about 500,000 gp, which the Vault's 500,000 gp covers to reach 999,999 gp.
campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:3: > **Entry and Activation:** Two separate mechanics govern Bregan D'aerthe in this campaign. **Membership** runs during **Trollskull Alley**, like any other faction: drow lieutenants shadow the party for several days, then J.B. Nevercott (Jarlaxle in disguise, posing as a haberdasher) visits Trollskull Manor and offers enrollment to any interested PC. The only gate is party action — if the party reported the watchers to the Watch, the **BD Contact Severed** flag closes the faction for the campaign. The **Ryvarra Identified** flag (from **The Neighbors** event in **Trollskull Alley**) changes how the visit opens, not whether it happens. **Villain activation** is a separate track: Jarlaxle has known of the Stone, the Eyes and the vault since he arrived in Waterdeep, and he enters the Grand Game as a named player at the table when the **Jarlaxle Informed** flag is set during **Fireball!** and read in **Gralhund Villa**. The flag records only that the party spoke directly with him. It does not mean he learned anything, and it gates his Bregan D'aerthe team at **Gralhund Villa** and his Phase 1 Zardoz Zord activity. His identity stays hidden behind J.B. Nevercott and Zardoz Zord until **Sea Maidens Faire**. The personality phases below key off the Jarlaxle Informed flag, not BD membership. See: **GM Guide: Session Zero**; **GM Guide: Player Factions Overview**; **Bregan D'aerthe** organization page.
campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:50: | Renaer Neverember | Political connection | Worth cultivating; Renaer's relationship with Laeral is an indirect path to the Open Lord's attention; Renaer doesn't know who he met |
campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:52: | The PCs | Most interesting variable | Has been watching since **Finding Floon** (Nar'l's reports reached him, and his agents were in the Xanathar sewer hideout); knows their names, faction ties, and general competence by **Sea Maidens Faire** |
campaign/setting/villains/jarlaxle.md:60: **Key design note:** Jarlaxle is the only villain whose faction (Bregan D'aerthe) the PCs can join. This must be written directly into the campaign structure, not treated as an edge case. A party that joins Bregan D'aerthe inverts **Sea Maidens Faire** — the Faire becomes their base, and Jarlaxle sends them after another faction's Eye.

campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:7: Arc A is the campaign's inciting incident. It introduces Waterdeep as an active faction battlefield, teaches the Three Clue Rule investigation structure that recurs throughout the campaign, and delivers the party's home base as the arc's reward. The arc exists to establish the scope of the Grand Game without overwhelming first-level characters: three villain factions are visible in Scene 1, but the party's only job is to find one man.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:13: A street-level rescue mission drops the characters into the middle of a three-faction crossfire. Two people were kidnapped from a Dock Ward alley. One is in a warehouse, held by the wrong gang. One is in the sewers, held by the right one. The investigation teaches players exactly how the Three Clue Rule works — multiple independent paths to every answer — and ends with the party holding a deed to a derelict tavern that makes them homeowners, neighbors, and targets.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:27: Manshoon's Zhentarim hold the warehouse. Five of Urstul Floxin's thugs grabbed Renaer Neverember and Floon outside the Old Xoblob Shop two nights ago, brought them here, and questioned Renaer about his father's embezzled hoard. They took his mourning locket. Renaer believed it held a keepsake from his mother; it hid an Eye of the Stone of Golorr in a sealed compartment. The Eye was delivered to Kolat Towers before the Xanathar Guild raid killed most of the cell. Four kenku now search the wreckage. Renaer hid when the raid hit and is still hiding in a back room. Floon was mistaken for Renaer and dragged through a trapdoor into the sewer. Renaer, once found, tells the party what he saw: Xanathar agents, circle-eye tattoos, Floon unconscious over one man's shoulder, the trapdoor in the cellar floor. Three more clues in the warehouse confirm the route. They follow.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:35: **Purpose:** Establish the Yawning Portal as a stage for faction conflict, introduce Durnan as formidable authority, deliver Volo's quest, and plant the BD observer seed. Three villain factions are visible before the party accepts any job.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:45: Yagra's line is the party's earliest signal that the Dock Ward killing was personal — and that the faction at that bar is not the same faction that Durnan will eventually introduce in the form of Davil Starsong. Two Zhentarim cells operate under the same symbol; Yagra's Doom Raiders and Urstul Floxin's Splinter are enemies in everything but name. This distinction is introduced through lived conflict, before any NPC explains it.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:75: *Volo's Quest.* Volo (see Appendix A) pushes through the settling crowd, steered to the party's table by Threestrings. He has already decided to hire the party based on Threestrings's recommendation; the troll fight confirmed his judgment. He offers 10 gp per character upfront, 100 gp per character when Floon is returned alive. DC 10 Wisdom (Insight): Volo is honest but stretching the truth about the 100 gp (he is awaiting royalties from *Volo's Guide to Monsters*; the book he is currently writing — *Volo's Guide to Spirits and Specters* — is not going well). He describes Floon: wavy red-blond hair, early thirties, more charming than sensible, wearing princely garb the night he disappeared. He last saw Floon at the Skewered Dragon last night. He does not know that Renaer Neverember met Floon after Volo left.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:93: - **Blood in the Streets.** En route from the Yawning Portal into the Dock Ward — before the party reaches the Xoblob Shop or the Skewered Dragon — they cross a City Watch crime scene. A dozen guards have arrested three surviving Zhentarim thugs from a street brawl: black leather armor, hands on heads, flying snake tattoos visible on necks and forearms. The Watch waves the party off, but the tattoos are in plain view. These are **Manshoon's Splinter Zhentarim**, not the Doom Raiders; they report to Kolat Towers, not to Davil Starsong. A DC 13 Intelligence (History) check with any criminal, faction, or guild background identifies the tattoo as Zhentarim; distinguishing which cell is not possible from a tattoo alone.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:95: - **Skewered Dragon.** A dim Dock Ward dive owned and operated by **Manshoon's Splinter Zhentarim** as an informal ward headquarters. Regulars here remember Volo and Floon from last night. After Volo left, Floon stayed and met Renaer Neverember. They drank, played Three-Dragon Ante, and left together around midnight. Five men followed them out. "Those men haven't come back," says one regular. "Flying snake tattoos. They keep a warehouse on Candle Lane." This clue delivers Revelation #1. The regulars will also name Candle Lane if pressed, giving the party both revelations in a single stop; this is not a formal third path to Revelation #2 per the Three Clue Rule, but it is the fastest route for parties who follow Volo's lead. The current regulars are Splinter men on their own ground; the party is in unfriendly territory if they push too hard without social skill.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:102: — *Two dockworkers eating lunch in a doorway on Dock Street* (both human male, nervous): they have seen the snake-tattoo men; DC 13 Persuasion (DC 11 if the party shows any faction credential or Watch badge) confirms: a warehouse at the end of Candle Lane. "Don't say we told you."
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:104: - **Blood in the Streets prisoners.** The three arrested Splinter thugs are held at the crime scene while wagons are arranged to transport them. **The party has no faction contacts at Arc A level.** The Watch guards will not release the prisoners or allow unsupervised access. Options: (a) A character who names Floon Blagmaar or Renaer Neverember as the subject of a kidnapping can request a brief supervised conversation — the Watch sergeant (DC 14 Persuasion) is skeptical but allows it with a cooperative prisoner; (b) a bribe of 5 gp to a specific guard (DC 12 Persuasion) buys a five-minute supervised exchange; (c) a character with a City Watch background can invoke prior service without a check, limited to two questions per prisoner. Any cooperative prisoner names Candle Lane. Any prisoner who refuses requires DC 15 Intimidation — possible but difficult with four Watch guards present and watching.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:109: *Faction Visibility.* Xanathar Guild: yellow chalk territory marks (passive). Manshoon's Splinter Zhentarim: under arrest at the crime scene, or at the Skewered Dragon, or approaching to intimidate. Doom Raiders: not present in the Dock Ward; the bar brawl at the Yawning Portal established the faction fracture.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:117: **Purpose:** Deliver the arc's first major location — a twelve-area keyed warehouse occupied by Xanathar's kenku — teach the stealth-or-force choice that recurs in every heist arc, introduce Renaer Neverember as an ally, and establish three clues pointing to the sewer hideout. The warehouse is the arc's emotional pivot: the party finds the wrong person, learns what actually happened, and gets new leads pointing down.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:121: *Location Overview.* The warehouse sits at the back of a yard behind a high fence; the gate is unlocked. Three entry points: front door, loading bay, painted-over window — all locked (DC 12 Dexterity with thieves' tools, DC 10 Strength [Athletics] to force). A black winged snake is painted above the front door handle; DC 10 Intelligence identifies it as the Zhentarim symbol. Four kenku occupy the building — left by Xanathar to kill any Zhentarim who return. Renaer Neverember is in area Z2 (storage closet), having slipped his bonds and hidden before the kenku began their search. The building is Manshoon's Zhentarim property. The Doom Raiders have no presence here.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:126: - **Z2 — Storage Closet.** Renaer Neverember is here: unarmed, bruised, cooperative, and waiting for the kenku to be dealt with before he attempts escape. The room smells of rancid pickled herring.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:175: *Mad Mage Seeds.* The Q11 stone portal is the deepest Undermountain seed in Arc A. When Nihiloor steps through and the black doorway closes behind it, the party has witnessed a mind flayer access a route they cannot yet follow. The portal terminus in X22 of Xanathar's lair connects the sewer hideout to the dungeon's faction economy — a thread that becomes load-bearing in Arc F. Parties that examine the inert pillar in Q11 after Nihiloor has fled collect one piece of the puzzle; the rest arrives in Arc F when they reach the other side.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:193: | 1 → 2 | A | The party clears the Zhentarim warehouse and finds Renaer Neverember alive | 100 |
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:202: *Transition to Arc B.* The party holds a deed to a derelict tavern. They are residents of Trollskull Alley. The city faction recruitment, Manor renovation, and the first rumors of something called "Neverember's Enigma" begin in Arc B.
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:212: ***The Two Zhentarims in Arc A.*** The original adventure presents the Zhentarim as a single faction. This remix uses Arc A to establish the faction fracture that defines the Manshoon subplot. Yagra Stonefist — a Doom Raiders operative, Davil Starsong's people — is at the Yawning Portal bar, openly wearing the Zhentarim pendant, fighting Xanathar Guild thugs. The Candle Lane warehouse belongs to Manshoon's cell, Urstul Floxin's people, operating separately from Davil. Players who later join the Doom Raiders in Arc B and then raid Kolat Towers in Arc I will have seen both Zhentarim factions from Scene 1 — one as a potential ally, one as an adversary, both operating under the same symbol. The distinction should be felt before it is explained.
