campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:260: - **Arc B** — Trollskull Manor becomes the party's home base; Renaer and Floon recur as Alley NPCs; BD observer resurfaces; faction recruitment begins; Dock Ward fallout from Arc A escalates
campaign/structure/arc-a-finding-floon.md:266: - **Appendix A** — NPC profiles: Volo Geddarm, Durnan, Renaer Neverember, Floon Blagmaar, Yagra Stonefist, Ryvarra (BD Section).
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:35: **Purpose:** Convert Volo's deed from reward to project. Establish the manor's physical state and Lif the poltergeist as its resident obstacle. Introduce the renovation economics that drive faction recruitment in Scene 4.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:51: *Renovation Economics.* The numbers: 1,000 gp to renovate over 12 days, 250 gp for guild licenses and contracts paid upfront. Total: 1,250 gp. A 2nd-level party fresh from Arc A is unlikely to have this. Financing options available immediately: Renaer offers a no-interest loan up to 200 gp when told about the cost, embarrassed by the party's thanks and with no expectation of repayment; faction assistance is available once contact is established (see Scene 4 and **Appendix F, Faction Renovation Assistance**). The gap between what the party has and what the renovation costs is the engine driving faction recruitment.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:67: *The Cellarers' and Plumbers' Guild (New Content).* The inspector finds the sewer access grate at the alley's east end (T7) and connects it to the manor's cellar through old city infrastructure — a direct link to Waterdeep's sewer network that predates the current building by at least two centuries. He flags this for "structural heritage documentation," which is not a blocking condition but must be noted. **This passage becomes relevant when faction response teams use the sewer approach, and when the party re-enters the sewer network in Arc C.**
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:113: **Purpose:** Deliver faction recruitment as a managed menu across the renovation period. Invitations are per-character based on eligibility — not per-party. Arc B establishes when and how each faction arrives; the full First Meeting content lives in Appendix C.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:117: *Invitations Arrive.* Over the course of the renovation period, each faction sends invitations to eligible party members through characteristic delivery methods. **Invitations are per-character, not per-party** — the same faction may recruit multiple party members simultaneously, and different characters may receive different factions' invitations in the same week.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:131: *Faction Renovation Assistance.* Once contact is established with a faction, assistance with the 1,250 gp renovation cost becomes available. Cross-ref **Appendix F, Faction Renovation Assistance** for full terms: Mirt's personal loan (500 gp, repayable as a favor), Jalester's civic grant (200 gp), the Enclave's *Fabricate* casting (−250 gp off renovation cost), Savra's guild connections (faster scheduling, favorable rates), Istrid Horn's Black Network loan (400 gp, party-set repayment), Jarlaxle's full bankrolling (up to 1,250 gp, repayable in operational access), Vajra's Tiny Hut and vault scrolls. Each offer has strings; the party's financing choices are their first real faction commitments.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:133: *Level 2 Missions Become Available.* Once enrolled, characters receive their first faction assignments. Quick reference below; full write-ups in **Appendix D**:
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:149: *Filthy Meg.* The Mistshore information broker is introduced during Arc B through faction referral: Remallia Haventree (Harpers) or Yagra (Doom Raiders) names Meg as a reliable Dock Ward source. The party can reach her without a referral through Dock Ward exploration, but the referral removes her initial suspicion (drops the opening Charisma (Persuasion) DC from 16 to 12). Cross-ref **Appendix A, Section 3** (Filthy Meg profile).
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:151: **Tone:** Political, flattering, faintly competitive. The factions want specific people. The invitations reveal how each faction thinks and what it values.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:173: A party of four that wins the tournament takes home roughly 500 gp between Zord's fee and the prize purses — enough to close most of the renovation gap if faction financing has covered the rest. Losing the final still pays 390 gp. The arena is one of the few legitimate sources of this much money at the party's level, and Zord knows it when he sends the invitation.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:193: *Xanathar Guild Recruitment.* After the final bout, a Guild scout makes contact — professional and direct, offering paid work in the Dock Ward and handing over a token for the Old Xoblob Shop. Not a viable faction path (villain faction), but establishes the Guild's reach and creates pressure the party has to manage: accept the token, discard it, or use it as intelligence. If the party beat Meloon's team, Xanathar himself has been notified. That notification becomes relevant in Arc F.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:207: *Staff Hiring.* In the days before the opening, candidates arrive for each open position. **The interview process is a low-stakes social encounter with real consequences** — candidates have agendas, faction affiliations, and secrets that will matter later. Cross-ref **Appendix F, Staff Candidates** for full interview profiles (five bartenders, five servers, five cooks, five bouncers, five house bards). Interview framing:
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:216: - Faction contacts the party has enrolled with, whose presence signals to the room that this tavern operates under faction awareness.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:236: *Festival Atmosphere.* Two processions converge on the Market: one moving south from the Castle Ward, one east from the Sea Ward. The crowd fills the intervening streets; vendors operate from temporary stalls; City Watch manages pedestrian flow with professional exasperation. The party is participating in Waterdeep civic life now, not observing it — they have neighbors in this crowd, faction contacts, and a tavern that people here have visited. Cross-ref **Ch. 3: Running the Campaign** (Festival Calendar) for placement in the campaign timeline. The Twin Parades fall on Ches 21 — the opening day of Fleetswake (Ches 21–30), the maritime festival that fills Waterdeep Harbor with tall ships. The Sea Maidens Faire fleet arrived in harbor this morning; the party may notice the carnival flotilla from the parade route's eastern end. Full event structure: **`sources/24. Addendum The Twin Parades.pdf`**.

campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:294: 7. **Filthy Meg via faction referral.** Harpers (Remallia Haventree) or Doom Raiders (Yagra) name her as a Dock Ward source. Direct Dock Ward exploration works but opens at DC 16.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:298: 9. **Sandbox scene ordering.** Scenes 1–3 are sequential (the party must arrive, deal with guilds, and meet neighbors before factions can recruit them). Scene 4 overlaps the renovation period (faction invitations arrive while the renovation runs; so does the Field of Triumph invitation). Scene 5 (Field of Triumph) occurs during the renovation period, before opening. Scene 6 (Grand Opening) is the renovation's culmination. Scene 7 (Twin Parades) is a scheduled city event following the opening.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:304: - `sources/adventure-wdh.json`, Chapter 2 — Trollskull Alley keyed areas (T1–T7), neighbor descriptions and stat blocks, faction recruitment overview, guild permit requirements, Emmek Frewn sabotage sequence, Bregan D'aerthe surveillance detail
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:317: - **Appendix D: Running the Factions** — Level 2 faction missions (all seven factions); Force Grey Mission 1 (Hlam) and Mission 3 (Meloon — first encountered as himself in Scene 5) specifically
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:327: ***Seven Scenes for an Episodic Arc.*** Arc A has four scenes because Arc A is a linear investigation — it flows from clue to clue, from the surface to the sewers, and ends when Floon is found. Arc B is structurally different: the party has a home base, multiple concurrent obligations, and no investigation to follow. The seven-scene structure imposes narrative progression on open-ended content without prescribing player choices. Scenes 1–3 run sequentially because the party cannot receive faction invitations before they have somewhere to receive them. Scene 4 overlaps the renovation period with faction recruitment running parallel to neighbor introductions. Scene 5 (Field of Triumph) slots into the renovation period — the party is recruited, fighting in the city's arena, and hiring staff all in the same stretch of time. Scene 6 (Grand Opening) is the renovation's endpoint and the arc's emotional culmination. Scene 7 (Twin Parades) is the city's calendar, not the party's — it falls when it falls. The DM runs Arc B as weeks of parallel activity, with these scenes as waypoints rather than a script.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:339: ***Arc B Contains Less Original Mechanical Content Than Any Other Arc.*** This is intentional. The tavern renovation system lives in Appendix F. The staff candidates live in Appendix F. The faction recruitment scenes live in Appendix C. The faction missions live in Appendix D. Arc B's job is to establish *when* each appendix system becomes relevant and *what the party is doing* when those systems activate — not to duplicate the content. Arc B is the conductor's score; the appendices are the instruments. A DM who reads Arc B and then Appendix F will have a complete picture of the renovation period. A DM who reads only Arc B will have enough to run the arc. The appendix depth is there when they want it.
campaign/structure/arc-b-trollskull-alley.md:367: ***The Most Arc-B-Specific Faction Content.*** Force Grey Missions 1 and 2 are the only Level 2 faction missions designed primarily to feed information threads rather than build renown in isolation. Hlam's warning (Mission 1) gives the first specific reference to Manshoon by implication ("evil's twin hides its face"). Zelifarn's barnacle-covered shipwreck lair at the harbor bottom (Mission 2) contains physical evidence of unusual deep-harbor activity pointing toward Skullport. Force Grey is the only faction where the Level 2–3 missions function as intelligence operations. Parties who join Force Grey in Arc B arrive at Arc F with more operational context than any other faction affiliation provides — and that context was earned through missions that felt like their own stories, not like exposition delivery.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:13: Dalakhar, a rock gnome spy working for exiled Lord Dagult Neverember, had been carrying the *Stone of Golorr* toward Trollskull Manor when four separate factions converged on the same alley at the same moment. Urstul Floxin's Zhentarim moved to intercept him from the north. The Gralhund nimblewright — a Lantanese construct sold by Jarlaxle Baenre's Sea Maidens Faire, armed with a *necklace of fireballs* by Lady Yalah Gralhund — watched from a rooftop, under orders to secure the Stone before any other faction could claim it. When Floxin appeared about to take Dalakhar alive, the nimblewright hurled a bead into the crowd. The fireball killed Dalakhar, two Zhentarim sellswords, eight bystanders, and the DM's chosen Arc B victim. Floxin survived — burned and furious — the Stone already gone. The nimblewright dropped from the roof, rifled Dalakhar's body, pocketed the Stone, and fled east across the rooftops toward Gralhund Villa. Floxin followed it back to the estate. Lady Gralhund disarmed him at swordpoint, confined him in a ground-floor room, and refused to yield the Stone. He killed his guards, escaped without it, and is now assembling a strike team for a forced return. That return is Arc D. The investigation that begins in Arc C must reach Gralhund Villa before the night of Ches 24th, when Floxin's team arrives and the situation becomes unrecoverable.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:55: *Speak with Dead.* Available after the Watch takes custody of the bodies. Three access methods: faction access at Renown 1+ in the Harpers, Lords' Alliance, Order of the Gauntlet, or Force Grey arranges a cleric through the appropriate temple; a temple donation of at least 25 gp per casting buys one casting with questions provided in advance; a character who knows *Speak with Dead* can attempt their own access with a DC 13 Persuasion check with Barnibus or Cromley. Dalakhar can answer up to five questions: he stole the *Stone of Golorr* from the lair of a beholder called Xanathar, deep in the dungeons below the city; he worked for the rightful Open Lord — meaning Neverember, not Silverhand; the Stone is the key to a hidden hoard of gold dragons sealed somewhere in the city; he heard of adventurers who rescued Neverember's son and decided his hands were safer than his own; Renaer Neverember vouched for them. The two dead Zhentarim (Bashekk Ortallis and Wern Malkrave) can each answer up to five questions: they worked for Urstul Floxin; they were assigned to intercept a gnome named Dalakhar; whatever Dalakhar carried would make them "as rich as kings"; they do not know where Floxin billeted the cell.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:91: - **Speak with Dead — Dalakhar's Body.** The bodies remain available for five days after the Watch takes custody. Dalakhar can answer up to five questions about the Stone directly: that he stole it from Xanathar's lair, that it is the key to a hidden vault of gold dragons somewhere beneath the city, and that Renaer Neverember vouched for the party. See Scene 1 for access methods.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:96: *Player Faction Contact Reactions.* As the fireball investigation proceeds, each player faction contact responds in their own register. These exchanges can be delivered as flying snake messages, chance encounters, or requested meetings depending on the party's established communication patterns.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:98: - **Harpers (Mirt, Renown 1+):** During the investigation — any point before the party reaches the Sea Maidens Faire — Mirt takes a Harper member aside for a private word. The Cassalanters funded the Howling Hatred cult three years ago; he quietly extracted their agents from Watch custody as a political courtesy. He is not certain whether their current interest in the Grand Game connects to any of this. He is sharing it because he is worried, which is different from being certain.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:106: *Faction Response Teams Deploy.* All four villain factions move on the crime scene within hours. Xanathar Guild gazers post to watch the alley (DC 16 Perception to spot them); Manshoon's Zhent operatives run a parallel investigation through their own network; Bregan D'Aerthe observers activate conditionally, their level of engagement scaling with whether the party later reaches the Sea Maidens Faire; the Cassalanters send flowers and a warm handwritten condolence note — Ammalia's handwriting. Cross-ref Appendix E (faction response team rosters and behavior by arc).
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:121: - **Faction Intelligence.** Any faction contact can identify "nimblewright" from the witness descriptions and direct the party to the Gond temple. Harpers are fastest — Remallia Haventree knows the high priest personally.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:122: - **Watch Cooperation.** Barnibus's Watchful Order contacts can identify the construct type from the Arcana trace and witness descriptions within 48 hours. Slower than the other two paths but available to parties without established faction contacts.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:174: - **Path 3: The Crystal Ball (Hardest, Maximum Intelligence).** The *Scarlet Marpenoth* submersible — whose model stands in the House of Inspired Hands main hall — is docked beneath the *Eyecatcher*, connected by a keel collar. Discovering it requires DC 18 Perception from the docks at night, or a faction tip. Accessing it and operating the crystal ball provides recorded footage of the murder. An aspirational target for a 3rd-level party — establishes the *Marpenoth* as a high-value intelligence asset for Arc H.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:218: *Victoro's Pitch.* After the first course, Victoro raises the fireball. He expresses condolences. He mentions that the family had some acquaintance with Lord Neverember's later projects and that the object Dalakhar was carrying has "historical and financial significance beyond what most people appreciate." He does not name the Stone of Golorr. He offers to share what the Cassalanters have learned — contacts, research, documents — in exchange for a first conversation if the party recovers the object. His language is courteous, precise, and carries no threat. He does not ask for the Stone. He asks to be in the room.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:220: *What the Cassalanters Know.* Victoro knows: Dalakhar worked for Neverember, the Stone is the key to a concealed vault, all three Eyes were separated from the Stone years ago, and the vault's physical entrance is in the Brandath family crypt. He does not share any of this at the dinner. His goal is assessment — he wants to know how much the party has found and whether they are worth cultivating.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:236: **Purpose:** Optional investigation branch rewarding thorough parties with Grand Game history, a Cassalanter connection through Kalain, and vault-opening clues that pay off in Arc J. Non-essential for reaching Gralhund Villa — gated behind proactive behavior, not hidden by design.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:244: - Watch authority: Renown 1+ in any faction, or a Watch badge obtained at the crime scene, grants automatic entry.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:266: *Kalain's Tower.* A half-elf painter, once celebrated in Waterdavian circles, now alone in a dilapidated tower near the harbor. Former mistress of Lord Dagult Neverember. **Roleplaying:** Believes everyone who visits is secretly Dagult's assassin. Loses conversational threads mid-sentence and resurfaces on something different. Rubs her cheek with increasing agitation as she grows distressed. Calls animated figures from her smaller, older, peaceful paintings — sleeping cats, bowls of fruit, quiet landscapes — to fetch things or run small errands. The newer paintings are all monsters, all wearing the same face; creatures from these can be animated against intruders. DC 14 Persuasion to calm her on arrival. DC 12 Persuasion to talk her down if she accuses the party of killing Dalakhar on Dagult's orders.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:269: - Dalakhar worked for Neverember. She says so openly, and adds that she is glad he is dead — meaning Neverember. It takes a moment to establish which dead man she is talking about.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:270: - Neverember was obsessed in their final months together with something he called a "Melairkyn ceremonial vault." She cannot now recall the specifics — the memory feels burned away, like a word she cannot retrieve. The *Stone of Golorr*'s influence, still active, suppresses the memory in everyone who has been near it.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:271: - **Lord Victoro Cassalanter visited a few weeks ago, asking many of the same questions Dalakhar had asked years before — the vault, Neverember's last projects, whether she kept his papers.** She will not volunteer this. Asked directly whether anyone else has come asking about Neverember, she confirms it. He was polite. She found his politeness the most unsettling thing about the visit. This is the first concrete Cassalanter-to-vault link.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:277: - An unsigned letter dated four years ago: *"The last of the three Eyes has been secured."* This confirms all three Eyes were in Neverember's hands four years ago — and that he deliberately separated them from the Stone. Pays off in Arc J.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:297: - Dalakhar was Neverember's spy, carrying the Stone to them when the fireball killed him.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:301: - Every major faction in Waterdeep is pursuing it simultaneously.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:304: - The Stone requires three Eyes to reveal the vault's location; all three Eyes were in Neverember's hands four years ago.

campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:357: - **Arc J: Vault of Dragons** — Lord Dagult's papers (Scene 5, optional): vault-opening method, Kraddoc correspondence, unsigned Eyes letter confirming all three were in Neverember's hands four years ago. All three pay off at Arc J.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:358: - **Appendix A: NPC Roster** — Profiles required for: Barnibus Blastwind, Saeth Cromley, Fala Lefaliir, Jezrynne Hornraven, Renaer Neverember, Valetta, Nim, Captain Zord (Jarlaxle), Myl Dunpier, Kalain, Urstul Floxin, Ammalia and Victoro Cassalanter, Davil Starsong, Tashlyn Yafeera.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:368: ***Three Phases, Not One Chain.*** The original WDH investigation runs as a sequential chain: find the nimblewright → go to the House of Inspired Hands → get the detector → find it at Gralhund Villa. One missed witness or one failed check can sever any link. The three-phase redesign distributes each critical revelation across independent paths — every conclusion has at least three routes — so no single failure blocks progress. The three phases (identify the construct, find all owners, determine which one is guilty) are not a required sequence; they are three distinct moments of synthesis that teach different investigative skills. Phase 1 is scene reading. Phase 2 is city-scale elimination. Phase 3 is convergence under faction pressure. Together they build the intelligence habits the heist arcs will demand.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:386: ***The First Arc Where All Four Villain Factions Pursue the Same Object.*** Arc A establishes the Xanathar Guild and Manshoon's Zhentarim as adversaries. Arc B introduces the Cassalanters as patrons and Bregan D'Aerthe as observers. Arc C is the first arc where all four factions reach for the same object at the same time in the same city. The design makes this concrete rather than atmospheric: Xanathar gazers in the alley, Zhent parallel investigation, BD conditional observers scaling with party choices at the Faire, Cassalanter condolence note and dinner invitation. These are not flavor. They are the Grand Game becoming visible for the first time — the moment the party understands they are not the only investigators in Waterdeep, and that the city is full of people who already know more than they do.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:390: ***Investigation Quality Has Consequences.*** If the party reaches the Sea Maidens Faire, Jarlaxle enters the Grand Game as an active participant — he positions BD agents at Gralhund Villa, makes a direct approach, and begins treating the party as players worth engaging. If the party bypasses the Faire entirely and goes directly to the North Ward, he remains a background presence: aware, watching, not deployed. This is not a punishment for skipping Scene 4. It is a realistic consequence of investigative depth. The party that turns over more stones gives Jarlaxle more surface to react to.
campaign/structure/arc-c-fireball.md:400: ***Non-Essential, Consequential.*** Scene 5 cannot block the party's path to Gralhund Villa — parties that skip it still reach Arc D. But parties that pursue it gain Kalain's account, the first concrete Cassalanter-to-vault link, the vault-opening clue, and the unsigned Eyes letter confirming Neverember's deliberate separation of Stone and Eyes. These are among the most consequential intelligence finds in the entire campaign, gated behind exactly the kind of proactive habits the investigation arcs are trying to build: exhaustive canvassing, leaving contact information, following up on letters addressed to dead men. The design rewards the investigative instinct without penalizing its absence.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:35: **Purpose:** Give the party a genuine consultation round before committing to the villa. Each contact delivers a distinct tactical perspective, emotional register, and faction agenda. The party hears five versions of the same situation and must decide whose advice carries weight — without any of them being straightforwardly wrong.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:41: *Mirt (Harpers — available if renown 1+).* The quietest nudge, carrying the clearest self-interest. Mirt does not order the party in. He offers gratitude — stated as gratitude, not as a threat — if the Stone comes to him first. He notes, almost as an aside, that the Watch's involvement "tends to end these things." He is not wrong. He does not explain that the Harpers have been trying to get a lead on the Stone for weeks, or that coming to him first gives him leverage over every faction that doesn't. The party can read what they want into his warmth.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:51: *Renaer Neverember (available if Renaer Contact: Gralhund Villa Named attunement is set from Arc C ev-01).* The source who named the villa does not have a faction brief to deliver. He wants to understand what his father's vault has to do with the Gralhunds, and what the Gralhunds have to do with the Zhentarim that burned Trollskull Alley. His access to Harper contacts gave the party the address; his family history gives him a stake in what happens once they cross the gate. He does not ask the party to act. He asks to know what they find. He will be at his townhouse, in the study, when they come back.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:55: *If They Don't Go.* If the party specifically decides not to enter Gralhund Villa after being given the address, the Alexandrian's fallback applies: Floxin's team arrives on the night of Ches 24th regardless. Two days later, broadsheets name the event the "Gralhund Villa Bloodbath." Faction contacts reach out; the party hears from Mirt, Davil (before his arrest), or Renaer with secondhand accounts of what happened inside. The Stone's location becomes the next investigation — wherever the faction that left the estate with it has taken it. The party has not lost the campaign; they have changed its shape.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:77: If this condition is not met — the party bypassed the Sea Maidens Faire entirely, or entered it and set Ledger Stolen or Crystal Ball Accessed instead of Jarlaxle Informed — Artheyn Manor is empty and the fourth faction is absent from the confrontation.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:79: *Reconnaissance Mechanics.* A party that scouts carefully enters the confrontation knowing where each faction is positioned. Specific checks:
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:94: **Spending 30+ minutes watching from a fixed position:** The City Watch patrol passes Saerdoun Street irregularly, roughly every 20 minutes. Any audible commotion outside the walls — a fight, breaking stone, shouting — draws a Watch response in 1d6 + 4 minutes (1 mage, 6 veterans). This timing is not available through a skill check alone; it requires either sustained observation or a knowledgeable faction contact (Cromley, Jalester, a city-wise NPC).
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:137: *Peaceful Entry.* If the party approaches openly — the front gate, a visiting card, a polite request — they can access Lord Orond. DC 12 Persuasion to be received as guests; DC 15 if they mention the fireball or the Zhentarim. Lady Yalah never appears for a peaceful visit. She stays upstairs. Orond, if cultivated (DC 10 Charisma, disadvantage if Yalah has been warned), reveals under pressure: the Stone is in Yalah's keeping, Floxin was there of their own invitation (though he now understates the arrangement), and the Stone "has something to do with the Neverember matter." He does not know what the matter is. He does not know Manshoon is Floxin's master. He knows the Zhentarim offered protection and money, and that the arrangement has gone badly wrong.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:156: **G11 (Orond's Study).** Locked (DC 15 thieves' tools). During the day, the nimblewright stands dormant against the northeast corner — alone in the room. Orond uses the library (G12) while at home; the study is his working office, not his reading chair. The nimblewright activates if the estate's *Alarm* network triggers or Yalah speaks the command word from G16. The desk holds the *Gralhund Study of the Grand Game* — Orond's personal journal entries on the six Grand Game factions, including his "Eagle" and "Catoblepas" embedded agents in Renaer's household, and his deductions about the Three Eyes and the Stone. A DC 15 Investigation finds a false book concealing the private volume. Cross-ref Appendix E for the full document listing.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:160: Separate from the trapped tome: a folded letter on a reading stand. *A Missive from Castle Ward*, signed "Chirada," dated two weeks prior. The exact text: *"We are now certain that the Second Eye was carried from the warehouse in the Dock Ward to Yellowspire in the Castle Ward. I no longer believe that it remains within Yellowspire, however. After observing the Zhentarim traffic here, I am convinced that they must be using a teleportal within the tower. If we wish to track the Second Eye further, we will need to access that teleportal or otherwise determine where it leads. – Chirada"* The Second Eye is one of three objects required to fully awaken the Stone of Golorr; this letter is a direct intelligence feed into the Grand Game, addressed to the Gralhunds by someone who has been watching Yellowspire on their behalf. It pays off in Arc E. Cross-ref Appendix E, Yellowspire section.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:229: - The house funded Zhentarim agents who abducted Renaer Neverember months ago — before the fireball, before Floxin, before the Stone. Orond says this quietly, as if saying it softly makes it less true. Yalah arranged it. The point was to use Renaer as leverage to recover the Stone from Dalakhar, the gnome spy who stole it from Lord Neverember's people in the first place. "It went sideways," he says. "The gnome didn't come for the boy." He does not say what happened to Renaer after that. The party may already know.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:299: - **Tactical assist:** A gazer swoops in and fires a paralytic ray at whoever is carrying the Stone, crippling them for one round and letting the party close distance. Xanathar would rather the party have the Stone than any other faction — he can always take it from the party later.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:302: *If the Chase Fails.* The Stone reaches a Bregan D'Aerthe outpost (Fenerus Stormcastle in the Trades Ward), a Zhentarim safe house, or is carried back to Xanathar — depending on who took it. From there it moves to the faction's primary lair within 2d6 days. The party has not lost the campaign; they have lost their current leverage and must find a new approach to the faction now holding it. Cross-ref Appendix E outpost and lair entries for which faction ends up with the Stone and what that implies for the next arc.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:334: - **Mortal Enemy:** If any PC has designated Istrid Horn as their **Mortal Enemy** faction contact (from the Doom Raiders recruitment track), this meeting is not neutral. She is both terrified and obligated — she cannot refuse to come, and the party holds the leverage. A PC with this history may extract the courier cache information and the 40 platinum and full shelter cooperation without any check, simply by naming the arrangement clearly. Alternatively: a PC who turns her in despite having named her mortal enemy forfeits the Mortal Enemy bond and takes Doom Raiders renown −3. Tashlyn's note arrives the next morning: *"That was our business. Not the Watch's."*
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:344: - **Bregan D'Aerthe (conditional on Arc C ev-04: Jarlaxle Informed):** Word reaches the party through whatever channel Krebbyg established during Arc C: *"We saw what happened at Gralhund. We know which factions were there. We know who left with what. If the party is interested in a full account — faction by faction — the theater has a back room. Price is one conversation. Not a mission. A meeting."* Jarlaxle was on the rooftops. He watched every entrance and exit.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:350: - **The Stone of Golorr:** Does the party hold it? If not, which faction does, and where is it now?
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:354: - **The Gralhund Study of the Grand Game:** In hand if G11 was searched. Orond's journal. No mechanical payoff — pure characterization.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:356: **Tone:** Comedown after violence. Political and procedural, with Istrid's thread as the one scene with actual personal stakes. The Watch machinery is grinding. The faction landscape has shifted. The party has (or doesn't have) the Stone. Arc E is already in motion.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:374: - The Stone is a transformed aboleth — an inanimate vessel for compressed memory, created by a wizard to hold Neverember's vault knowledge.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:380: *Transition to Arc E.* Whatever the party holds at arc's end, the next phase of the Grand Game is already in motion. The Eyes of the Stone — the three objects needed to fully activate it — are distributed across the four villain faction lairs. The post-crackdown landscape has shifted: Davil is in Watch custody, Tashlyn is reorganizing, and Jarlaxle is reassessing the party's usefulness. The faction outpost phase begins. Arc E opens with the party deciding which faction to probe first.

campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:423: - **Appendix E: Villain Factions** — Manshoon's Weave motivation and blackmailed Masked Lords (Corylus Thann, Jelenn Urmbrusk); *A Directive from Jarlaxle* document listing; *Gralhund Study of the Grand Game* document listing; Yellowspire section (Chirada, Second Eye, teleportal); Xanathar Guild response teams (Scene 5 gazer intercept option).
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:435: What breaks if the party doesn't get the Stone? The campaign continues, but the party loses their leverage. They arrive at every subsequent negotiation without the asset that makes them worth negotiating with. Jarlaxle, who would have dealt with them as equals, deals with them as petitioners. Whichever faction holds the Stone begins locating the vault within 2d6 days. The party is now racing against an active countdown rather than holding the controlling position. This is consequential, not catastrophic — but it changes the campaign's dramatic shape significantly.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:441: This guide makes the metric explicit. The faction contacts in Scene 1 all reference the Ches 24th deadline, and Scene 2's reconnaissance phase explicitly tells the party what each state looks like before they commit to entry. A party that moves fast and arrives during the day gets a guarded household and a one-on-one chance to extract the Stone before Floxin's team arrives. A party that delays gets a battle already in progress and the chaos that creates — which is simultaneously more dangerous and more exploitable. Both are real choices with real tradeoffs.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:451: The DM running this arc should resist the temptation to plan the sequence in advance. Prepare the adversary rosters, understand each faction's goals, and then play the situation as it develops.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:471: If the chase fails — if the Stone reaches a Bregan D'Aerthe outpost, a Zhentarim safe house, or Xanathar's custody — the campaign continues, but the party enters Arc E negotiating from weakness rather than strength. Jarlaxle will deal with them. Xanathar will not (Xanathar never deals). Manshoon's cell will know the party is active and has failed. These are real consequences that reshape the social landscape of the subsequent arcs without ending the campaign. The Stone is not irretrievable — but retrieving it from a faction lair is Arc E's problem, not Arc D's.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:483: The "Bad Time to Be a Zhent" crackdown and Davil's arrest are the city's direct response to what just happened on Saerdoun Street — and they reshape the faction landscape for every arc that follows, not just for parties with Doom Raiders members.
campaign/structure/arc-d-gralhund-villa.md:485: The Watch's sweep removes Davil from play, puts Tashlyn in a provisional leadership role she has not asked for, and signals to every faction in the city that the Grand Game has become visible to law enforcement. Manshoon's cell goes quieter. Xanathar's operations in the North Ward become more careful. Jarlaxle's team leaves no evidence at Artheyn Manor and is never definitively connected to the villa's events. The party will be questioned if they were seen. Their choices during the crackdown — who they protect, who they hand to the Watch, how they handle Istrid Horn — begin to define their faction relationships in the second half of the campaign.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:9: This is the outpost phase: preparation for the chapters to come. The goal is to enter each lair heist knowing enough to have a plan, and to complicate that goal by raising the alert level of each faction whose intelligence is taken.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:15: Ten outposts are distributed across Waterdeep's wards, maintained by four factions, each one a data point in the web of the Grand Game. The party cannot storm the lairs blind — Xanathar's sanctum, Cassalanter Villa's temple, the Sea Maidens Faire, and Kolat Towers are not targets until the party knows enough to attempt them. The outposts are the research: entering a Xanatharian front to find how the Guild accesses its own lair, or breaking into a cultist's apartment to find a map the Cassalanters spent months assembling.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:33: When Dagult Neverember was removed from the Open Lord's chair eleven years ago, he did not surrender his deepest secret. The Vault of Dragons — five hundred thousand gold dragons cached below Waterdeep in a chamber sealed by the Stone of Golorr and commanded only by Neverember's spoken word — stayed hidden. He removed the Stone's eyes to blind it, then distributed all three Eyes to parties he calculated would keep them safe: one to a drow arms dealer whose silence could be purchased, one entrusted to agents whose infernal patrons guaranteed survival, and one brought deeper into the criminal networks beneath the city than any investigator would comfortably follow.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:35: That calculation is no longer accurate. Neverember is in Neverwinter. The parties holding his Eyes have been in Waterdeep long enough to develop their own plans for whatever the Stone might reveal. Jarlaxle Baenre holds his Eye aboard the *Scarlet Marpenoth*, where it serves as leverage and curiosity in equal measure. The Cassalanter family, working through a network of cult agents and infernal diviners, located a second Eye buried in the crypt of a noblewoman who died without heirs, and extracted it. Xanathar holds the last in Sylgar's fishbowl in the lair beneath Skullport. Zhentarim envoys stole it from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter and carried it to Xanathar as a gift. Xanathar killed them, kept the stone, and has never learned what it is.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:37: Manshoon holds no Eye. His envoys carried one to Xanathar and never came back. He wants the Stone itself — specifically, the compressed aboleth memory within it, which encodes structural knowledge of the Weave that Mystra's servants have never catalogued. The vault is an instrument he would use to fund his actual goal. The Stone is the goal. He needs the Stone and any two Eyes, but he never intended to open the vault the way Neverember did — only to hold the Stone long enough to extract what he needs.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:51: *The Three Eyes.* Once attuned, the Stone communicates in impressions rather than words. The message is clear regardless: three gemstone Eyes were removed from the Stone's face by Neverember when he chose to hide the vault from everyone, including the Stone. Without all three Eyes set in their proper sockets, the Stone cannot produce the vault's location or the command phrase to open it. It knows the Eyes exist. It knows they were separated. It has residual psychic impressions from the last beings that handled each one.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:53: *Eye Impressions.* Three fragmentary visions, each pointing toward a faction without naming it:
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:77: **Purpose:** Genuine consultation round before the party commits to their first outpost target. Each contact delivers a tactically distinct, self-interested analysis calibrated to their faction's knowledge and agenda. Mirrors **Gralhund Villa**, Scene 1 in format; differs in register — this consultation is about operational commitment, not dramatic alignment.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:95: **Tone:** More transactional than **Gralhund Villa**, Scene 1. That consultation asked whether the party would act. This one asks where they will act first. Every contact has done homework. Every contact's analysis reflects their intelligence gaps as much as their intelligence. The party should feel the web of competing agendas tightening, and notice that all seven contacts are nudging them toward action — each for reasons that serve the faction offering the nudge.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:105: *Player-Driven Order.* The party chooses which faction to investigate first, which chain to complete first, and whether to pursue multiple chains in parallel. There is no prescribed sequence. The GM makes all four faction threads visible through Scene 2 and the Stone's impressions, then steps back. If the party pursues two chains simultaneously, each faction tracks its own escalation state independently, and spillover rules govern cross-faction effects.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:113: *Finding Outposts Without Faction Help.* Every faction chain has at least one entry point discoverable without faction contacts and without prior outpost documents — through the party's own investigation. These are the street-level entry points:
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:119: **Cassalanter outposts:** The infernal presence near Coachlamp Lane is detectable by any character with Divine Sense within 60 feet of the Converted Windmill, and confirmable with *detect evil and good*. Parties without such abilities can find the location through Coachlamp Lane neighborhood inquiry — neighbors report strange smells, unusual visitors at odd hours, a couple who never quite fit in (DC 13 Charisma, no faction required).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:123: *Revelation Lists.* Every outpost yields intelligence about its own faction's lair and at least one clue pointing toward a different faction. Captured documents are live intelligence, not flavor text. See **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) for the authoritative revelation list. This quest orchestrates those lists; it does not reproduce them.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:125: *Escalation — Baked-In Consequences.* Each outpost entry specifies exactly what happens to that faction's alert state when the outpost is hit. No GM judgment between sessions is required. Each outpost's Getaway section names the exact tier the faction moves to, which response team deploys, and on what timeline. The four-tier system: **Unaware** (faction doesn't know the party exists as a threat), **Suspicious** (faction has noticed unexplained losses), **Alert** (faction knows the party is active, response teams deployed), **Lockdown** (faction in full security posture, lair defenses hardened). The GM reads the Getaway entry and applies it.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:127: *Cross-Faction Spillover.* Xanathar and Manshoon are actively at war; each monitors the other's losses. Hitting a Xanathar outpost raises Manshoon's alert tier by 1, and vice versa — both factions assume the other is responsible before they conclude it was the party. Cassalanter imp surveillance of the Sea Maidens Faire creates a reciprocal intelligence channel; Cassalanter losses alert Jarlaxle that something is moving against infernal-adjacent operations in the city. See **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) for full inter-faction dynamics.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:129: *Founders' Day Clock.* Every outpost entry includes a time estimate: how many in-game days the operation consumes. Encounter-plus operations run 1–3 days. Mini-heist operations run 3–7 days. Total duration: two to four tendays. The party learns about Founders' Day (Flamerule 1st) through Cassalanter intelligence and faction contacts, not through this section. The GM tracks the calendar and makes the approaching deadline legible through environmental detail — festival preparations appearing in the wards, Cassalanter documents referencing a family obligation in Flamerule, contacts mentioning the Open Lord's Founders' Day calendar.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:153: *Step 4 — The Operation.* Primary objectives: recover the *Instructions for Gladiatorial Weaponry* from Keln's desk (Area 6–7), which names Grinda Garloth as the supplier and Ahmaergo as the authorizing agent; recover the *Injury Reports* logbook (Area 5 Upper Landing), which contains an entry noting a wound caused by "incautious observation of the windmill on Coachlamp Lane" — a cross-faction clue pointing toward the Cassalanter outpost chain; recover the *Ill Man's Note* (Area 8–10 Sick Rooms), which contains the Orb Confectioners passphrase in verse. Optional: follow a guest being transported to the gladiatorial event. The trap door in the kitchen leads to an underground dock; guests are blindfolded before descent and transported by boat to Area X4 of Xanathar's Lair. This route is a genuine lair entry method, but arriving via gladiatorial transport means arriving unprepared, blindfolded, and unarmed. See the **Xanathar's Guild** Factions guide page for keyed area details.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:165: *Approach.* Grinda operates openly as a black-market armorer. Anyone asking about Xanathar weapons, Guild suppliers, or black-market arms in the Dock Ward gets pointed to Mistshore within a day (DC 12 Charisma in Dock Ward taverns or street contacts; no faction membership required). Three additional paths: the *Instructions for Gladiatorial Weaponry* from Terasse Estate names her; a captured Xanathar response team member's *Directive from Nar'l* names her as a supplier; Agorn Fuoco's *Report to Agorn Fuoco* (from any Zhentarim response team) confirms her location. Parties can approach as customers, investigators, or raiders. Grinda does not fight for the Guild — she fights for her business. She will negotiate if the party offers more immediate value than Xanathar does, and she will report the encounter afterward.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:203: *Approach.* Four paths to the Interrogation House: the *Directive to Urstul Floxin* from **Gralhund Villa** names Avareen Windrivver and Brindul Alley explicitly; Yellowspire's crumpled note from Avareen (for parties who hit Yellowspire first); questioning a captured Zhentarim response team member (prisoners taken by Zhent teams are brought here — they'll say so); or Trade Ward canvassing for parties lacking **Gralhund Villa** documents (DC 14 Investigation over a day; no faction membership required). The house appears unremarkable from the street. Use house L1 layout (DH p. 62).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:211: *Intel Yield.* Manshoon's name (first use in the campaign). The pass-amulet from Avareen (route to Kolat Towers). Ott Steeltoes as a potential **Xanathar's Lair** contact. The *Directive to Zorbog* naming Fenerus Stormcastle — a cross-faction thread pointing toward Bregan D'aerthe (Fenerus is a BD lamplighter; Manshoon has identified him as a soft target).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:213: **Escalation: Manshoon moves to Alert (Tier 3).** Avareen's flying snakes carry the alarm faster than the party can move; if even one escapes, Manshoon knows within hours. Vevette Blackwater's team deploys within 1d4 days. If Xanathar cross-faction spillover applies, Manshoon moves directly to Lockdown (Tier 4). **Founders' Day Clock: 1–2 days.**
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:239: **Purpose:** Two outposts structured as religious horror. Through their own investigation, the party discovers what no faction contact has managed to uncover: that the Cassalanters are connected to genuine infernal operations. The Asmodean Shrine is a place of worship, not a warehouse. The Converted Windmill is domestic horror — two people living normal lives around a blood pentagram and a map the Cassalanters have been assembling for months.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:267: **Escalation: Cassalanters move to Alert (Tier 3).** Imp survivors report to the family within hours. Seffia Naelryke's response team deploys within 2 days. If BD outposts have also been hit (cross-faction spillover), the Cassalanters flag the party as a threat to their Faire surveillance arrangement. See the **Cassalanters** Factions guide page (Response Teams section). **Founders' Day Clock: 1–2 days.**
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:275: *Step 1 — Gather Information.* Five paths to the Converted Windmill: Seffia Naelryke's visiting card found on Vevette Blackwater from a Zhentarim response team (Seffia approached Vevette to open a communication channel; Vevette rebuffed her, suspecting a trap, but kept the card); Arn's Book of Worship at the Asmodean Shrine; the *Injury Reports* logbook at Terasse Estate ("the windmill on Coachlamp Lane"); a Cassalanter Villa report if **Cassalanter Villa** is completed first; or the infernal presence detected by Divine Sense within 60 feet of the building (DC 12 Wisdom for any character with Divine Sense; no faction required). Parties without Divine Sense can reach the windmill through Coachlamp Lane neighborhood inquiry — neighbors report strange smells, unusual visitors after midnight, a couple who never quite fit in (DC 13 Charisma; no faction required).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:283: Area W8 — Room of Rituals: a blood pentagram drawn in salt and ash. A DC 15 Arcana check reveals it as a demonic divination circle. The working notes describe a search for "Neverember," "Golorr," and "that which has been lost to the waters of Lethe." A desk beside the pentagram holds the most important document in this quest: **a map of Waterdeep with the Brandath Crypt circled in red ink.** This map is the primary clue path to **Vault of Dragons**'s physical entrance. Seffia carries *A Letter from Victoro* confirming the Third Eye was found "in the crypt of Lady Brandath" — this letter requires either stealth or a confrontation to obtain.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:345: **Purpose:** Guidance for running faction response teams as live encounters during the outpost phase. Response teams are consequences of the party's choices, not random encounters — and they are intelligence opportunities.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:349: *When Response Teams Deploy.* Each faction deploys response teams when they reach Alert (Tier 3) or above. The exact timing is specified in each outpost's Getaway section. A faction at Suspicious (Tier 2) may dispatch a single investigative agent — surveillance, not a combat team. Alert and above means a full team in the field.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:351: *Response Team Compositions.* See the relevant Factions guide pages for full compositions and carried documents. Every team leader carries faction-specific documents that function as intelligence opportunities: Zhentarim teams carry directives naming Brindul Alley or Kolat Towers; Xanathar teams carry operational correspondence from Nar'l or Ahmaergo; BD teams carry playbills or coded messages in Drow; Cassalanter teams carry instructions from Lord Cassalanter. The party should treat every response team encounter as a potential document recovery, not just a combat encounter.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:363: **Purpose:** Close the outpost phase. Compile intelligence, assess escalation states, deliver faction debriefs, and orient the party toward their first lair heist. The debriefs are the party's reward for thorough investigation — each contact responds specifically to what the party learned.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:367: *GM Checkpoint.* Before the debrief scene, the GM reviews: which outposts were hit, which documents were recovered, which NPCs were rescued or cultivated, and each faction's current escalation tier. This assessment determines which lair is most accessible (the faction with fewer hit outposts will have lower lair defenses) and which contacts have the most to offer.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:375: *Savra Belabranta (Order of the Gauntlet).* Asks about captives and methods. Were innocents harmed? Were prisoners rescued? If Ott Steeltoes was recovered from the Interrogation House, Savra registers it as a moral victory regardless of the prisoner's faction affiliation. She asks about him by name if she has any intelligence on the Interrogation House operation.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:391: | 4 | **Faction Outposts** | Complete the first outpost chain (any faction) | 1 |
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:393: *Completing a chain:* Hit at least two outposts of the same faction and recover at least one piece of actionable lair intelligence. For Xanathar (three outposts), two of three is sufficient. For all other factions (two outposts), both must be cleared. A party that hits one Xanathar outpost and one Zhentarim outpost has started two chains but completed neither.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:397: *Transition.* The party holds enough intelligence to attempt at least one lair heist. The GM reviews each faction's escalation tier and communicates it through environmental detail: Dock Ward patrols are heavier than last tenday; a Castle Ward merchant notes that the old tower on the hill has gone dark; the Sea Maidens Faire hasn't performed in a week. Party chooses which lair first. **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, and **Kolat Towers** are available in any order.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:405: 1. **One file.** `arc-e-faction-outposts.md`. Cross-faction mechanics, escalation consequences, and the structural guide must be visible in one place. Splitting by faction would scatter the interdependencies.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:407: 2. **Nine scenes.** Three framing scenes (1–3), four faction sections (4–7) treated as a menu rather than a sequence, one response teams scene (8), one aftermath scene (9). The faction scenes are non-sequential by design.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:413: 5. **Player faction hooks gap-filled.** Enclave: infernal resonance confirmed as infernal but source unknown (→ Cassalanters). Gauntlet: Splinter intelligence trail (→ Manshoon). Force Grey: BD operational infrastructure (→ theater and message architecture).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:421: 9. **Chain completion defined.** Two outposts of the same faction plus one piece of actionable lair intelligence. The first completed chain awards 1 Milestone Point (cumulative 10), not enough to reach level 5. Level 5 is reached on the first completed lair heist (**Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, **Sea Maidens Faire**, or **Kolat Towers**).
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:432: | `sources/17. Outpost and Lair Revelation Lists.pdf` | Cross-faction clue web; authoritative document attribution. Consolidated in **Running the Villains** (GM Guide). |
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:437: | `sources/21. Faction Reports of the Grand Game.pdf` | Document prop texts for planted clues. |
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:444: - **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) — Authoritative outpost entries, rosters, revelation lists, response team compositions, escalation framework, and inter-faction dynamics. This quest orchestrates this content and does not reproduce it.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:461: ***The Problem of Non-Linear Content in a Linear Format.*** Every quest through **Gralhund Villa** moved in a single investigative current — a question asked, an investigation run, a confrontation resolved. **Faction Outposts** cannot function this way. Ten outposts in any order, at the party's discretion, cannot be scripted into a sequence without defeating the point. The nine-scene architecture is the solution: three framing scenes that run in order (Stone attunement, faction consultation, operational briefing), four faction sections that function as a menu, and two closing scenes that apply after the menu has been used. The faction sections are labeled as scenes to maintain the document's structural vocabulary — each has a Purpose, Content, and Tone — but the GM should understand that "Scene 4" does not mean "fourth in sequence." It means "this is the Xanathar section."
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:467: ***Baking In vs. Tracking.*** The Alexandrian's framework uses a five-stage escalation system requiring the GM to assess and advance stage between sessions. This guide's standing rule is that the GM must make no creative decisions between sessions that the text could make for them. Each outpost's Getaway entry is the implementation of that rule: it names exactly what changes (faction tier, response team, timeline), eliminating the GM's role as interpreter. Without it, lair defenses feel static regardless of how many outposts were hit, and player choices become invisible in their consequences. The baked-in system makes choices legible without requiring the GM to track them.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:469: ***Cross-Faction Spillover and the Xanathar–Manshoon War.*** The spillover rules — hitting Xanathar raises Manshoon's tier and vice versa — have a specific purpose beyond realism. They ensure that a party pursuing only one faction's chain still generates meaningful pressure across multiple fronts. A party that focuses entirely on Xanathar outposts will encounter Manshoon response teams without having engaged Manshoon directly, surfacing the inter-faction war as lived experience rather than exposition.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:473: ***The Factions Don't Know.*** The Cassalanters' infernal operations have been intentionally withheld from every faction contact — including Mirt, including the full Harper intelligence apparatus. Their cover is the best in Waterdeep: no enemies made, no traceable crimes committed, nothing but admiration from every quarter. Mirt's "nobody is that clean" is as close as external observation gets. The purpose of this choice: it makes the players the first people in Waterdeep to uncover the truth, through their own investigation, rather than receiving it as briefing material from a contact. The Mirror of the Fiend, the cultists' testimony, Victoro's signed letter at the Windmill — the party assembles the picture one piece at a time, the way investigators are supposed to. Discovery earned is worth more than discovery given.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:479: ***Name, Identity, Motivation.*** Manshoon's revelation has been staged across three quests: name at the Interrogation House, identity at **Kolat Towers**, motivation at **Vault of Dragons**. The decision to surface his name on a document that arrives live during the party's operation — rather than in a cutscene or NPC speech — is deliberate. The *Directive to Zorbog* bearing his signature is the party's first tangible evidence that the Splinter has a named leader. It lands differently there, in a house they just broke into, while a prisoner they may or may not rescue is strapped to a chair nearby, than it would in a faction briefing. The emotional environment matters.
campaign/structure/arc-e-faction-outposts.md:489: ***Chain Entry Points.*** Every faction chain has at least three independent paths to its first outpost: one from prior quest documents (**Gralhund Villa** fallout, **Fireball!** Faire visit), one from within the faction's chain (cross-outpost documents), and one requiring no prior relationship or document (street-level entry). The last path — Grinda's reputation in Mistshore, Trade Ward canvassing for Brindul Alley, Divine Sense near Coachlamp Lane, theater district playbills — ensures that a party who has avoided faction contacts and found no cross-outpost documents still has a way in. Three Clue Rule requires three independent paths that cannot all fail simultaneously; it does not require three equally prominent paths.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:9: Three months before the Trollskull fireball, Manshoon dispatched two Zhentarim lieutenants to Skullport carrying a gift: Eye #1 of the Stone of Golorr, stolen from the Protector's Enclave in Neverwinter. The Eye was offered as a peace overture — proof that Manshoon's splinter faction could be a valuable partner in the Grand Game. Xanathar received the envoys in its throne room, listened to their proposal, and ate them. It kept the Eye and has never learned what it is. The Eye passed to Ott Steeltoes, who placed it in Sylgar's fishbowl because it had a pretty color and Xanathar liked Sylgar's bowl to look interesting.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:11: The lair beneath Skullport has been sealed for weeks. Since the fireball disrupted the Street of Silver trade routes, Xanathar has withdrawn into paranoia — convinced that one of the other Grand Game factions has mapped the lair's entrances. Ahmaergo runs external operations while Xanathar broods in the Sanctum, running its eye rays across the empty room and interrogating Guild members whose faces it does not immediately recognize. The gladiatorial games continue because the arena revenue continues, and because stopping them would signal weakness. But Xanathar does not attend from its throne as it once did. It watches from invisibility, alone.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:23: **Purpose.** The party holds the Stone of Golorr and knows an Eye is somewhere in Xanathar's lair; that much was established in **Faction Outposts**. This scene converts that knowledge into operational intelligence. Seven faction contacts provide lair-specific information, each from a different angle on the same target. By the scene's end, the party has at least one confirmed entry method, two potential inside contacts, and enough threat assessment to plan an approach.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:27: *Running This Scene.* Each contact below is available only to party members of that faction. A party with no Harper has no channel to Mirt; a party with no Force Grey member has no Vajra briefing. The scene's intelligence yield depends entirely on which factions the party joined during **Trollskull Alley** and **Fireball!** — a party that spread its memberships widely arrives knowing a great deal; a party that clustered into one or two factions arrives with significant blind spots. Jalester can identify a 90-minute window on the third watch that still exists even at elevated patrol density. campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:169: **X35 — Nar'l's Office.** Sparse and organized by a mind that trusts nothing to memory when paper will do. Nar'l has kept this from Xanathar for eleven months. Finding it is one of three clue paths to **Sea Maidens Faire**.

campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:302: *Founders' Day Clock.* This quest consumes 1–2 tendays total: several days of intelligence briefing and casing (Scenes 1–3), 1–3 days for the operation and escape (Scenes 4–5), and 2–4 days of recovery and debrief (Scene 6). campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:362: In this quest, the panopticus is a puzzle with visible pieces. The party can learn that sensors exist (through passive detection or faction briefings), learn which areas are monitored, understand the detection consequence, find the control room, and execute a solution. This converts the surveillance from an imposed threat into a problem the players can solve. The dramatic tension comes not from whether they'll be detected by something they don't know about, but from whether their plan for the thing they do know about will work.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:400: The case for it is real: a villain faction eliminated, a dangerous location made safe, a complicated enemy relationship resolved with finality. The case against it is also real: permanent loss of Guild intelligence, Nihiloor freed from his puppet's leash and operating unconstrained from Undermountain, Nar'l and Thorvin killed unless the party explicitly evacuates them, the Undermountain connection severed before the campaign descends. The party that destroys the lair is trading a faction they understand — paranoid, predictable, with known structures and exploitable NPCs — for an unknown quantity in Nihiloor, who is smarter than Xanathar, does not have Xanathar's attachment to a goldfish, and has been running the Guild in secret for months without anyone noticing.
campaign/structure/arc-f-xanathars-lair.md:408: The Puppet revelation is the most significant: Xanathar has been Nihiloor's instrument for four months. Every aggressive, paranoid, apparently irrational decision attributed to the beholder in **Faction Outposts** and this quest was Nihiloor's decision. This changes the party's understanding retroactively. The mastermind they've been treating as the main threat was itself controlled by something worse. The real threat from Xanathar's Guild faction was never Xanathar.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:11: The desperation began with Dagult Neverember. His embezzlement from the city treasury gutted the Cassalanter banking operations — loans underwritten against civic funds that no longer existed. Victoro's investments collapsed in sequence. The family's reputation held because reputation lags reality, but the ledgers told a different story. Ammalia found the path through infernal research: a patron who could restore their fortune and guarantee their children's futures. The contract arrived through a pit fiend intermediary. They signed it together, in the temple beneath their own villa, on a night when Osvaldo was sleeping two floors above.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:15: Eye #2 came to the Cassalanters through infernal divination. A commune ritual performed in the temple — Asmodeus answering through a pit fiend proxy — identified the Eye's resting place: the crypt of Lady Alethea Brandath, Renaer Neverember's mother, interred in the Brandath family mausoleum beneath the City of the Dead. Victoro dispatched Seffia Naelryke's team to extract it. The Eye now rests behind the base of the Asmodeus statue in A7, the ceremonial hall of the temple beneath the villa — warded by infernal magic, guarded by the cult's bearded devils, secured in the one place the Cassalanters control absolutely.

campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:33: The intelligence here is shallower than **Xanathar's Lair**'s — no faction has an embedded agent inside the villa, and the Cassalanters' social position makes direct surveillance difficult. campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:47: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe — renown 1+).* Available only if a PC is a BD member. Jarlaxle has been watching the Cassalanters through an embedded source he does not name (Vessa, his doppelganger in the Cassalanter social circle), and he knows what the family has done. He keeps it from Bregan D'aerthe members unless they work it out themselves. He considers them "the most dangerous faction in the Grand Game because they have something none of the others have: a deadline." He will not explain what the deadline is for. He can add, if pressed, that the Cassalanters need the vault gold urgently — not from ambition, but from a fixed date on a calendar. If the party has already found evidence of Cassalanter operations through **Faction Outposts**, Jarlaxle confirms what they have rather than leading with it: "Then you've seen the edges of it. The center is underneath the villa." At renown 3+ and only with that prior evidence in hand, he provides his operative's observations on the villa's schedule and staff patterns — and adds, obliquely: "They intend to solve a problem with other people's lives. One hundred invitations. Ninety-nine cups of wine. A calculation dressed as charity." He will not explain the calculation. He does not mention "Vessa."
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:49: **Tone.** Measured and ominous. The target looks normal. The intelligence is social and political — not architectural. The party is casing a household, not a dungeon. The walls are painted, the food is excellent, and the hosts are charming. The faction contacts can sense something wrong without being able to prove it.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:133: **"Vessa" (Lymeria Lhaurilstar).** During social events, a doppelganger posing as the noblewoman "Vessa" circulates in C15 (the banquet hall). She is a Bregan D'aerthe operative planted to monitor the Cassalanters. She knows the villa's public layout, the approximate location of the cult's gathering space ("below the wine cellar"), and that the Cassalanters have something the Grand Game factions want. If the party has BD renown 1+ (Initiate rank — any BD member), she reveals herself on approach — a brief flash of her true features, a knowing look. Otherwise, she is a mysterious ally who seems to know too much and asks pointed questions about why the party is really here. She provides: distraction (a fainting spell, a spilled drink, a loud argument with another guest), cover stories ("They're with me — the Lhauri family's representatives"), and passage through the banquet areas without social exposure checks. She will not enter the temple.
campaign/structure/arc-g-cassalanter-villa.md:147: **Path 1 — Help the Cassalanters Find the Gold (Cooperative).** The buy-out clause requires 999,999 gold pieces and ninety-nine souls. The Cassalanters have approximately 500,000 gold from their own fortune. They need the Vault of Dragons' 500,000 GP to complete the financial component. If the party discovers the full truth — the contract, the children, the deadline — and approaches the Cassalanters with this knowledge, the Cassalanters are open to cooperation. They are desperate parents, not ideological devil-worshippers. Victoro drops the social mask. Ammalia weeps. They explain the terms and insist the sacrifice is the only way — but a DC 12 Wisdom (Insight) check reveals they would accept any alternative that saves their children. The party can propose: help get the Vault gold in exchange for the Eye and a cessation of cult activities. The Cassalanters agree without hesitation. Cost: the entire Vault goes to Asmodeus. The party receives no gold from Neverember's hoard. And the ninety-nine — the Cassalanters will still attempt the feast unless the party explicitly addresses this. The sacrifice clause is separate from the gold clause.

campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:35: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance — renown 1+).* The Sea Maidens Faire uses private docking arrangements that circumvent standard Watch inspection protocols — Jalester flagged this irregularity in **Fireball!**. campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:101: **Path 2: Alliance with Jarlaxle (diplomatic).** The party negotiates directly. His ask: "Luskan deserves a seat at the table. I need someone at the council who can make that argument." The political cost is real: Luskan has a terrible reputation (piracy, drow influence, historical slavery), and sponsoring its admission strains every faction relationship the party has built. This path bypasses Scenes 4 and 5 entirely. A DC 16 Charisma (Persuasion) check with advantage if the party presents concrete commitments from two or more faction contacts. Jarlaxle's Insight is +11; he detects empty promises instantly.

campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:304: **Tone.** Decompression weighted with consequence. campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:342: - **Kolat Towers** — Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) and the Zhentarim pass-amulets (Scene 5) both provide independent Three Clue Rule paths to **Kolat Towers**.
