campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:356: The GM should telegraph this genre from the briefing onward. When faction contacts describe Jarlaxle, they should convey that this is not a paranoid beholder in a sealed lair or desperate parents in a gilded villa. This is a man who enjoys being robbed if the thieves are interesting enough. That single characterization reframes the entire arc.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:360: This quest is the only lair heist that offers three fundamentally different approaches to obtaining the Eye. This is because Jarlaxle is the only villain who would willingly trade it for something he values more. Xanathar does not negotiate — it eats envoys. The Cassalanters are too desperate — the children's deadline consumes their attention. Manshoon holds no Eye. Jarlaxle is the one Grand Game villain who treats every interaction as a transaction, and the Eye is currency he is willing to spend if the price is right.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:364: All three paths lead to the same aftermath structure, but the faction state changes and debrief reactions differ significantly. The text presents all three without advocacy.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:388: Manshoon is the correct choice for this quest because: he has the intelligence resources (scrying, double agents) to know the Eye's location; his Zhentarim are the most violent faction, creating genuine danger contrast against Jarlaxle's charm; the strike team's pass-amulets to Kolat Towers provide a Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**; and on the alliance or patron path, the Zhent attack gives Jarlaxle a personal reason to send the party after Manshoon — transforming a clean diplomatic resolution into one that carries a new obligation and provides the strongest possible **Kolat Towers** entry.
campaign/structure/arc-h-sea-maidens-faire.md:426: **To Kolat Towers.** Two independent paths from this quest: Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (J30) references Manshoon's operations and Kolat Towers; and the Zhentarim pass-amulets recovered from the Manshoon strike team (Scene 5) provide physical access to Kolat Towers. These join the existing paths from **Xanathar's Lair** (Samara's testimony from X7, Nihiloor's files from X23) and **Cassalanter Villa** (Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game from C6 or C22). The Three Clue Rule is satisfied to **Kolat Towers** through any combination of prior quests.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:15: He spent two years building the Splinter cell — the faction that bears his name but operates independently of the Zhentarim's surviving leadership. The structure is hub-and-spoke: Kolat Towers as the command center, with the Interrogation House in the North Ward and Yellowspire in the Castle Ward serving as field offices connected by teleportation circles. A pass-amulet system replaced the need for a trusted courier network; amulets could be revoked remotely, confiscating access without confrontation. The towers' extradimensional sanctum — a space behind the towers that is not physically behind the towers, accessible only via a teleportation ring keyed to a specific circle in K22 — houses Manshoon's closest lieutenants and his most sensitive operations.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:29: *Running This Scene.* Each contact below is available only to party members of that faction. A party with no Harper has no channel to Mirt; a party with no Force Grey member has no Vajra briefing. The scene's intelligence yield depends on which factions the party joined during **Trollskull Alley** and **Fireball!** — a party that spread its memberships widely arrives knowing a great deal about the towers' defenses; a party that clustered into one or two factions arrives with real gaps. This is by design. The raid genre does not allow the party to compensate for intelligence gaps with improvisation as freely as the caper or social genres; blind spots in the briefing become operational liabilities in Scene 4.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:43: *Tashlyn Yafeera or Davil Starsong (Doom Raiders — renown 1+).* The richest briefing. The Doom Raiders have been building toward Kolat Towers for longer than any other faction — six missions of accumulated intelligence over **Trollskull Alley** through **Faction Outposts**. What they provide depends on which missions the party completed: the pass-amulet distribution ledger (Mission 5), a diagram showing the three force-field gap locations and their 6-hour rotation cycle drawn by Ziraj during his own surveillance (Mission 6), a list of 43 identified Splinter operatives by name and role, and a garrison estimate. If Missions 5 and 6 are not complete, Tashlyn still provides the 43 names and garrison estimate — that intelligence has been accumulating since **Trollskull Alley**. At renown 10+, Davil commits to full joint-operation planning: synchronized entry timing, objective assignment between the party and the DR team, designated rendezvous point at K22, and a contingency signal protocol for communication mid-raid. Below renown 10+, the Doom Raiders' operation launches regardless — it always does — but coordination is improvised rather than planned.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:45: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe — renown 1+).* Available only if a PC is a BD member. Jarlaxle's interest in Kolat Towers is personal. If **Sea Maidens Faire**'s Manshoon parallel strike fired — and it always fires — Manshoon sent agents to attack Jarlaxle's ships in his own harbor. The experience left Jarlaxle more annoyed than alarmed, which for him is the more dangerous emotional state. "They came to my ship. In my harbor. I want to know where they came from, and I want it to stop." BD intelligence on Kolat Towers is ground-floor-only — the Bregan D'aerthe never penetrated past the second floor of the towers and have no information on the sanctum. What Jarlaxle provides: partial interior layout (K1 through K9), confirmation of the pass-amulet system by name and mechanism, confirmation that Sidra Romeir commands internal security, and the observation that Manshoon's agents carry amulets minted with a specific design that distinguishes them from amulets issued to the towers' service staff. If the party recovered pass-amulets from the defeated Zhentarim strike team during **Sea Maidens Faire**'s Scene 5, Jarlaxle confirms they work — those amulets bypass the force field and unlock the front door. At renown 10+, Jarlaxle provides one additional piece: "The wizard in those towers lost an arm in Undermountain. Whatever he is — and I have suspicions — he came up from below." The Halaster connection is stated without elaboration. It is the first hint that Manshoon's identity is stranger than a Zhentarim faction leader.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:57: *Identifying the Score.* Kolat Towers holds no Eye. The Stone of Golorr offers no impression pointing to this location. The party's motivation is strategic: neutralize Manshoon before he ambushes them at the Vault, recover the blackmail ledger, seize intelligence that makes **Vault of Dragons** viable. This is the only lair heist where the party might legitimately ask "why are we doing this?" The answer comes from three convergent directions. First, every faction that provides intelligence about Manshoon eventually ends the same way — "and he will not stop." The ledger, the Vault approach intelligence, the political reach — leaving Manshoon operational means arriving at **Vault of Dragons** against an adversary who has had weeks to prepare. Second, Lady Gondafrey. Savra's briefing makes her existence and her location known; the party knows there is a prisoner in K13 who has been there for 113 years. Third, the intelligence haul itself has specific quest consequences that every contact will name if pressed. "The Blackstaff wants that spellbook. The Open Lord wants that ledger. Whatever Manshoon knows about the Vault, you want to know it first." The party should arrive at Scene 4 having decided what they are there to get.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:62: - Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game (**Cassalanter Villa**): references Manshoon and identifies the towers' location in the Southern Ward, correcting Samara's misdirection.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:63: - Jarlaxle's Report on the Grand Game (**Sea Maidens Faire**, J30): references Manshoon's Splinter operations.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:67: This quest is the convergence point for every faction's Manshoon intelligence. A party that spread across multiple quests arrives here having assembled the picture from multiple angles. A party that skipped most of those quests arrives having assembled less but still enough — Samara's testimony alone names the target.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:158: **E9 — Library and Astral Vault.** The library is accessible normally. The vault at the back of the room is a bloodlocked mechanism: DC 20 Thieves' Tools to bypass with disadvantage, or DC 17 Arcana to bypass via dispel magic or disguise self (making the magical bloodsensor read the user as Manshoon). Inside: 3 spare pass-amulets, 2 spare teleporter rings, and Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game. The Report is the critical **Vault of Dragons** intelligence — it describes the Brandath Crypts approach, identifies Aurinax as "a guardian of considerable patience and considerable power," includes a partially decoded Elvish command phrase, and names Manshoon's analysis of all four faction positions in the Grand Game. This document goes directly to **Vault of Dragons** preparation.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:164: Manshoon talks first. This is not a courtesy — he is assessing. He wants to know who sent the party, whether they have the Stone, whether the Vault approach has been compromised, and whether any of them represent a faction he might negotiate with. If the party has something he wants, he offers a trade. If they do not, he offers mercy: "Leave. Tell no one. This is the only time I will make this offer." If they refuse: he uses the simulacrum as a shield if it is present and activated, wall of force to isolate the most dangerous party member, dominate person on the second most dangerous. He does not commit to lethal combat until cornered. The Retributive Strike Gambit: at 30 HP or below, Manshoon grips the staff of power and states, clearly, that he will break it. This is not a bluff — a broken staff of power in E12 releases a 30-foot-radius force explosion dealing 8 × charges remaining force damage with a DC 17 Dexterity saving throw for half. The party must find a way to disarm him (DC 16 Sleight of Hand, a targeted attack to knock the staff from his grip, or a sufficiently compelling negotiation that takes him below the 30 HP threshold of despair) or accept the consequences.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:209: *Facing Manshoon.* Regardless of which room the party reaches him in, Manshoon talks first. The conversation is an assessment — he wants to know what the party knows about the Vault, whether they have the Stone, and whether there is anything they want badly enough to trade for. His opening is never a threat; it is a question. "What do you actually want?" If the answer reveals that they have come for the ledger, he does not offer to trade it — that leverage is too important. If the answer reveals they have come for the Report on the Grand Game, he may be willing to discuss an arrangement, because the party having accurate Vault intelligence does not hurt him if he reaches the Vault first. If the answer reveals they have come to neutralize him entirely: "Then at least you're honest." Combat.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:215: Documents in E9 (Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game, which refers to "my predecessor" and "the original network" in a way that implies historical continuity without explaining it) + a sigil on documents in E13 (DC 14 Intelligence (History) to identify as the historical Zhentarim founder's personal mark, in use 200 years before the current Manshoon was born) + Lady Gondafrey's testimony, if she was freed ("He told me he founded the Zhentarim centuries ago. I thought he was mad. Then I saw what was left of his arm — the joint where it ends, the way it ends — and I stopped thinking he was mad.") combine to establish:
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:241: **Purpose.** Resolve the raid. No Eye to insert, no Stone upgrade. Instead: deliver the intelligence haul, record the Manshoon faction state, stage the identity reveal through faction debriefs, and position the party for **Vault of Dragons**. Scene 6 is shorter than in prior quests — the dramatic question of **Kolat Towers** resolves on a strategic rather than an emotional register. The party has done something violent and decisive; the aftermath reflects that.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:251: **Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game (E9 astral vault).** The most directly actionable **Vault of Dragons** item. Describes the Brandath Crypts approach in architectural detail, identifies Aurinax by name and species, includes a partially decoded Elvish command phrase (the party has the partial — the complete phrase will require additional research or the Stone's Full Awakening), and analyzes all four Grand Game factions' positions as of the report's date. The analysis is accurate. Manshoon's intelligence on the vault is as complete as anyone's outside the original Masked Lords who created it.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:277: **Jarlaxle (if contacted in Scene 1).** If the Manshoon parallel strike fired in **Sea Maidens Faire** and the party helped or acknowledged it: "Now we're even. Or close enough." Jarlaxle wants to read the Report on the Grand Game — specifically Manshoon's analysis of BD's position in the Grand Game, which he suspects is more accurate than anyone outside BD has any right to be. If the party assembled the full Manshoon Identity Reveal: Jarlaxle confirms the one piece he withheld from his Scene 1 briefing. "I said I had suspicions. The arm was one. There is another thing about him — the way he holds the staff. No one born to a wand holds a staff like that. You hold it like something that has always been yours." He suspected. He wanted to see if the party could find the rest.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:281: *Transition.* The party holds the Stone of Golorr with between 0 and 3 Eyes. If 3 Eyes are restored, **Vault of Dragons** launches — the Full Awakening has already occurred and the Vault's location is known; **Kolat Towers**'s intelligence confirms the approach route. If Eyes remain unrestored, other lair heists are still available; the intelligence haul from **Kolat Towers** (specifically Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game) enriches whichever quests follow. The Report's partial command phrase is a direct **Vault of Dragons** hook regardless of Eye count — the party knows the phrase is partially decoded and that completing it requires either the Stone's Full Awakening or additional research.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:283: **Tone.** Tactical decompression. **Kolat Towers**'s aftermath has a different texture than the prior lair heists' endings: no revelatory awakening (**Xanathar's Lair**), no moral reckoning (**Cassalanter Villa**), no relationship reassessment (**Sea Maidens Faire**). This is strategic positioning — the party has done a violent thing that was necessary, has recovered intelligence that changes the **Vault of Dragons** situation, and has set a faction state flag that will determine what they face at the Vault. Professional satisfaction or grim assessment, depending on how the operation went.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:307: **Kolat Towers**'s transition is not open-ended in the same way as prior lair heists. The remaining task is **Vault of Dragons**. The intelligence haul — Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game, the Blackmail Ledger, the partial command phrase — feeds directly into **Vault of Dragons** preparation. If the party completes **Kolat Towers** with all three Eyes restored, **Vault of Dragons** launches immediately.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:316: - **Cassalanter Villa** (Cassalanter Report on the Grand Game) — identifies Kolat Towers' Southern Ward location. Three Clue Rule path to **Kolat Towers**.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:319: - **Vault of Dragons** — "Manshoon operational?" flag + intelligence haul (Report on the Grand Game, partial command phrase) directly shape the Vault approach.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:324: - **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) — Manshoon faction overview, escalation framework, Kolat Towers lair entry (Southern Ward).
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:343: **Kolat Towers** is the only lair heist without an Eye recovery. The structural risk is that the scene feels lower-stakes than **Xanathar's Lair**, **Cassalanter Villa**, or **Sea Maidens Faire** — that without the Stone's awakening, the climax lacks weight. The intelligence haul addresses this by giving each recovered item a specific, named **Vault of Dragons** consequence that the party understands before they enter the towers. The blackmail ledger is not "valuable treasure" — it is the political tool that neutralizes Manshoon's Masked Lord influence. The Report on the Grand Game is not "lore" — it contains the partial command phrase that opens the Vault. Every item in the haul has a downstream consequence that the party can predict, which makes recovering it feel as meaningful as restoring an Eye.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:359: The "always fires" decision ensures that the Doom Raiders' investment pays off regardless of the party's faction choices. If the party never engaged with the Doom Raiders, the DR still launch and the party encounters them as an independent force — which is itself a characterization of what the DR are. They do not need the party's permission to finish their own war.
campaign/structure/arc-i-kolat-towers.md:363: The three-quest reveal structure — Name in **Faction Outposts**, Identity in **Kolat Towers**, Motivation in **Vault of Dragons** — treats Manshoon differently than the other three villain faction leaders. Xanathar is immediately legible; Jarlaxle makes himself legible on his own terms; the Cassalanters are legible once the party reaches their temple. Manshoon is the one faction leader whose true nature requires assembly from distributed evidence, because his true nature is genuinely strange — a clone of a 200-year-old wizard, not just a Zhentarim leader — and because understanding it changes the party's strategic relationship with him in a way that understanding the other faction leaders does not.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:13: Lord Dagult Neverember found it by accident, arranging his wife's interment. Lady Alethea Brandath had asked for a simple tomb among her ancestors. Neverember gave her something else — he seized on the oldest of the family mausoleums, the resting place of one Lord Anri, evicted the old lord's remains to a newly built crypt nearby, and buried Alethea in the place of primacy instead. In doing so he found the passage the Brandaths had forgotten, the antechamber the Maroon Brotherhood had used for their mummery, and beyond it, the sealed doors of a vault that predated Waterdeep itself. He recognized an opportunity his city would never think to look for. Once the embezzled gold was safely inside, he concealed the vault's location using the Stone of Golorr — an aboleth-forged artifact he had acquired years before — and then blinded the Stone entirely, popping its three Eyes free and scattering them so that even a thief who stole the Stone itself would learn nothing. One Eye he hid in a crypt near his wife's tomb, reasoning that no one alive knew to look there. He was nearly right. The Cassalanters found it during an exhaustive, demon-guided survey of every site connected to his name — and never realized, in all their searching, that they had been standing almost directly above the vault the entire time.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:15: The gold needed a guardian, and Neverember had one in mind. Aurinax, an adult gold dragon, had lived openly in Waterdeep for decades — a privilege granted by the dragonstaff of Ahghairon, which let him ignore the dragonward that kept every other creature of his kind out of the city. The staff had come to him from Maaril, the last archmage to wield it, who went mad shortly before the Spellplague and fled the city, leaving the staff in Aurinax's care rather than see it fall to someone who might misuse it. Neverember offered a trade: Aurinax would keep the staff, and in exchange he would guard a vault beneath the city until Neverember himself, or someone Neverember named as an authorized vassal, came to remove what was stored there. Aurinax agreed. It seemed, at the time, like very little to ask of a being who measured his patience in centuries.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:17: No one has come. Neverember's embezzlement was exposed, his lordship stripped, his ousting from Waterdeep made permanent and public. The vassals he might have named were never named. Aurinax has spent years alone in the dark beneath a mausoleum, watching from a distance as the man who bound him fell from every height he once held, waiting for an authorization that time has made increasingly unlikely to ever arrive — and increasingly uncertain, in the small ways a dragon allows himself to be uncertain, whether the oath he swore to Dagult Neverember still means anything, now that the man is only the Lord of Neverwinter and has lost the office that made the promise worth keeping.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:19: As the Stone's third Eye clicks into its socket — in whichever lair delivered it — every surviving player in the Grand Game receives the same piece of information at the same time: the vault is real, it is beneath the Brandath Crypts, and it can now be opened. Xanathar, if it survives, prefers the gold stay buried and the Stone stay lost — a vault it cannot reach is still leverage it can threaten to use. The Cassalanters, depending on how the party left them, either wait on a promise or plan to take by force what they were once willing to earn. Manshoon, who holds no Eye, has been counting on exactly this moment — he intends to let someone else do the work of opening the vault and then take it from whoever succeeds. And in Neverwinter, word of a Waterdeep in open faction war over a half-million gold pieces has already reached a man who has been waiting years for a reason to come home. Dagult Neverember is packing.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:25: **Purpose.** **Vault of Dragons** opens with resolution, not investigation. By this point the party holds all three Eyes, the Stone has spoken in full — in whichever prior quest delivered the third Eye — and every faction contact has one final piece of business before the vault opens. This is not a briefing in the lair heist sense; there is no more casing to do, no more surveillance to gather. The seven contacts are not filling gaps in the party's knowledge. They are calling in what they're owed, discharging what they promised, and telling the party what happens next. Scene 1's job is to convert nine quests of relationship-building into concrete, quest-specific payoffs, and to give the GM one table that summarizes every branch the rest of **Vault of Dragons** depends on.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:41: *Running This Scene.* Run this as a montage across several days, organized by the kind of thing each contact delivers rather than by faction order. Instruments come first — the Masked Lord's Writ, Jeryth's readiness, Force Grey's Commander standing — because these are tools the party will use directly. Warnings come second — the Illuun compromise, the assassination fallout — because these recontextualize intelligence the party may have already acted on. Optional companions come last — Vajra, Skeemo — because whether they join the party at the vault is often the final piece to settle before Scene 2 begins.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:45: *Jalester Silvermane (Lords' Alliance — renown 1+).* If Lords' Alliance Mission 6 succeeded, Jalester delivers the Masked Lord's Writ's practical terms one final time: the Vault district gate opens to it, Watch officers defer to it within four rounds of presentation, and Laeral has one legal intervention available during **Vault of Dragons** — usable once, for a rival faction complication, a legal challenge, or an NPC arrest. If the party argued Laeral out of the Manshoon leverage offer, Jalester confirms it: "He has no card to play with her. Whatever he tries at the vault, it won't be that." If the offer was accepted instead, Jalester warns that Manshoon can still delay Watch cooperation for up to two hours, or threaten to expose a Masked Lord publicly, if he reaches the vault able to make good on it. At renown 10+, Jalester personally escorts the party to the Vault district gate and stands with them through the Watch's initial questioning, cutting what would otherwise be a lengthy procedural delay down to minutes.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:49: *Savra Belabranta (Order of the Gauntlet — renown 1+).* If the party marked **Order Recognized** in Order of the Gauntlet Mission 6 ("The Writ and the Weight"), the Halls of Justice have formally recognized the party as Order associates. Savra states plainly what that buys them: Watch officers who see the party in conflict with a Cassalanter operative will not automatically intervene, and she will personally testify on the party's behalf if any complication from the vault operation goes legal. She adds a line that has nothing to do with paperwork: "Whatever you're doing with the vault — do it fast." The Founders' Day timeline, however it resolved, has left her with the sense that Waterdeep does not have unlimited patience left for the Grand Game's loose ends. If **Order Recognized** is not marked, Savra gives the party a plain Halls of Justice letter and no testimony. A party that ran three heists plays Mission 6 only after the Vault, so this is the usual case for them. At the Final Approach she also reads each member's tithe from Order of the Gauntlet s01. **Tithe Paid** earns a greeting by name, **Tithe Paid After Asking** a nod, and **Quietly Reassigned** a note from Brother Hallin Ostrel with the gate instructions. At renown 10+, Savra positions herself at the City of the Dead's main gate during the operation, prepared to intervene if Watch response arrives before the party is ready for it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:51: *Vajra Safahr (Force Grey — renown 1+).* If the party delivered a full Grand Game briefing at any point — and especially if Force Grey Mission 6 ("Smoke in the Tower") succeeded and named the party Force Grey Commanders — Vajra is present at the vault opening itself, not merely briefed on it. She provides active magical containment during Scene 4 and stands ready to deal with anything the vault holds that should not be held. If the party never delivered a complete picture, she arrives late, during Scene 5 at the earliest, and finds a situation she was not prepared for. At renown 10+, Vajra casts *nondetection* on one party member for 8 hours before the operation and confirms she will answer a *sending* from inside the vault if things go wrong.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:55: *Jarlaxle (Bregan D'aerthe — renown 1+).* Available only if a PC is a BD member. If the party completed Bregan D'aerthe Mission 6 ("The Dive"), Jarlaxle arrives at this conversation already positioned — that mission concluded the BD operational arc leading into **Vault of Dragons**, and he treats this meeting as its payoff rather than its starting point. Jarlaxle's business here is the clearest of any contact's: he wants the dragonstaff of Ahghairon, and he has a specific plan for the gold. At Dread Lord renown, he proposes returning the entire recovered hoard publicly, in the party's name, to Laeral Silverhand — in exchange for a letter of introduction to the Lords' Alliance council on Luskan's behalf. "I get what I actually came here for. You get credit for the single largest act of civic generosity in a century. Everyone is happy except the people who wanted to keep it." If the party's relationship with Jarlaxle is Allied or Patron, he treats this as a genuine partnership and will fight alongside the party at the vault if a hostile faction shows up. If Hostile, he does not appear in Scene 1 at all — his interest in the staff has not gone away, only his willingness to negotiate for it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:63: **Tone.** Convergence. Every relationship the party built, every renown they earned, every faction debrief they sat through — all of it resolves into a handful of final conversations before the last door in the campaign opens. This should feel like the moment before a held breath is released, not like another briefing. The contacts know it too. Nobody in Scene 1 is asking the party to gather more information. They are wishing them well, or asking for their cut, or both.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:69: **Purpose.** The approach dungeon — and the last true dungeon crawl of the Dragon Heist campaign. Unlike the casing scenes of the lair heists, there is no surveillance to conduct and no entry method to select; the party already knows where the vault is and walks there directly. What Scene 2 offers instead is the campaign's final quiet space: a slow, funereal descent through the resting place of the family whose name has shadowed the entire Grand Game, before the noise of Scenes 4 and 5 begins.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:77: - Kalain's letter and Lord Dagult's papers (**Fireball!**, Scene 6, if pursued) — an unsigned letter confirming all three Eyes were once in Neverember's hands, and papers describing the vault-opening method in Neverember's own hand.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:78: - Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game (**Kolat Towers**, area E9) — if the party secured the contents of the bloodlocked vault during the Kolat Towers raid, this document describes the Brandath Crypts approach and identifies the mausoleum by name. A party that completed **Kolat Towers** and extracted this intelligence enters **Vault of Dragons** already knowing the route.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:85: *The Brandath Mausoleum.* The largest tree by the main entrance to the crypt-complex is a treant. It wakes as the party approaches and warns them off: "Only those of Brandath blood are welcome here! Begone!" If no one present carries that blood, it animates two nearby trees and attacks — too large to follow into any mausoleum but the Grand Crypt's main chamber. If Renaer Neverember is with the party, the treant lets them all pass unmolested, and says to him in passing, "Your mother was a lovely person." This is the treant's only concession; it does not extend courtesy to Renaer's companions on his word alone unless he is physically present.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:95: **Lady Alethea's Tomb.** The critical path. This building was the original Brandath mausoleum — Lord Anri's resting place for centuries, until Neverember evicted his remains to a hastily built replacement mausoleum nearby and buried Alethea here instead, giving her the place of primacy his ego demanded even though she had asked only for something simple. Her sarcophagus, sculpted in her likeness, bears a bouquet of flowers that has not yet fully wilted — left by Renaer, though he has never told anyone. A faded, chipped portrait of Lord Anri adorns the ceiling, identified by a labeled banner; a DC 14 Intelligence (History) check reveals the portrait was actually painted centuries after Anri's death, and that he is depicted holding a dwarven compass and wearing an alexandrite-tipped pin — Maroon Brotherhood symbols, retroactively assigned to a man who was never a member. A DC 15 Wisdom (Perception) check finds that the south wall is false, concealing a staircase down to the Old Crypts.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:99: *The Old Crypts.* Torch sconces line the walls, empty; a thick coat of dust has been recently disturbed by heavy foot traffic. A DC 12 Intelligence (Investigation) check finds the top step scorched by a triggered *glyph of warding* — the Cassalanters' team, years ago, removing the second Eye. Further in, a DC 14 Intelligence (Investigation) check finds the remnants of a second glyph, deliberately disarmed, and an empty secret compartment behind a torn-loose sconce: this is where Neverember hid the Eye the Cassalanters eventually took. The passage's collapsed eastern end is an illusion concealing an intact archway and a two-foot steel vault door — installed by Neverember himself, damaging some of the ancient Maroon Brotherhood symbols carved into the arch around it. The door has a DC 22 lock and an *alarm* spell keyed specifically to Dagult Neverember; it will not warn anyone else. Beyond it, a ten-foot passage slopes down toward the ceremonial vault itself.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:101: **Tone.** Quiet and elegiac. This is the last dungeon of Dragon Heist, and it should not feel like the others. No faction agents are converging here yet — this is the one scene in **Vault of Dragons** that belongs entirely to the party and the dead. Let the crawling claws and the treant provide texture, not tension. The real weight of Scene 2 is Lady Alethea's tomb: a father's ego, a wife's simple wish denied even in death, and a campaign's central mystery hidden in the gap between what someone asked for and what they got.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:126: - Manshoon's Report on the Grand Game (**Kolat Towers**, area E9) — the same document that names the Brandath Crypts includes a partial account of the vault-opening ceremony alongside a partially decoded Elvish command phrase. A party that extracted this intelligence in **Kolat Towers** enters Scene 3 with the ceremony's outline already in hand; the command phrase is usable, if incomplete, and reduces the work of sourcing the remaining details.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:128: *The 15-Minute Window.* Once opened, the doors remain open for exactly 15 minutes before sliding shut. This matters most in Scene 5 — if a hostile faction times its approach to the ceremony rather than to the party's exit, the window becomes a genuine complication. A party that performs the ceremony without first confirming the area is secure may find themselves fighting to hold the door rather than simply walking through it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:166: **Oath-Release.** The path Aurinax has been waiting years for. If the party brings something he recognizes as genuine authority — Renaer Neverember, presented formally and not merely as a familiar face; the Masked Lord's Writ from Lords' Alliance Mission 6; or the release phrase, if the Stone's Full Awakening or subsequent research produced one — Aurinax considers his oath discharged without a fight. He is old enough to recognize the difference between a trick and a genuine instrument of authority, and Hlam's presence, if the party arranged it, grants advantage on the Persuasion check that seals the moment (per Hlam's established relationship with the dragon). This is the ending his own patience has been built for: "See the gold legitimately transferred," and he considers his duty done.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:170: **Legal Argument.** The most novel path, and the one the Grand Game itself has made possible: Dagult Neverember's ousting is public record. If the party can demonstrate — with the Writ, with testimony, with simple established fact — that Neverember's authority to bind Aurinax's oath no longer exists in any legitimate sense, they give the dragon genuine philosophical grounds to reconsider what he owes. This is not a trick. It is the argument Aurinax has quietly been making to himself for years, and a party that makes it explicitly and honestly gives him permission to agree with something he already half-believes.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:172: **Combat.** The path Aurinax does not want and will not seek. He attacks only in defense of himself, the staff, or the gold — never as a first resort. Any attempt to intimidate him automatically fails. His Legendary Resistance defeats attempts to charm him outright. If combat becomes unavoidable, run him as a boss encounter (see the **Bestiary** (not yet drafted)) rather than a standard dragon stat block — he does not chase a retreating party, since Neverember's binding kept him anchored to this vault, not to a pursuit of intruders who flee it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:184: **Purpose.** Every faction thread the campaign has built converges here. Who shows up, and how dangerous they are, is a direct function of the flags set in the Faction-State Summary Table from Scene 1 — this scene is where those flags become an encounter. Dagult Neverember's arrival is the one constant: staged as a second wave, spatially separate from the villain confrontation, so his very personal reckoning with Renaer does not get lost inside a multi-faction brawl.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:188: *Running This Scene.* Build the roster from the Faction-State table before play begins. The confrontation happens in two waves. The first — any hostile villain factions — intercepts the party at or just inside the vault entrance, timed to the ceremony's 15-minute window if the party lingered, or to their exit if they moved quickly. The second — Dagult Neverember — arrives afterward, at the crypt entrance in the City of the Dead, once the immediate danger has passed and the party is carrying (or has decided not to carry) the gold.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:204: | Bregan D'aerthe | Robbed | If BD has identified the party as the thieves: Jarlaxle with drow gunslinger lieutenants, treating this as Hostile — personal grievance rather than faction interest. If BD has not identified the thieves: Jarlaxle absent. GM determines identification from how the **Sea Maidens Faire** heist resolved and whether the party left evidence. | Reckon with the party over the **Sea Maidens Faire** heist, or no presence at all if the thieves were never identified |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:212: If more than one hostile faction is present simultaneously, they do not coordinate with each other — Manshoon, Xanathar's remnants, and an Active-Adversarial Cassalanter team are all here for the same gold and are as much a threat to one another as to the party. A party that recognizes this can let its enemies do some of the fighting for it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:216: *Dagult Neverember's Arrival.* Unconditional. Word of open faction war over his stolen gold reached Neverwinter weeks before **Vault of Dragons**'s final scenes, and Dagult has been in Waterdeep — quietly, staying with Watch loyalists who never stopped answering to him personally — waiting to see how the Grand Game would resolve before he acted. He arrives at the City of the Dead's gate with hired muscle and whatever Watch loyalists he can still call on, expecting to reclaim "his" gold from whoever walks out with it. He is not a combatant by inclination; he prefers legal threat, political leverage, and the simple assumption of authority he has spent his life relying on. If directly challenged or publicly humiliated, however, he will not back down — losing face in front of witnesses is a cost he will not accept even when losing the confrontation itself might be cheaper.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:222: **Tone.** Reckoning. Every choice the party made about every faction, across every arc, resolves here into a single afternoon. Let the villain confrontation feel earned rather than arbitrary — the GM should be able to explain, from the Faction-State table alone, exactly why this specific group of people is standing at this specific gate.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:236: | **Cassalanter logistics** | If the Cassalanters are Cooperative, a dozen carts of empty barrels and trusted servants arrive within the hour | Fast and clean, but the party is now visibly obligated to a faction that is, at best, morally complicated |
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:242: A prominent stone replica of Mt. Waterdeep sits at the heart of the hoard pile, easy to miss beneath the coin — a single sapphire worth 25,000 gp is set into it, echoing the Heart of the Mountain from Undermountain's sixth level. The sapphire was never part of Neverember's embezzlement; the city has no claim to it. It is a clean reward, unconditionally available to a party willing to dig it out, and a fitting one for a party that chooses to return the rest.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:248: If the Cassalanters were Exposed in **Cassalanter Villa** — arrested or fled before **Vault of Dragons** — the children's souls were forfeited to Asmodeus on their birthday without the infernal contract being destroyed. They became lemures. From prison or exile, Victoro and Ammalia vow to destroy those who thwarted them, but this is a long-term threat rather than a **Vault of Dragons** consequence. The party may encounter their wrath in future quests — Undermountain or beyond — but **Vault of Dragons** ends with the Cassalanters neutralized as an active faction; whatever vengeance they intend requires time, resources, and freedom they do not currently possess.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:266: If Jarlaxle was Impressed — the party made off with Eye #3 and faced him without turning the confrontation hostile — he does not press for the gold or the staff. He congratulates the party on the vault's resolution and offers a remark of genuine admiration before departing. No negative faction consequences follow; BD's posture toward the party improves. He will contact them later with a business proposition, or simply note, through an intermediary, that he is watching their careers with interest.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:278: *Campaign Calendar.* **Vault of Dragons** consumes roughly one tenday: several days for Scene 1's final faction business, a day for the crypts and the ceremony (Scenes 2–3), an afternoon for the vault and its confrontation (Scenes 4–5), and the remaining days for aftermath and debriefs (Scene 6).
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:280: **Tone.** Resolution, not epilogue. This is not a coda tacked onto the real ending — it is the ending. Let every faction's final word land with the weight of nine quests behind it, and let the Mad Mage hooks feel like an invitation rather than an assignment.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:313: By the end of Scene 6 the Grand Game is formally concluded, and the campaign's central mystery — what happened to Neverember's stolen gold — is fully resolved by the party's own choices rather than by the GM's fiat.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:327: - **Notable Figures** — Existing profiles used directly: Aurinax, Renaer Neverember, Hlam, Sir Ambrose Everdawn, Laeral Silverhand, Mirt, Jalester Silvermane, Vajra Safahr, Savra Belabranta, Jarlaxle, Manshoon. New profile added: Dagult Neverember.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:330: - **Running the Villains** (GM Guide) — The Grand Game setup (Part 1) and faction escalation framework, both load-bearing for Scene 5's roster assembly.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:339: **Xanathar's Lair** through **Kolat Towers** each tested a different genre and a different set of party capabilities — dungeon infiltration, social infiltration, caper improvisation, raid violence. **Vault of Dragons** is not a fifth genre in that sequence. It is the point where all four genres' consequences arrive at once. The party does not case the vault, because there is nothing left to case — they already know where it is and what guards it. What replaces the heist framework's dramatic engine (plan, execute, adapt) is accumulated consequence: every faction relationship, every renown level, every choice about who to trust and who to betray, all resolving simultaneously. A GM running **Vault of Dragons** should not look for a new structural trick. The trick was already played, nine times, across the quests that came before it.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:351: The four resolution paths in Scene 4 are deliberately structured as a puzzle with real, discoverable clues rather than a GM's arbitrary choice between "talk" and "fight." Hlam's advantage on Persuasion, the Masked Lord's Writ as a literal instrument of authority, Renaer's standing as Neverember's actual son, and Dagult's public ousting as demonstrable legal fact are all clues the campaign has planted across multiple quests. A party that has been paying attention arrives at the vault already holding the tools for a peaceful resolution, without the GM needing to hint at it in the moment. A party that has not is not punished for it — combat remains available and mechanically fair, per the boss-design framework — but it is framed, correctly, as the outcome Aurinax himself did not want.
campaign/structure/arc-j-vault-of-dragons.md:353: Note on source material: the Alexandrian's own "Addendum: The Dragon of Dragon Heist" recommends replacing Aurinax with a straightforward red dragon, on the grounds that his backstory is convoluted continuity to introduce at the very end of a campaign. That concern does not apply here — this remix has been seeding Aurinax's existence, the dragonstaff's significance, and Dagult Neverember's role in the Grand Game since **Finding Floon**. The gold dragon was never a last-minute complication in this version of the story; he is the payoff of a thread the party has been walking beside the entire time.

campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:58: - The two Zhentarims: establish that in Waterdeep, "the Zhentarim" refers to two unrelated organizations. The Doom Raiders (Davil Starsong's cell) are criminals who present as legitimate businesspeople; they are a joinable player faction. Manshoon's splinter cell is an enemy faction. Characters who join the Doom Raiders are NOT working for the campaign's main antagonist. This distinction causes confusion if not established at session zero.

campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:144: Each player chooses one Debt of the City: a specific, lingering obligation that exists before the campaign begins. Unlike a backstory event, a Debt doesn't end — it follows the character. A social and narrative anchor, it plants them inside the city's power structures and the Grand Game before the first session ends.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:187: > **Warning: Two Zhentarims.** Floxin works for Manshoon, not the Doom Raiders. A character whose debt is held by Floxin is already in the cross-hairs of the campaign's villain faction, whether they know it or not. Davil Starsong's cell will not automatically help this character — they are a separate organization. Let the character navigate the distinction without the DM explaining it for them.
campaign/structure/ch1-beginning.md:206: Renaer Neverember trusted you with something he does not trust easily: the truth. Not the public truth about his father's corruption — that story is becoming common knowledge — but a specific, private piece of it: a name, a location, a transaction that implicates someone who is not his father — still active, still in Waterdeep. He told you because he needed someone outside his usual circles to hold the information. Because he thought you would know what to do with it if something happened to him.

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:5: > Setting: [Waterdeep Lore](../setting/waterdeep-lore.md) · [History of Waterdeep](../setting/history.md) · [The Grand Game](../setting/grand-game.md)
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:13: The DM reference chapter. Read before the campaign begins — not during sessions. Provides the world context, faction histories, and villain intelligence the DM needs to run Waterdeep as a living city with a specific past rather than a set of keyed locations.
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:15: Sections 1–3 (Lore, History, Grand Game) are read once before play. The four villain sections are consulted throughout the campaign each time the DM needs to know how a villain would respond, escalate, or negotiate. The History section in particular is foundational: it covers the city's political backstory and the origin of every faction competing for the vault. Read it before anything else.
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:21: Waterdeep has always been governed by secrets — the Masked Lords, the hidden guild structure, the underworld that feeds on the legal economy's margins. The Grand Game is the city's nature, clarified by one unusually high-stakes prize.
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:37: - **Trades Ward** (commerce, guilds, day-and-night activity): most guild halls; faction front businesses cluster here; Kolat Towers (Manshoon's fortress) occupies a fortified block here
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:44: - **Open Lord Laeral Silverhand**: one of the Seven Sisters, former Chosen of Mystra; politically formidable; knows more about the Grand Game than she lets on; would prefer to recover the vault quietly if it ever surfaces
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:45: - **Masked Lords**: anonymous governing council; their identities are the city's most closely guarded secret; individual Lords may have faction affiliations the PCs never learn
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:53: - Most major guilds have at least one faction operative embedded in leadership
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:72: **Tone:** Dense, specific, practical. Every fact should imply a consequence. The dragonward matters because Jarlaxle moves by sea. Guild membership matters because Trollskull Manor needs permits. The Watch matters because faction violence eventually reaches it.
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:78: **Purpose:** The complete narrative backstory of the campaign, told in chronological order. A DM who reads it should understand not just what happened, but why every villain is exactly where they are when the campaign begins. Every faction's present is a consequence of something that happened here.
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:80: **Structure:** Written as continuous narrative history organized by era, in the manner of CoS:R's History of Barovia — not a timeline table, not organized by faction. Events build on each other. Each era sets up the next. Faction histories are woven into the chronology as they become relevant, not treated as separate threads.

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:121: - Who Neverember was: not a villain by origin; an ambitious politician who genuinely improved Waterdeep — harbor expansion, post-Spellplague rebuilding in the Dock Ward, Watch funding increases; the improvements were real
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:123: - The Stone of Golorr: an aboleth artifact Neverember had modified to encode the vault's location in a form only the three Eyes could unlock; he scattered the Eyes among trusted contacts before his ouster; the trust did not outlast his position

campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:130: - Her relationship to the Grand Game: watching and choosing not to intervene yet; her calculus is to let the factions exhaust themselves competing, then move when the gold is found and the winners are tired; she has contingencies
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:135: - Dalakhar: Neverember's agent sent to Waterdeep to recover the Stone of Golorr; the chain of events that puts the Stone in the Gralhunds' hands and the Eyes in motion; this thread leads directly to the fireball
campaign/structure/ch2-city-of-splendors.md:138: **Tone:** Continuous narrative history organized by era. Each era is a story with named people making identifiable decisions for understandable reasons. No timeline tables. No faction-organized subsections. Events cause later events — the history should feel like a river arriving at the campaign's present, not a set of parallel biographies.

campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:13: The Adventure Summary gives the full campaign shape in one view. The Structural Rules section is consulted whenever a recurring mechanic triggers. The Grand Game in Play section is the DM's living faction tracker — read before each session, updated after. The Design Notes explain why the major structural choices were made.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:39: | III | The Heists | 4–7 | E, F, G, H, I | 16–28 | Can the PCs recover all three Eyes before any faction assembles them? |
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:62: - **The Doom Raiders** (Davil Starsong's cell): a player-joinable faction; presents a public face of legitimate business and moderate criminal enterprise; will not cooperate with Manshoon and does not acknowledge his cell as legitimate Zhentarim; found in Trollskull Alley and across the city as apparent professionals
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:63: - **Manshoon's Splinter Cell**: the villain faction; operates from Kolat Towers; the "Zhentarim" that conducts assassinations and uses mind-controlled servants; Manshoon views the Doom Raiders as deserters who stole his name
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:69: Each villain faction maintains mobile squads — small groups (2–4 members) deployed reactively when the PCs hit faction assets. Cover:
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:70: - Composition: what a typical response team looks like for each faction (personality, tactics, escalation threshold)
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:71: - Deployment triggers: what activates a response team (PC incursion into a faction asset, faction surveillance flagging PC activity, orders from a faction lieutenant)
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:73: - Escalation: hitting one faction's assets does not automatically alert others; factions do not share intelligence freely; but PC actions that become visible citywide (Watch involvement, public confrontation) may reach multiple factions
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:84: The Waterdeep festival calendar is a structural device, not flavor. Festivals provide social cover for heist preparation, create timing pressure, and anchor the Grand Game to specific dates. The DM should establish the campaign's starting date and track the calendar actively.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:114: - Finding each villain faction's lair (Arc E)
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:138: Between sessions, the DM should update a simple faction state record for each villain faction. A brief checklist is enough. For each faction, track:
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:141: - What the faction is actively pursuing this week independent of PCs
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:142: - Whether the faction's position in the Grand Game has shifted (Eye acquired or lost, ally gained or turned, key operative compromised)
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:144: Inter-faction activity continues off-screen. If the PCs spend three sessions on Cassalanter outposts, Xanathar and Manshoon have not been idle. The DM should have a rough answer for what each faction did during that time if the PCs investigate.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:150: ### The Grand Game in Play
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:152: **Purpose:** Gives the DM a practical faction reference — what each faction knows and is doing at campaign start, how they escalate, and how they interact with each other independent of the PCs.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:158: What each villain faction knows before the PCs do anything:
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:163: - **Bregan D'aerthe:** Nar'l Xibrindas has fed Jarlaxle accurate intelligence about the Stone and the Eyes since before the campaign begins; Jarlaxle has the most complete picture of any faction; the Sea Maidens Faire is in port and operational; he is waiting to see which party proves most useful
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:167: For each villain faction: how their posture toward the PCs shifts across five tiers.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:172: | 2 — Observed | Faction has registered the PCs as a variable | PCs appear at a faction-adjacent scene | Nar'l/intelligence assets begin tracking; no action taken |
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:173: | 3 — Investigated | Faction is actively learning about the PCs | PCs hit a faction asset or acquire something faction wants | Response team deployed; clue-seeding props in play |
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:174: | 4 — Engaged | Faction has made the PCs a priority problem | PCs have cost the faction significantly or hold an Eye | Lieutenant takes personal interest; job offer OR assassination attempt depending on faction |
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:175: | 5 — Confrontation | Faction is in direct conflict with PCs | PCs have broken an agreement, stolen from a principal, or blocked a core goal | Full mobilization; faction leader attention; Arc-level consequences |
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:177: Each faction reaches these tiers at different paces. Xanathar escalates faster and more irrationally. Jarlaxle almost never reaches tier 4 without choosing to. The Cassalanters prefer tier 3 indefinitely — the less visible their pursuit, the better.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:181: The Grand Game does not pause for the PCs. Between sessions, the factions act on their own interests:
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:185: - Jarlaxle and Manshoon: fragile non-interference; each considers the other the only genuine peer in the game; neither wants direct conflict because both would rather the other faction absorb the other factions' attention
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:188: When a PC action weakens one faction significantly, the other factions respond — not because they know what the PCs did, but because they notice the suddenly quieter territory or the absence of a known operative. The DM should play this organically: faction activity fills vacuums.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:193: - When a faction withdraws from a conflict it is losing (Doom Raiders will almost always negotiate; Cassalanters retreat to legal/social pressure; Xanathar rarely backs down once engaged)
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:194: - What a faction wants in exchange for standing down (Doom Raiders: information; Manshoon: an asset or a demonstration of usefulness; Cassalanters: discretion; Bregan D'aerthe: the political favor they've always wanted)
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:195: - What a faction remembers (all of them; Xanathar especially; grudges held by Xanathar are permanent unless expunged through an act that satisfies the beholder's ego)
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:197: **Tone:** Operational. This is how a DM runs a living city between sessions without needing to improvise faction behavior from scratch every week.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:203: **Purpose:** Brief operational overview of the factions PCs can join, so the DM understands each faction's role and character before play begins. Full profiles, mission tables, and renown progression live in the appendices.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:213: | Harpers | City's shadow conscience; intelligence-first, non-lethal where possible | Information on faction activity; protection of vulnerable NPCs; exposure of corruption |
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:220: *The unique faction: Bregan D'aerthe*
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:222: Bregan D'aerthe is the only villain faction PCs can join. This changes Arc H's structure entirely — the Sea Maidens Faire becomes a home base, and Jarlaxle sends the party after another faction's Eye rather than being the target of a heist. The arc's inversion is the point: PCs working for Jarlaxle experience the same heist framework from the other side.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:224: Bregan D'aerthe is available only if the nimblewright investigation (Arc C) leads to the Sea Maidens Faire and the PCs respond to Jarlaxle's approach by negotiating rather than stealing. PCs who join take on Jarlaxle's goal (Lords' Alliance membership for Luskan) as their own faction obligation, which comes due in Arc J.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:228: PCs in different factions will have conflicting faction interests by design. The Lords' Alliance wants the gold returned; the Doom Raiders want Manshoon destroyed; Force Grey wants everything kept quiet; Bregan D'aerthe wants a political favor. This is the campaign's social texture. The DM should let faction loyalties create real pressure on individual PCs without forcing party fragmentation.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:230: **Full faction detail:** Appendix C (faction overviews — goals, key members, territory, resources); Appendix D (running factions in play — missions by renown tier, faction hooks per arc, conflicting loyalty guidance).
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:244: The original Dragon Heist uses a "season villain" structure — one villain faction per run-through. This is addressed directly and the decision explained: what the season structure accomplishes (cleaner focus, dedicated arc), what it costs (players lose the political texture of competing factions, the city feels less alive, the specific villain chosen skews the campaign's tone uncontrollably). The remix's choice to feature all four simultaneously is argued on three grounds: it makes Waterdeep feel like a genuine power struggle rather than a scripted encounter chain; it creates organic player agency when PCs can pick which faction to hit first; and it means no single villain defines the campaign's identity.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:268: The design note defends the conditional entry mechanic — Jarlaxle enters the Grand Game as a named player only if the nimblewright investigation reaches the Sea Maidens Faire. This is argued on player agency grounds: if Jarlaxle is always present and always relevant, he dominates the campaign's tone; his entry being conditional means groups that follow the thread get the full Bregan D'aerthe arc, while groups that don't still have three complete villain arcs. The note also covers the backup obligation: the Three Clue Rule requires at least three independent paths to the Sea Maidens Faire, and at least one must be passive (requiring no active player investigation to trigger). A DM who finds the group is unlikely to follow the nimblewright thread is expected to introduce the backup path rather than let the arc disappear.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:272: Arc J produces 500,000gp. The original adventure suggests returning it to Waterdeep as the most heroic outcome. This design note argues against structuring Arc J around this expectation: the players must have genuine agency over the gold for the campaign's moral questions to have weight. The note explains all five resolution paths (keep, return, distribute, use for a specific purpose, trade to a faction) and what each one means for the political aftermath. The DM is told explicitly: do not railroad the gold away from the players. The consequences of keeping it should be dramatic, not punitive.
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:290: 4. **Faction state tracking method:** The text recommends a between-session checklist but does not prescribe a format. The DM should decide whether to use the Appendix E faction tracker, a simple notebook system, or a digital tool. The chapter should reference whichever method the DM will actually use, not describe an elaborate system that will be abandoned after session three.

campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:302: - `21. Faction Reports of the Grand Game.pdf` — what each faction knows at campaign start; inter-faction intelligence

campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:310: - Arc A: First application of Three Clue Rule (Finding Floon); first faction response team encounter
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:312: - Arc C: Three Clue Rule under pressure (fireball investigation); faction response teams all deploy; Jarlaxle's conditional entry gates here
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:313: - Arc E: Revelation lists in full operation; faction state tracking most complex
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:315: - Arc J: Grand Game resolution; faction state from all previous arcs shapes vault confrontation
campaign/structure/ch3-running-the-campaign.md:316: - Appendix E: Villain faction operations and escalation detail; full revelation lists
